# @graview/pages

## 0.1.63

### Patch Changes

- c91af18: A record's page draws a blank fact an act the seat may run writes as an empty slot, Roadmap request:record-pages-draw-writable-but-blank-facts-as-empty-editable-slots. A placement made without its allocation or its role had nowhere on its own page to be given them: a blank fact was not drawn, and the act that writes it (`edit-placement`) surfaces only as the dashed editor on a fact, never as a button — so a new record's empty field could be set only from a form somewhere else. Now each field with no value that an act the seat may run writes — taking the value and asking nothing else — stands where the kind's page orders it (`display.page`, `set-page-fields`) as its label and a dim "add", a button named "Add Allocation" that the keyboard reaches; pressing it opens the same in-place editor a value opens, a choice opening on no choice, and saving fills the fact. A blank no act writes (a `fixed` field) stays unsaid, and a seat the policy keeps from the act — a reader — is offered no slot; a file field is still filled where its files are put (FR-163), and another app's address or a record in one is still picked where it is drawn. Underneath, `readableFields` takes `blanks` — the fields to say as empty slots, never on a glance — and a `ReadableField` read that way carries `blank: true`; `recordFacts` passes `blanks` through, the derived record page asks `blankWritable(store, id, principal)` for them where values are changed in place, `RecordField` draws the slot, and `EditableValue` takes `blank`. Pages' `a-blank-fact-an-act-writes-is-an-empty-slot.test.tsx` and core's `a-blank-a-page-may-fill-is-said-where-it-stands.test.ts` hold it, and a new harness, `verify-blank-facts`, opens a staffing firm's placement in three engines and finds the slot by Tab, fills it by Enter, and finds no slot for the fixed field or for a reader (aBlankFieldAnActWritesIsAnEmptySlot, aBlankFieldNoActWritesStaysHidden, aReaderSeesNoEmptySlot). And a filled value is drawn as a control only where an act the seat may run writes it, Roadmap request:in-place-editors-ignore-grants: the in-place editors offered every act that wrote a field whatever the seat's grants, so a reader saw dashed values the store would refuse; `editableFields` now takes `{ principal }` and offers only the acts `store.permittedMutations` gives it, `useEditableFields` passes the seat's, and a reader's values are read-only on every face — as are everyone's under a provider handed no seat over a policy, which the store would refuse anyway (the same check the blank slots make; aReaderSeesNoEditableValue in `verify-blank-facts`). The graview-pages skill says it in one sentence.
  
  A hosted page's first load stays within its budget. The pages face alone carries 1_329 / 463 more, a visitor's form alone 225 / 83, a visitor's form handed a compiled app 225 / 88, every face 529 gzipped and the embed with the studio handed in 520 gzipped, each under a raise of its own (`scripts/budget-raises/a-blank-fact-an-act-writes-is-an-empty-slot-*.json`); Cloud's worker, which had no room, 600 bytes.
  
  Compatibility: ops and primitives — unchanged. Stored formats — unchanged. The wire — unchanged. The declaration and check finding codes — unchanged. Derived tool names and input schemas — unchanged. The look: a record's page draws a blank writable fact as `[data-graview-blank-fact="<field>"]` with a `button[data-graview-blank]` named "Add <label>". What Graview Cloud does: nothing; a reader's values stop looking editable, a placement's allocation is set from its own page, and a host that drew its own "add" for a blank field can take it away.
- c85bcc0: A Notion connection makes the properties it needs, and one database holds several kinds, Roadmap request:syncing-with-notion-needs-to-allow-for-graview-to-autocreate-the-necessary-properties (Nick, from the site). A Notion connection kept one data source as one kind, and a property it mapped that the data source lacked was only said ("no property in the database for Status, so it is not kept in step") — a person set up every column by hand before anything synced, and a database whose data sources were a project's tasks and its rooms took one connection per source, each with its own credentials and passes. Now a connection may name `sources: [{ "source", "kind", "properties"? }]` in place of its own `source`, `kind` and `properties`: one engine of several resources (`pages:<kind>`), each data source read with a cursor of its own and each page written to the source its kind is kept in; a relation between two of them is a Notion relation property, kept as an edge as one between two connections is. And on its first pass, and whenever one goes missing, a connection that writes gives a data source each property a mapped field lacks (PATCH `/data_sources/{id}`), of the type the field is held in — words a text, a choice a select with its options as people read them ("In progress"), several choices a multi-select, a date, a checkbox, a number, a URL, an email, a phone, and a relation one to the source keeping the kind it reaches — named as the declaration names it, or, matched by name, as the field reads ("Due date"); the name field is the title Notion always has. It never renames or deletes a property somebody made, says one of another type and leaves it, makes no status and no people property, and says what it made in the report and the pass's line: "Made 3 properties in Notion: Status, Due and Done" (`SyncReport.said`, new — what the transport did there or found, as sentences, the first time). `create: false` turns it off; a connection that only reads makes none. A record's page says the source that keeps it, "Kept in step with Notion · Tasks", with Open in Notion to its own page where it came from there and to the database otherwise (`keptInStepWith` on `@graview/core/words`; a calendar's says Google Calendar). `graview check` refuses a source of a kind the document lacks (`connection-kind` at `connections.<name>.sources.<n>.kind`), a property mapped to a field the kind lacks (`connection-field` at the source's `properties.<field>`), and, as the new error `connection-sources`, two sources for one kind, one data source named twice, or `sources` beside the connection's own `kind`, `source` or `properties`; a connection with neither is `connection-kind`. `scriptedNotion()` changes a data source's properties as Notion does, and says a source's properties (`properties(id)`). Core's `notion-makes-the-properties-it-needs-and-keeps-several-sources.test.ts`, pages' `a-record-says-the-notion-source-it-is-kept-in-step-with.test.tsx` and ship's `declared-connections-run-beside-the-store.test.ts` hold it against the scripted Notion; the graview-sync skill says the two forms and what is made. A hosted page carries 0.2 KB more up front (200 bytes minified, 66 gzipped) — the compile reading each source's fields — under a raise naming this changeset; a page handed a compiled app carries nothing more; the pages face alone 1_179 / 464 more (the record page's line), every face 3_295 / 1_192 and the embed with the studio handed in 3_295 / 1_189, and Cloud's worker 11_148 bytes, each under a raise of its own; the scene face before it draws measured 1_078_654 B (1054 KB).
  
  Compatibility: ops and primitives — unchanged. Stored formats — a connection of one source keeps its links under `pages` and its cursor as before; one of `sources` keeps each source's links under `pages:<kind>` and its cursor as `{ "by": { "<resource>": … } }`, which a host keeps without reading. The wire — unchanged. The declaration and check finding codes — a Notion connection gains `sources` and `create`, its `kind` is optional when `sources` names each kind, and the checker gains the error `connection-sources` and says `connection-kind` and `connection-field` under `sources.<n>`; a declaration that passed before still passes, and a connection that writes now adds to its data source the properties it mapped and did not find — `create: false` keeps a data source exactly as it is. Derived tool names and input schemas — unchanged. What Graview Cloud does: its connections form offers a Notion connection's data sources as a list — each a data source of the database picked by name and the kind it keeps, with its field-to-property mapping — writing `sources` when there are several, and a "Make the properties it needs" switch (on by default, `create: false` off); it shows `SyncReport.said` in the connection's activity, and draws nothing of its own for the record's line, which the pages face draws.
- 511184d: A person proposes changes and a reviewer accepts them, Roadmap f-proposals-for-review. Only the app's own actor could leave a change waiting for somebody: a helper who could see a task and was not trusted to close it met a struck-through act and a sentence, and an agent acting for them could do nothing at all.
  
  A grant may now say what its roles may propose and not run: `{ roles: ["helper"], mutations: [], propose: ["close-task"] }` (acts by name or `"*"`, on the grant's `kinds`; a grant that only proposes says `mutations: []`). Reviewing takes no grant of its own: whoever may run the calls decides. `proposes(policy, principal, act, kind)` and `store.mayPropose(call, principal)` say whether a seat may propose a call it may not run; `proposedMutations` is on `@graview/core/tidying`.
  
  `propose(store, principal, calls, intent)` on `@graview/core/tidying` keeps the calls as the same suggestion op the app's own actor makes, with the person as its author and a lineage naming them (`proposedBy`), its basis their words, else "Proposed by Ada". It travels as a call of the store's own `propose-changes` act (`PROPOSE_CHANGES`, `{ calls, intent? }`), which every store answers to and no list offers, and the store makes its op itself: refused, in a sentence, where the seat may neither run nor propose one of the acts, and each call rehearsed as the store would judge it for the reviewer — the act's own guard, the rules, what the seat may see. The same change already waiting is the one that waits. Whoever may run the calls accepts it (`acceptSuggestion`), made as themselves, the lineage naming the proposal and its proposer (`suggestion`, `proposedBy`); a decline answers it in words ("Declined Ada's proposal: …"); its proposer withdraws it (`withdrawSuggestion`). Accepted, it lands in the working state, and reaches readers on the next publish where the app publishes. `isProposal(suggestion)` tells a person's proposal from the app's own suggestion.
  
  An act's form whose seat may propose it and not run it reads "Propose" and keeps the change as the seat's proposal ("Proposed: it waits in Suggested for somebody who may make it."); a record's page offers such an act beside the rest rather than striking it through. The Suggested tray lists a person's proposals apart and first, under "Proposed", each "Proposed by Ada:", with Withdraw in place of a vote for its proposer. An agent acting for somebody who may propose is offered `propose_changes` (`{ calls: { act, args }[], intent? }`), and `list_suggestions` says `proposedBy`. The studio keeps a grant's `propose`. The permissions and tidying skills say how.
  
  The store's own "Propose changes", the proposal it makes, a grant's `propose` and the form's "Propose" cost a hosted page 2.7 KB more up front (2_813 bytes minified, 834 gzipped), and a page handed a compiled app 2.4 KB (2_492 / 737), under raises naming this changeset; the pages face alone 4_120 / 1_191 more, a visitor's form alone 3_909 / 1_127, a visitor's form handed a compiled app 2_968 / 940, every face 7_378 / 2_234, the embed with the studio handed in 6_430 / 1_993 and Cloud's worker 10_754 bytes, each under a raise of its own; the scene face before it draws measured 1_096_224 B (1071 KB).
  
  Compatibility: ops and primitives — a suggestion's op may now have a person as its author, made by the built-in `propose-changes` act, its lineage naming them as `proposedBy` (1–120 characters, which `lineageOf` holds it to); no primitive is new, and accepting, declining and withdrawing are the ops they were. Stored formats — the one lineage key, and nothing moves: a build before this one reads a proposal as any suggestion and keeps `proposedBy` as a key it does not know. The wire — unchanged in shape: a proposal travels as a `call` of `propose-changes`, which a server that predates it refuses as an act it does not have. The declaration and check finding codes — a grant may say `propose`, `grant-act` reads it too (at `policy.grants.<n>.propose`), `add-grant` and `remove-grant` take it, and a grant may name no act to run where it names acts to propose; nothing that compiled before is refused. Derived tool names and input schemas — `propose_changes` is new, listed only for a seat whose policy lets it propose an act it may not run, so a surface whose policy names no `propose` keeps its tools and its hash; `list_suggestions` carries `proposedBy` on a proposal. What Graview Cloud does: takes this build, lets an owner say who may propose in its permissions editor, and draws its review queue from `suggestions()` with `isProposal`, accepting as the reviewer and declining or withdrawing with the functions above.
- e92448c: A place handed to the headless check without its kind is read from the app, and the opening screen is a status in its page's own words, Roadmap headless-view-check-drops-places-without-a-kind, splash-html-aria-label-without-role, splash-html-takes-where-it-opens and splash-shader-light-through-water. `runWorkerViewHeadless` read the places it was handed through the one vocabulary, which takes a place over a kind, and passed over every one handed without a kind without a word: Graview Cloud handed `{ slug, title }` for each of `placesOf(app)`, and every declared lens a view linked to was then said to be no place of the app. A place with no kind is now given the kind the app's own places say — from the declaration when the host hands it as the new `app` option (`placesOf(app, { views })`, which cannot be wrong about it), and from each kind's list and each view's place the run knows otherwise — and a slug that is none of them is refused with a `TypeError` naming every such slug and the way out ("hand each place's `kind` as `placesOf(app)` lists it, or the app"), never checked against nothing. The home and the Scene stay over no kind. `a-place-handed-without-its-kind-is-read-from-the-app-or-refused.test.ts` holds it. The same drop is what HQ's bug-describe-place-on-a-kept-view-reports-lens-places-as-missing-while-the-draft-check-accepts-them saw: once a home view linking to lens slugs was kept, describing the home said every link went nowhere, "Its places: home, overview". `a-kept-home-view-reaches-a-lens-by-its-slug-on-every-path.test.ts` holds a kept home view's link to a lens on every path its places go through: the declaration's names (`placeNamesOf`), the headless run a describer uses, handed places with their kinds or without them and the app, and the live host's vocabulary, read from the embed's registry with the kept view registered on the declared lenses or layered over them.
  
  The opening screen's root carried `aria-label` with no role to be named by, which axe calls aria-prohibited-attr; it is `role="status"` now, named by its title — it says the app is opening — and the ascii title that types itself on is hidden from a reader, who hears it once in that name. `splashHtml(brand, { title?, kicker?, fade? })` takes a page's own words over the brand's `splash.title` and `splash.kicker` (the place it opens on, set large; an empty kicker says none), since the brand is one for the app and a page's opening words are its place's; and `fade: false` takes the screen away at once rather than over 320 ms, as a browser driven by automation (`navigator.webdriver`) and a reader asking for less motion already have it. A fourth shader ships by name, `caustics` — light through water, the image refracted under moving caustic light in the tint, drawn by the same loop at the same pace as `drift`, `ripple` and `veil` and still for a reader who asks for less motion — and `SPLASH_SHADERS` on `@graview/core/document` names it, so `graview check` takes it. `an-opening-screen-is-a-status-in-the-words-of-its-page.test.ts` runs axe on every style, and the preview harness holds that axe finds nothing on the screen in any style (theOpeningScreenIsAStatusAxeFindsNothingOn) and that light through water draws, moves, and stands still for less motion (lightThroughWaterDrawsMovesAndStandsStillForLessMotion). The pages README, the graview-brand skill and the graview-host-page skill say it.
  
  The kinds read for a handed place and the shader cost Cloud's worker 2_033 bytes, under the raise `scripts/budget-raises/headless-places-and-splash-worker.json`; nothing a hosted page loads up front changed.
  
  Compatibility: ops and primitives, stored formats and the wire — unchanged. The declaration and check finding codes — `brand.splash.shader` takes one more name, `caustics`; a build before this one says `brand-splash-shader` of it, so a host names it only on a build that ships it; no code is added. Derived tool names and input schemas — unchanged. The JavaScript API — `runWorkerViewHeadless` takes `app`, and a place handed without a kind that nothing resolves now throws where it was dropped; `SplashOptions` takes `title`, `kicker` and `fade`; the screen's markup gains `role="status"`. What Graview Cloud does: hands `app` beside `places` to the headless check (or hands `placesOf(app)` whole, kinds and all), passes each page's place as `splashHtml`'s `title` rather than through `brand.splash`, and may offer `caustics` beside its other shaders.
- affa912: The right-click menu says what is under the cursor, Roadmap request:add-copy-link-under-open-in-a-new-tab and request:right-click-context-menu-options-to-be-more-relevant-to-the-item-under-my-cursor. On the routed face a record, a list or a place offered "Open in a new tab" and nothing to paste elsewhere; now "Copy link" stands directly under it, puts the whole address on the clipboard and says "Link copied" quietly at the foot of the window. "Inspect" stands at the foot of every menu, after a rule: a page cannot open the browser's developer tools, so it opens a small sheet of what Graview knows of the thing under the cursor — its kind in words and by its key, the record's id, the field, the place and the picture that drew it (a status board, a view, a lens, by its name), the column it stands in and the link to its page, and, for whoever may change the app (a host's `keptViews` that says so, or an app with no policy), how its kind is declared; keys and ids are written as code. The sheet is fetched the first time somebody asks. Text a person selected is no longer swallowed: a right-click on the selected text keeps the browser's own menu, as before, and a right-click elsewhere while it stays selected opens the menu with "Copy" first, which copies the selection; a finger resting on text no longer opens the menu over it — the phone's own selection handles win — and opens it only on a card, a row, a link, a value, a column or another handle. Safari, and every WebKit on a Mac, selects the word under a right-click before the menu event, so the menu used to find "text somebody selected" under every right-click on words and leave the browser's own; a selection the right-click itself made now counts for nothing — it is put back and the menu opens, while text selected before the press still keeps the browser's menu. And a column under the cursor answers: a status board's column now says `data-column="<field>:<value>"` (with `data-column-kind` and `data-column-label`), a worker view may mark any region the same way, and a right-click on its ground offers "Add a task in Doing" — the kind's add act begun with the column's value given, its form asking only what is left (`PointerMenu.begin`, which the Inspector's menu opens on) — and "Show only Doing", the place or list narrowed by its address (`?filter=status:doing`, as `narrowedPath` writes it). A menu target says the column as `MenuTarget.column` (`MenuColumn`: kind, field, value, label), so a host's section can read it too. The graview-view-links skill says the attribute. `the-menu-says-what-is-under-the-cursor.test.tsx` holds it, and the menu harness proves it at a desk and a phone's width: theMenuOffersCopyLinkUnderOpenInANewTab, inspectSaysWhatIsUnderTheCursor, aSelectionIsCopiedNotSwallowed, and aColumnOffersAddInItAndShowOnlyIt on the tasks' status board and on a worker view that marks its own columns; its long-press claim now rests on a value rather than on a heading's text.
  
  The rows, the column's reading and the sheet's door cost the pages face alone 4.8 KB more (4_939 bytes minified, 2_070 gzipped), every face 10.1 KB (10_307 / 4_261) and the embed with the studio handed in 4.9 KB (5_001 / 1_974), each under a raise naming this change in `scripts/budget-raises/the-menu-says-what-is-under-the-cursor-*.json`; a hosted page stays within its budget.
  
  Compatibility: ops and primitives — unchanged. Stored formats — unchanged. The wire — unchanged. The declaration and check finding codes — unchanged. Derived tool names and input schemas — unchanged. `@graview/react` gains the optional `MenuTarget.column`, `PointerMenu.column` and `PointerMenu.begin` and the type `MenuColumn`, all additive. What Graview Cloud does: nothing is required; a view that draws columns marks each `data-column="<field>:<value>"` to have the menu offer adding in it and showing only it, and a host that keeps the declaration says `may.lenses` or `may.views` through `keptViews` for Inspect to show the declaration.
- 45a60d6: What LifeLogics Estimates found, Roadmap f-lifelogics-findings: five requests from one Cursor session on 2026-10-28 (bug-a-timeline-lens-is-accepted-but-never-becomes-a-place, bug-money-shows-00-cents-on-some-amounts-and-none-on-others, bug-option-ids-are-made-into-labels-badly-and-describe-lines-and-badges-show-raw-values, more-than-20-computed-fields-per-kind-and-glances-that-can-show-computed-fields, bug-link-changes-made-through-act-batch-are-missing-from-history).
  
  A timeline is accepted only where it draws. `add-lens` with lens `timeline` over an initiative's start and end dates applied cleanly, `get_problems` said nothing, and the lens was never a place: a timeline lays out spans by number — minutes of a day, 0 to 1440 — across columns, and dates with no column gave it neither. `add-lens` now refuses a timeline whose `start` or `end` is not a number field, or that has no columns, as `lens-cannot-draw` at `edits.<i>.bindings.<kind>.<role>` or `edits.<i>.options.columns`, and its fix names the bindings a timeline takes and the calendar that draws spans of dates (`"lens": "calendar", "bindings": { "initiative": { "start": "start", "end": "end" } }`); the checker says the same of a declaration written by hand, a warning at the binding's path. A timeline over numbers with columns is accepted and draws as before.
  
  Money never reads ".00". A list read "$11,424.00" beside "$7,840" and "$22,758.40": a sum worked out as 11423.999… was not whole, so it took two places that rounded to ".00". A sum is now rounded to the cent before it is judged whole, so it shows cents only where it has them. A money field — or a computed field said as money — may say `"places": 0`, and a template `{total | money: 0}`: the sum in whole units, rounded ("$22,758"); `set-format` sets it (`"places": 0`) and clears it (`null`), and the document refuses it on a field that is not money. A field's describe line and label say it so too.
  
  An option is said by its label, and keeps its author's capitals. A kind's `describe` line, its label, a rule's sentence and a card's badge said a choice named bare by its id ("done by Dec 28"), where every other face said its label; now each says the option's label, and where none is given, its id spoken — as a chip is when the field is the whole template, in lower case among other words ("Rainy day (idea)", "supply chain", never "supply-chain"). An option with no label written with capitals keeps them: "LifeLogics", "OpenSet" and "Open Set" stay as written where they read "Life logics" and "Open set" (`humanizeValue` on `@graview/core`); one in lower case or camelCase is spoken as a key is. A field's key is spoken as it was.
  
  A kind works out 60 computed fields, where it could 20 (`MOST_COMPUTED` on `@graview/core/document`). The cap bounds what one record costs to read: each value is worked out for every record a card, a row or a page draws, from a budget of steps of its own. `set-glance` takes a computed field, as the document and every card already did, but not one kept `hidden`; and `toDocument` keeps a computed field in a TypeScript kind's glance, where it dropped it.
  
  A batch of links is in history, said by each link. The framework's history did have one `act_batch` that linked twelve records — one gesture, and each record it linked is in its ops — but it said the batch by its first link alone, so twelve links read as one. A gesture of several acts its caller named nothing for is now said by each act's sentence, "Link A to B; Link C to D", and past three, the first two "and N more" (`Batch.intent`, `ticksOf`, `historyInWords`, Activity and Undo's words).
  
  A hosted page carries 2.3 KB more up front (2_381 bytes minified, 789 gzipped), and handed a compiled app 1.7 KB more (1_786 / 587), under raises naming this changeset: the timeline's bindings in words, a sum's rounding and places, each kind's option labels for a template. The pages face alone 2_090 / 673 more, a visitor's form 1_317 / 451 and handed a compiled app 1_845 / 666, every face and the embed with the studio handed in 5_359 / about 1_650, the guest host about 146 / 55 on each of three measures, and Cloud's worker 9_409 bytes, each under a raise of its own; the scene face before it draws measured 1_080_480 B (1056 KB).
  
  Compatibility: ops and primitives — unchanged; a batch's `intent` where its caller gave none is now its acts' sentences joined rather than its first, and an op's `intent` is display only. Stored formats — unchanged; a document may carry `"places": 0` on a money field or a computed field, which a build before this one refuses by path, and a kind with more than 20 computed fields, which it refuses too. The wire — unchanged. The declaration and check finding codes — `places` and `{x | money: 0}` are new and optional; `set-format` takes `places`; `set-glance` takes a computed field; `add-lens` refuses a timeline that cannot draw as `lens-cannot-draw` (an error on the edit; the checker's finding of a stored declaration stays a warning, so a stored document still opens), and the finding now also says a timeline bound to a field that is not a number; no declaration that drew is refused. Derived tool names and input schemas — unchanged. What Graview Cloud does: on this version `propose_change` refuses a timeline that cannot draw with the bindings it takes, money reads alike in a list, describe lines and badges say option labels, and `set-glance` takes a computed field with nothing of its own. Its `get_problems` said nothing of LifeLogics' timeline although the checker warned `lens-cannot-draw` (no columns) of it: Cloud's `get_problems` should surface warnings of `lens-cannot-draw`. Its `history` and a record's changes left out the batch of links though the framework's history has it, one gesture whose ops name each record linked — reading a batch's records from its ops' primitives (an edge's ends, not only a node's id) is Cloud's half.

## 0.1.62

### Patch Changes

- 011ac97: A home's Recently leaves out the changes the reader cannot see, Roadmap f-recently-hides-unseen. A log is served to a seat with the ops it may not see withheld in place, as FR-16 has it, each saying only "A change you cannot see — Someone"; the derived home listed the last five ops as they came, so a stranger on a Secret Santa's public page — an app that keeps every match to its giver — read five such lines: a list that looked broken, and said activity existed that was never theirs to know of. Recently now lists the last five changes the reader may see, each as before, and folds what was withheld across the span those rows cover (the whole log, when fewer than five are visible) into one quiet line at its foot, "and 2 changes kept from you" — plain words, no author, no time, never a row per withheld op; a reader from whom nothing was kept sees no such line, and a reader who sees nothing recent is told once how many changes were kept. The Activity panel already folded them and is unchanged. `a-homes-recently-leaves-out-what-the-reader-cannot-see.test.tsx` holds it on the gift-exchange document, as a participant, a stranger and the organizer. The pages face alone carries 0.3 KB more (337 bytes minified, 140 gzipped) for the walk back over the served log and the one line, under the raise in `scripts/budget-raises/a-homes-recently-leaves-out-what-the-reader-cannot-see-pages-face.json`; a hosted page's weight up front is unchanged.

- 55649fe: The four follow-ups to 0.1.56 (Roadmap f-partial-dates-in-conversation, f-partial-dates-arithmetic, f-blocks-home-flagged, f-logo-on-dark-light-ground). A date has known only its year or its month since 0.1.56, and four surfaces had not caught up with it or with the dark page beside it.
  
  A rule compares a date that knows only its year or its month by the span it covers (`daySpanOf` on `@graview/core`: `2006` is January 1 to December 31, `2016-05` the first of May to the thirty-first). It is before another date only when the whole span is, after it only when the whole span is, and equal only when the spans are — so a month the day is in is neither before nor after `today()`, where by their letters alone "2006" < "2006-03-04" had been true. `days()` and `hours()` between such dates are measured from the first day each covers, and `date()` of one is that day. The calendar lens draws a month alone as an all-day bar across its month, and a span that ends in a month runs to that month's last day; a year alone is still not placed — a bar across a whole year says nothing a grid can place, and a day picked for it would be a false exact date — and the lens says once how many it left off ("2 know only their year, so they are not placed."; `daysCovered` and `knowsOnlyItsYear` on `@graview/primitives`). `a-rule-compares-a-partial-date-by-the-span-it-covers.test.ts` and `a-calendar-draws-a-month-alone-across-its-month.test.tsx` hold each case.
  
  The seat's own reading of a sentence keeps a year or a month as said: "Acme was founded in 2006" proposes `founded: "2006"` and "founded in May 2016" (or "sept. 2016", "2016-05", "5/2016") `2016-05`, for a date that may know only that much (`partial` on its form field), where every date had been filled with the day it was; a date that must be a day is still the day it is, and a whole day, an id or a number in the sentence is not read as a year. A record's facts say such a date back as "May 2016" or "2006", never "2016-05". `the-conversation-keeps-a-year-or-a-month-as-said.test.ts` holds it.
  
  A home written as blocks marks each record it draws that a broken rule names, as the derived home's chips do: the kit's flag beside its name, said as implicated in a problem, its own card in the warning tone, and `data-graview-flagged` on the listed item; `SpecPlace` takes `flagged`, and the home's view passes what it is handed. `a-blocks-home-marks-a-record-that-breaks-a-rule.test.tsx` holds it.
  
  A light scheme whose ground is dark has been drawn as a dark page since 0.1.56, but the light logo was still the one drawn there, so a dark mark made for paper vanished on it. `logoScheme(brand, scheme)` on `@graview/core` says which scheme's logo a scheme draws — the dark one in the dark scheme and on a dark light-ground — and `logoFor`, the theme's sheet and a static page's sheet hide the other by it, inverting a light logo that asked to be (`darkFallback`) there as in the dark scheme. `a-light-scheme-drawn-dark-draws-the-dark-logo.test.ts`, the logo case in `the-logo-switches-with-the-scheme.test.tsx` and `a-static-page-on-a-dark-light-ground-draws-the-dark-logo.test.ts` hold it.
  
  The span a date covers, a rule comparing by it, and which scheme's logo a scheme draws cost a hosted page 0.8 KB more up front (857 bytes minified, 316 gzipped), and a page handed a compiled app 0.8 KB (835 / 313), under raises naming this changeset; the pages face alone 2_829 / 1_139 more, a visitor's form alone 1_997 / 827, a visitor's form handed a compiled app 840 / 290, every face 1_063 gzipped, the studio handed in 975 gzipped, the guest host 424 / 162 before a worker is drawn, 424 / 156 registering a view, 409 / 150 drawing a worker and 794 / 329 on the open kit, and Cloud's worker 1_214 bytes, each under a raise of its own; the scene face before it draws measured 1_062_625 B (1038 KB).

The declaration and check finding codes — no shape or code changes; a rule that compares a date holding a year or a month alone (which a document's date has accepted since 0.1.56) is now judged by the span the value covers rather than by its letters, so such a rule may hold or break where it did the opposite before, and `days()` over one is measured from the span's first day. The JavaScript API — additive: `daySpanOf` and `logoScheme` on `@graview/core`, `daysCovered` and `knowsOnlyItsYear` on `@graview/primitives`, `flagged` on `SpecPlace`. - 7d85f11: An app's opening screen is in its declaration, Roadmap f-splash-in-document. Graview Cloud kept what a page shows in its first second — while the app's code loads, gone the moment a place has drawn — as a setting of its own, so an export, a remix into a self-hosted app and an import all arrived without it. Now it is the document's: `brand.splash` is `{ "style", "image"?, "title"?, "kicker"?, "shader"? }`, the style one of `plain` (the ground, the app's mark and its name), `editorial` (the image full-bleed, the title very large at the foot), `shader` (the image drifting through one of the shaders the renderer ships — `drift`, `ripple` or `veil`, named by `shader`, never written in a document: data is never code) or `ascii` (the image as characters in the accent, the title typing on); the image a mark as the logo takes one (inline SVG, or a path on the app's own host), the title in place of the app's name, the kicker one small line over it. It is judged where the rest of the brand is: `brand-splash-image` refuses an image that could act or lives on another origin, `brand-splash-shader` a shader the renderer does not ship (naming the ones it does) and warns of one named on a style that draws none, and `brand-splash-style` an edit's style off the list (a document's shape refuses it first). `set-brand` takes `splash` whole and `null` clears it, saying "The app opens on an editorial screen while it loads."; `diffDocuments` says the same. Cloud's `custom` style — a fragment shader of the owner's own — stays Cloud's: the framework never stores code in a declaration, so a remix of an app with a custom screen lands on the editorial one.
  
  The renderer is `@graview/pages/splash`: `splashHtml(brand, { scheme?, name? })` answers `{ html, css, script }` — one element, a stylesheet scoped to it and a plain script — with no import at all, not React and nothing of the framework, bundling to under 14 KB (a test holds it, and runs it in a bare context), so a host inlines the three in its first HTML before the app's root and the first paint never waits on the bundle the screen covers for. The script takes the screen away once a place says `data-graview-drawn` (FR-188's signal), or the page fails with an alert, or fifteen seconds pass, and under reduced motion at once and with nothing moving; WebGL refused, the shader screen is the editorial one. `graview preview` draws it: `servePreview` takes `splash` (the renderer) and serves the page with the screen inlined for a document whose brand has one, and the page as built for one that has none or for a visitor's form; the `graview` tool bundles the renderer beside the page it already carries (`dist/preview/splash.js`), so it takes no dependency on the pages face. The preview harness holds that the screen is in the first HTML, on the page when the document has parsed and gone once the place has drawn, for the shader and the ascii styles; the core tests hold that an export (the document as JSON), an import (compiled again) and a remix (the compiled app written back as its document) keep it. The brand skill says the shape.
  
  Reading the shape costs a hosted page 0.1 KB more up front (144 bytes minified, 65 gzipped), under a raise naming this changeset; every face 1_946 / 722 more and Cloud's worker 3_799 bytes, each under a raise of its own; a page handed a compiled app, the pages face alone, a visitor's form and the guest host are unchanged.
  
  Compatibility: ops and primitives — unchanged. Stored formats — unchanged; a compiled app does not carry the screen, which is a host's first HTML's, drawn from the document. The wire — unchanged. The declaration and check finding codes — the brand gains the key `splash`, which a build that predates it refuses by path (`brand.splash`); `brand-splash-image`, `brand-splash-shader` and `brand-splash-style` are new and judge only the new key; every other code is unchanged. Derived tool names and input schemas — unchanged. What Graview Cloud does: stores the opening screen in the document's `brand.splash` through `propose_change` with `set-brand` in place of its own setting, keeps its `custom` style and its default `graview` frame as settings of its own, and inlines `splashHtml(document.brand, { name: document.name })` from `@graview/pages/splash` in the first HTML its shell serves.

The look (not a surface): a notice's side accent is gone, its chip is `[data-testid="notice-chip"]` and its sentence `[data-testid="notice-sentence"]`, and a reportable toast carries `[data-testid="notice-report"]`. - 0e74201: Ask AI in an app proposes a change to the app itself (Roadmap f-seat-reshape). The seat's tools read, and the right-click menu's suggestions — learned and tidying — cover records, so "we also need to track who's driving" or "split this into before and after the trip" had no path inside the app: it went to a chat somewhere else. Now the person's words are matched to structure through the same judgments as the new-app starter: `@graview/tools/reshape`, a new entry, asks a Noul per trait the app lacks (`need:<trait>`: "does what they asked for need people who own things, which this app has no part for yet?") and a Noul per part it has (`drop:kind:<name>`, `drop:trait:<name>`: "did they ask to stop keeping areas?") over one state — what they said and the app in words — through whatever `decide` the host passes (Jev, as `dream` takes one; a stub in a test); `readReshape(answers, thresholds)` reads the answers (a trait added at 0.7, a kind or trait dropped at 0.7 — dropping loses records, so the bar is never lower) with every raw Noul kept; `reshapeCard({ said, document, add, drop })` says the card in words — `said` in one sentence ("This adds people who own things and keeps categories and vendors."), `adds` (each trait with what its module brings, from a new `TRAIT_BRINGS` beside `TRAIT_WORDS`: "a person kind (or the people the app has), an owner link, Give it to someone, …"), `keeps`, `drops`, and `breaking` as far as the seat can say ("All categories records go, with their links.") — and `proposeReshape({ said, document, decide })` runs the turn, its proposal null when the reading changes nothing. The conversation draws the card beneath the seat's answer (`ChatPanel`): added, kept and dropped as chips the person turns off and on, each toggle said again by the same function so what they accept is always the reading the card shows, breaking changes above the press; Accept hands the proposal — `{ said, add, drop, card }`, the reading as the person left it, never edits — to the host's `onProposeChange(proposal) → "applied" | "declined"`, on `GraviewProvider`, `FrameOptions` (so `mount`, `<Embed>` and the pages alone take it) and the pages' context, beside `onKeepLens`. The host composes the edits from that reading: `composeReshape({ document, add, drop })` on `@graview/core/compose` runs `startingDocument` on the app's own document and answers its `edits` (which `startingDocument` now carries beside `took`, `left` and `said`: the ones that took, in order, replaying onto the base in one `editDocument` call to the document it composed) with `breaking` said from each removal (`breakingOf`) and `notDone`; the host previews them with its data and applies them as the person, as one undoable change, under its own policy, or declines. Code writes every edit, the model never a kind, a field or a rule — and the composer never rides on a page: a page that fetched `@graview/core/compose` when asked made the editor and the critic live up front, which no page carries, so the vocabulary and a document's reading alone stand on a new entry, `@graview/core/traits` (`TRAITS`, `TRAIT_WORDS`, `TRAIT_BRINGS`, `traitsOfDocument`, `hasTrait`, `traitWords`), `traitsOf` of a TypeScript app stays on `/compose` with the composer, and the document barrel says `documentOf` and `rememberDocument` from where they live rather than through the writer. A host's model with tools is offered `propose_change` on its turn — the Roadmap's `propose-change`, spelled as the surface spells its tools — told the card and never an edit, and the card it proposed travels on the reply. The tool exists only for a seat whose role may change the declaration (`mayChangeDeclaration` on `@graview/tools`, the judgment the studio's door now shares: an owner or editor, never a viewer) with a host that handed the hook and a model that decides, over an app with a document to change — the one it was compiled from (`documentOfApp`); an app declared in code remembers itself first (`rememberDocument(app, toDocument(app).document)`, as Things does for its harness) or is offered none. For any other seat there is no door and no card, nothing refused. The panel reads the words for a change to the app conservatively ("we also need", "keep track of who", "add a field", "no longer need … any more"), and a reading that adds and drops nothing goes on to the usual answer. `graview serve`'s own seat passes no hook: the served page has no path that writes its document. A hosted page carries 0.1 KB more up front (145 bytes minified, 70 gzipped: the provider and the frame carry the hook, and the barrel exports `documentOf`); handed a compiled app, 0.2 KB (197 and 197); the pages face alone 334 / 218 more, the embed with the studio handed in 702 / 950, every face 23_150 / 9_033 across the doors a face can fetch — the reading, the card and the seat's tool riding the seat's model, never up front — and the guest host registering a worker view 0 / 1, its own bytes unchanged and the chunk names its imports carry gzipping a byte larger beside the new doors, each under a raise in `scripts/budget-raises/the-seat-proposes-a-change-to-the-app-*.json` naming this changeset. `the-seat-proposes-a-change-to-the-app-from-words.test.ts`, `the-seat-offers-a-change-to-the-app-only-with-a-host-to-apply-it.test.tsx` and the starting-document test hold it, and the seat harness drives the keeper's "we also need who's driving" on Things to the card and through Accept, with a member drawing none. The agent-seat and studio skills say it.
  
  Compatibility: ops and primitives — unchanged. Stored formats — unchanged. The wire — unchanged. The declaration and check finding codes — unchanged; `startingDocument` answers one more key, `edits`, and `composeReshape`, `breakingOf` and `TRAIT_BRINGS` are additions on `/compose`, `@graview/core/traits` a new entry carrying what `/compose` already named. Derived tool names and input schemas — unchanged on `graview mcp` and `toolDefinitions`: `propose_change` is listed only on a seat's turn whose context carries `reshape`, which only a host with `onProposeChange` hands it. What Graview Cloud does: pass `onProposeChange` to `mount` for an owner's or editor's seat; on its server, `composeReshape({ document, add, drop })` from the proposal, preview the edits through its own propose_change and apply them through apply_change as the person; decline for a plan without structure changes.

## 0.1.61

### Patch Changes

- c9fd45a: A choice takes several of its options at once (FR-233), Roadmap f-multi-select-enum; `capabilities().shipped` gains FR-233. An enum took one option and a list took free words, so a memo's recipients — the CTO, the developers, QA, product — had nowhere to be several fixed options. A field is now `{ "type": "enum", "multiple": true, "options": [...] }`: the one shape an enum already has, so its `options`, its `optionLabels` (0.1.56) and `set-options` carry over unchanged, rather than a `list` with `options`, which would have given options a second home on a type whose `of` already says what it holds. It compiles to a list of the options (`z.array(z.enum(...))`), which `fieldSpecOf` reads back as the same field and the generated types say as an array of the union. A record's facts, a card, a glance, a change's sentence and a sheet (`/csv`, `/words`) say each option it holds by its label, "CTO, QA"; a form on the routed face asks with a box per option and sends the list of those ticked, in the options' order; a record's page changes it in place as the same boxes; the scene's ask strip already toggled several of a choice. A list filters by one of its options (`/arrange`, the filter chips and a view's `where`) and is neither sorted nor grouped by it — a record would stand in several groups — which `graview check` says of a list declared grouped by one and of a board bound to one. An import (`/import`, `import_rows`) reads a cell that lists them, "CTO; QA" or "developers, product"; a Notion connection holds it in a multi-select (`notionFieldType`, `several choices` in `NOTION_FITS`), never a select; a derived act's JSON Schema and tool schema ask for an array of the enum (`/json-schema`); a migration keeps the options that fit when a field changes between one and several (`coerce`). The studio's meta-schema has `multiple` on a field, `add-field` and `retype-field` take it, `set-multiple` changes it alone, and a declaration's list of an enum round-trips through the studio's graph, its source and its edits. `multiple` on a field that is not an enum is refused as a shape finding at `kinds.<kind>.fields.<field>.multiple`, in `graview check` and in `document.schema.json` alike; a default outside the options, or a single one where a list is wanted, is `default-option`. The graview-node-kind skill says the shape in one line. `a-choice-takes-several-options.test.ts`, `a-form-asks-for-several-options-as-boxes.test.tsx`, `several-options-changed-in-place-as-boxes.test.tsx` and `a-choice-of-several-round-trips-through-the-studio.test.ts` hold it, and the field-types harness opens a rehearsal's sections on the page and in the form.
  
  Reading the shape, compiling the list and saying its options costs a hosted page 0.5 KB more up front (522 bytes minified, 278 fewer gzipped), and a page handed a compiled app 0.2 KB (161 more minified, 271 fewer gzipped), under raises naming this changeset; the pages face alone 2_246 / 565 more, a visitor's form alone 709 / 126, a visitor's form handed a compiled app 863 / 102, every face 1_480 gzipped, the guest host 36 / 12 registering a view, 6 drawing a worker and 72 on the open kit, and Cloud's worker 4_641 bytes, each under a raise of its own; the scene face before it draws measured 1_054_746 B (1031 KB).
  
  Compatibility: ops and primitives — unchanged. Stored formats — unchanged; a record of such a field holds a JSON array of its options, as a list field does. The wire — unchanged. The declaration and check finding codes — a field gains the key `multiple` (an enum's only; on any other type it is refused as `shape` at `kinds.<kind>.fields.<field>.multiple`, as `options` is), `default-option` also judges a list default, `retype-field` takes `multiple`, and a Notion `connection-field-type` finding names `several choices` for such a field; every other code is unchanged. Derived tool names and input schemas — a form field of a list of an enum is now one `choice` control with `multiple: true` where it was a `list` of `choice` controls (`FormField`); a tool's input schema for the argument is the array of the enum it already was. What Graview Cloud does: declares `"multiple": true` on an enum that takes several; draws a `choice` form field with `multiple` as checkboxes if it draws its own forms.

## 0.1.59

### Patch Changes

- d1d4e11: Starting fresh is asked for in the tab that does it, never by a link from somewhere else. Every app `graview create` writes opens its store with `fresh: browserStartsFresh()`, which read `?fresh=1` in the address as asking — so any site, or a message, that sent a person to `https://<app>/?fresh=1` erased their records, their history and their undo with nothing to take back, and `forgetFreshParam` then tidied the flag away. Now the Start fresh controls (the workbench's and the routed face's, and the seedbed's "Load the example garden") keep a one-time value in this tab's `sessionStorage` and go to `?fresh=<that value>` (`freshAddress` and `FRESH_KEY` on `@graview/core`; `freshHref` in `@graview/ship/browser` makes the same address), and `browserStartsFresh` honors the address only when it carries the value the tab kept, forgetting it as it reads it. A `?fresh=1` from elsewhere, a guessed value, or the same address loaded twice discards nothing, and `forgetFreshParam` drops `fresh` from the address whatever it says. A driven browser is unchanged: it starts fresh unless it says `?remember=1`, and takes `?fresh=1` as asking, so every harness and `graview create`'s scaffold work as they did. Ship's browser-adapter test holds that a store opened under `?fresh=1`, `?utm=x&fresh=1&remember=1` or a value the tab did not keep still holds what a person made, and that the control's own address starts fresh once. The graview-ship and graview-seed skills say Start fresh rather than `?fresh=1`.
  
  The one-time value costs a hosted page 0.3 KB more up front (286 bytes minified, 189 gzipped), and a page handed a compiled app 0.3 KB (286, 117), under raises naming this changeset; the pages face alone 239 / 96, a visitor's form handed a compiled app 286 / 133, every face 175 / 123 and the guest host registering a worker view 4 gzipped, each under a raise of its own, and the scene before it draws 230 bytes more, under 1023 KB.
  
  Compatibility: ops and primitives, the wire, the declaration and derived tool names and input schemas — unchanged. Stored formats — unchanged: what the browser adapter keeps is read and written as before; only what asks for it to be discarded changed. A host whose own control navigates to `?fresh=1` in a person's browser goes through `freshAddress` (or `freshHref`) now, or the address is not honored.

## 0.1.57

### Patch Changes

- 67e4b0d: A home view that bleeds starts right under the bar on a phone (FR-216), Roadmap f-bleed-top-on-phones. At 390 pixels a home whose worker view says `bleed: true` took back its side gutters and kept the page's 40 pixels of top padding, so the view sat under an empty band between the section nav and its first pixel — and a home view may not say `heading: "own"` to lose it. The home's page now gives its top padding back too on a face 680 pixels wide or less, so the view starts flush under the bar; at a desk the column keeps its spacing, as a view that bleeds keeps the column's width there. Where the view did not draw, what stands in its place keeps the padding, as it keeps the gutter. Guest's test opens the home with a view that bleeds and finds the page marked and the phone-only rule, and without `bleed` finds neither; `verify-address` measures the view's top against the page's at 390 and 1440 (`aHomeViewThatBleedsStartsUnderTheBarOnAPhoneAndKeepsItsSpacingAtADesk`). The pages face alone carries 244 bytes more minified (78 gzipped) and every face 244 (84), each under a raise of its budget (`scripts/budget-raises/a-home-view-that-bleeds-starts-under-the-bar-*.json`); a hosted page's first load is unchanged. `capabilities().shipped` gains FR-216.
  
  Compatibility: ops and primitives, stored formats, the declaration and check finding codes, derived tool names and input schemas — unchanged. The wire — additive: `capabilities().shipped` gains FR-216. What Graview Cloud does: nothing; an app whose home view says `bleed: true` starts under the bar on a phone once it runs this build.
- dda9073: A view of many may be its kind's list page (FR-203). In Graview Cloud an agent designed a view of a team's players and could not make it the players' list: a view of many was drawn at its own `/places/<slug>` when titled and nowhere when not, and `/players` always kept the app's own list. A worker view whose manifest says `replaces: "page"` on `cardinality: "many"` is now drawn on its kind's list page under the list's heading in place of the list — no arranging line, no rows, no forms: what is done there is what the view offers — as a view of one that says so is a record's page (FR-149); a titled one is its own place as well, which then offers no "All players as a list" back to itself. Should it fail, the kind's own face is drawn in its place. The list page reads whichever view the kind draws at many × full as the host's tidying leaves the registry, so a view the host deleted or hid stands in for nothing and the list is the app's own again; the host decides who may set the key, as for a view of one. `checkManifest` accepts the key on a view of many and refuses it only on the home's view, and `describePlace` says a list page so replaced as its heading and the view (`drawnBy: "view:<name>"`), with no form. The graview-worker-view skill and the guest guide say so. Pages' test opens `/players` with a view of many that replaces the page and finds the view and no rows, and without the key finds the derived list; guest's registers a real worker view each way and opens `/deliverables`; core's describes both. The pages face alone carries 840 bytes more minified (170 gzipped) and every face 843 (191), each under a raise of its budget (`scripts/budget-raises/a-view-of-many-may-be-its-kinds-list-page-*.json`); a hosted page's first load is unchanged. `capabilities().shipped` gains FR-203.
  
  Compatibility: ops and primitives and stored formats — unchanged. The declaration — unchanged; `describePlace` says a kind's list page a host's view of many replaces as that view. Derived tool names and input schemas — unchanged. The wire — additive: a worker view's manifest may say `replaces: "page"` on a view of many, which an earlier build refuses; `capabilities().shipped` gains FR-203. What Graview Cloud does: lets an agent's list view say `replaces: "page"` once `capabilities().shipped` has FR-203, gated as it gates a view of one's.

## 0.1.56

### Patch Changes

- c7bdc23: A date may know only its year or its month, and a wrong date can be taken away (Roadmap f-partial-dates). History often knows a year alone — APT1 was first seen in 2006 — and an agent that could keep only a day stored `2006-01-01`, a false exact date. A document's `date` now holds a year (`2006`) or a month (`2016-05`) as well as a day: said "2006" and "May 2016" wherever a day is said (a record's facts, a card's glance, a template, a change's sentence, `dayAsRead` and `whenAsRead`), sorted as written so a year comes before the months and days within it and a month before its days, and grouped by year or month in the year or month it names (a finer bucket gives it a group of its own, before the days within it). The date's editor on both faces reads "2006", "May 2016", "5/2016" and "2016-05" as a year or a month alone and says it back ("May 2016 (a month alone)"), and an import reads such a cell into a date column. A TypeScript date field (`ISO_DAY`) is unchanged; a document's date field's pattern is `DATE_VALUE` (`^\d{4}(?:-(?:0[1-9]|1[0-2])(?:-\d{2})?)?$`), which also refuses a month 13 it used to take, and its argument shape says `partial: true`.
  
  Clearing: `edit-<kind>` refused `null` ("expected string") and `""` for a date, and `importRows` read an empty cell as no change, so a wrong date could not be removed. Now `null` clears any field a record may be without (declared optional, not required and with no default) on a derived edit — the act says which in `clearable`, and `null` reaches `apply` past the field's own parse, so its tool schema is the field's as before; a required field still refuses it. `importRows(…, { match, blanksClear: true })` clears such a field where a found record's row leaves its cell blank ("First seen: Jan 1, 2006 → nothing"), through the kind's edit act. Core's tests hold a year and a month kept, said, sorted, grouped and read from a sheet, a wrong month refused, `null` clearing and undone, a required field refusing it, and an import that clears only when it says so; a pages test types "May 2016" into an act's form and "2006-02" over a year on the record. A hosted page carries 1.9 KB more up front, and one handed a compiled app 1.7 KB more, under raises of their budgets with Roadmap f-option-labels (`scripts/budget-raises/a-date-may-know-only-its-year-or-its-month-*.json`); the pages face alone and a visitor's form are raised alike, and the scene face's limit before it draws moves to 1007 KB.
  
  Compatibility: ops and primitives, stored formats and the wire — unchanged; a record that holds a year or a month alone is an ordinary string. The declaration — unchanged in shape; a document's date accepts a year or a month alone and refuses a month past 12. Derived tool names and input schemas — a document's date says the pattern above (announced on the conformance fixture `document:vendors`), and a derived edit accepts `null` for a field it may clear. The JavaScript API — additive: `DATE_VALUE` and `PARTIAL_DAY` on `@graview/core`, `readPartialDay` on `@graview/core/words`, `ImportOptions.blanksClear`, `MutationDefinitionSpec.clearable`, `partial` on a date's `ArgShape` and `FormField`, and `partial` on `WhenInput`. What Graview Cloud does: takes this build; its agents keep `2006` rather than `2006-01-01` and clear a wrong date with `null`, and its `import_rows` passes `blanksClear` when the person asks that blank cells clear.
- d3ab92d: A home says the problems of the records it draws, and no others (Roadmap f-problems-in-context). The derived home said "Rules are broken — see what, and what would fix it" in the warning color for a rule broken on any record anywhere, so a home whose own records were all in order opened on a warning about a kind it does not show. The home now points to a problem only when it names a record its gallery or its jobs draw (`drawnInTheGallery`); a rule broken on a kind the home leaves off (`pages.hide`) is said by the bar's count alone, which still opens the whole list, and the home says neither the warning nor "All rules hold." A home of the app's own (a home view, a worker view or blocks) is handed the broken records as `flagged`, as a place's lens is, to mark where it draws them, and the page says nothing over it. A rule the app wrote that a record does not meet is said in the page's quiet ink (`data-graview-problems-tone="rule"`); a rule that could not be judged at all, in the warning color (`"error"`). What a store enforces is unchanged, and a list or place that draws a broken record still marks it on that record. A hosted page carries nothing more up front; the pages face alone carries 0.5 KB more minified and 0.2 KB gzipped, and every face 0.5 KB more gzipped, with the Suggested tray's wrapping and Activity's merges beside it (`scripts/budget-raises/a-home-says-the-problems-of-the-records-it-draws-*.json`).
  
  Compatibility: ops and primitives, stored formats, the wire, the declaration and check finding codes, derived tool names and input schemas — unchanged. The look: the home's problem line is `[data-testid="problems-here"]`, and a home view's props carry `flagged`.

## 0.1.55

### Patch Changes

- b73b543: The home opens on the jobs, and one design is composed from the declaration rather than written into it (Roadmap f-design-composer). An app looked generic until somebody told a model, several times over, to make it usable and beautiful, and a host's answer was a table from a field's type to a lens that only ever added. An app that says what its people come to do (`purpose.jobs`) and draws no home of its own now opens on it: the first job with records waiting is the page's title, with how many answer it as the seat sees them, its first five records and the job's act on each (the record page's own act and form); the other jobs follow smaller, one with nothing waiting is a single quiet line, and the gallery of places comes after them, smaller. The rest of a job is the kind's list: an address narrows a list by a field's value, not by a job's rule, so the link says "All tasks". An app with no jobs keeps its home exactly. The embed hands its pages the app, so a hosted document's home answers its jobs too. `@graview/core/compose` proposes the rest as one change: `inferJobs(document | app, { records? })` reads at most three jobs off a declaration, conservatively — a choice that ends and the date a record is due by ("Which vendors are due within a week and not yet booked?"), a relation of one that a quarter or more of the records lack ("Which features have no area?"), a status whose first value a record waits at ("Which requests are still open?") — never drawn until a person declares them; `composeDesign(document | app, { records? })` answers `{ edits, said }`: those jobs where the app says none, the critic's own edits asked again until it has none left, and an accent from a fixed palette by the app's name where the brand has none. Applied, every document in this repository and its fixtures compiles and the critic warns of nothing. Said of the Graview Roadmap app: two jobs ("Which releases are still in draft?", "Which requests are still open?"), the four "Stage: X" places removed, five slices kept off the bar, and the open requests listed on its home. Said of todo: nothing, its jobs declared and its brand its own. `design-job-unanswered` is now said only of a declared home that lists none of a job's kind, and carries the `set-home` that answers it; the graview-pages skill says how the home opens. A hosted page carries nothing more up front, the jobs home being fetched with the pages; the pages face alone carries 5.9 KB more (6,084 bytes, 1,898 gzipped), the jobs answered as the home first draws (`scripts/budget-raises/the-home-opens-on-the-jobs-pages-face.json`).
  
  Compatibility: the declaration and check finding codes — `design-job-unanswered` is no longer said of an app with no declared home (the derived home answers its jobs), and now carries a `set-home` edit; `@graview/core/compose` is new (`composeDesign`, `inferJobs`, `accentFor`, `ACCENT_PALETTE`, `MOST_INFERRED_JOBS`), its edits held to `editDocument` and the critic (`docs/stability.md` §4). The look: the derived home of an app with jobs leads with them (`[data-testid="jobs"]`, `job`, `job-row`, `job-act`, `job-quiet`), its standing a line after them, and the gallery under the jobs is smaller (`[data-testid="gallery"][data-small]`). Ops and primitives, stored formats, the wire, derived tool names and input schemas — unchanged.
- 865d43a: What people looked for and could not do is recorded, in the graph's own words (Roadmap f-friction-log). The op log keeps every change, an AI interaction its outcome and a declined suggestion its decline, but nothing kept what happened before a change or instead of one. Now `@graview/core/friction` holds four events — `search-miss` (the words, and the kind they were looked for in), `unanswered` (a question, and the place), `refused` (an act and its refusal code) and `tool-invalid` (a tool, its act, the sentence, and `then`: the names and types of the next call of that tool that worked) — each with `{ id, at, by, kind? }`, never a click or a selector. `frictionEvent` bounds a draft's text, checks its names and refuses a visitor; `frictionOf` counts across distinct people, never who, and leaves out words fewer than `minPeople` (2) people used; `ownFriction` gives a person their own; `frictionDigest` says to a model the words searched for that no kind or field is called, the questions left unanswered and the tool form that worked, with no refusal's sentence; `withinRetention` lets the old go; `toolMistakes` watches an agent's calls; `memoryFriction` keeps it in memory. A page records through one door on the main entry, `recordFriction(store, draft, by)` over whatever `attachFriction` gave the store, and a store nobody attached a sink to records nothing. `aiContextOf` takes `friction` events from its host and says their digest; core reads no storage of its own.
  
  `serveStore` and `createStoreHandler` keep it at `/graview/friction`: `POST { event }` with the author the session's, as an act's is, never the body's — 201, 202 and nothing kept for a visitor, 400 for what is not one of the four, 413 over 2 KB, 429 with `Retry-After` past 30 a minute from one person (`frictionPerMinute`) — and `GET` the asker's own events and the counts (403 to a visitor). `friction` takes the host's `FrictionStore`, in memory unsaid; `false` keeps none and the route answers 410. `graview serve` keeps it in `friction.json` beside the store for 90 days (`fileFriction`, `frictionRetentionDays`; `--no-friction` keeps none), `browserFriction` keeps it in a page's storage for `openStore({ friction })`, and `remoteFriction` is the door's client. `openRemote` posts each draft (`friction: false` sends none, and a visitor's remote never does) and records an act the server refused where it takes it back. The routed face records words on `/search` that found nothing once they stand for 1.5 s, and a form's refused act with its code and kind; the quick ask records a question it had no answer to (not one AI was off or failed for); `graview mcp` records a tool call refused as `invalid` with the form of the next call of that tool that worked (`--no-friction`). The ship README says what is recorded, who sees it, how long it is kept and that a visitor never is.
  
  Core's test bounds the text, refuses a visitor, counts one person searching ten times as one, gives a person their own, lets the old go and says the digest; ship's posts as one seat claiming another, reads as Ada only her own and the counts, refuses a visitor, holds the size and the rate, answers 410 where the host keeps none, records a refusal the server made where the client takes it back, and keeps it in a file and a browser's storage; pages' records a miss once it settles and not a visitor's, and a form's refusal; tools' records the quick ask's unanswered question and an MCP call's mistake with the form that worked. A hosted page carries 0.8 KB more up front (808 bytes, 403 gzipped; handed a compiled app 808 and 416), under a raise of each budget by that (`scripts/budget-raises/the-friction-log-hosted-page*.json`), and the scene face's limit before it draws is 1004 KB. The pages face alone carries 727 bytes more (294 gzipped) and a visitor's form handed a compiled app 960 (412), each under a raise of its own (`the-friction-log-pages-face-alone.json`, `the-friction-log-visitor-form-compiled.json`).
  
  Compatibility: ops and primitives, the declaration and check finding codes, derived tool names and input schemas — unchanged. The wire gains `/graview/friction` (GET and POST); every other route answers as before. Stored formats: `graview serve` writes `friction.json` beside the store, outside the op log and every export; `openStore` takes an optional `friction`. Additive on `@graview/core` (`recordFriction`, `attachFriction`, `isVisitorAuthor`, `FrictionDraft`, `FrictionSink`), the new `@graview/core/friction`, `@graview/core/catalog` (`AiContextOptions.friction`, `AiContextState.friction`), `@graview/ship` (`frictionDoor`, `fileFriction`, `browserFriction`, `remoteFriction`, `FRICTION_PATH`, `frictionFileOf`, `RemoteOptions.friction`, the handler's `friction`, `frictionRetentionDays`, `frictionPerMinute`) and `@graview/tools/cli` (`settledAdapter`). What Graview Cloud does: hands its handler a `FrictionStore` over its own storage (or `false` for a workspace that turned it off), shows "What people couldn't find" from `frictionOf`, and passes `aiContextOf` the events for every connected chat's digest.

## 0.1.53

### Patch Changes

- e30703c: A view that draws its own heading is not titled twice. Book Manuscript's mind map, on Graview Cloud at 390 pixels: its place said "Mind map", "1 purpose" and "All purposes as a list", and then the view said "Mind map" again as its own heading — a quarter of a phone's screen before the view (Roadmap f-place-header-repeats-view-title). A worker view's manifest may now say `heading: "own"`: its place on Pages keeps its title for a screen reader and the keyboard (`PageTitle` takes `unseen`), draws neither it nor the count, and the way to the kind's list stays. A manifest that says it on a view that is not a place, or says anything but `"own"`, is refused with why. The graview-pages skill says so. `pnpm verify address` asks, at 390 and 1440, that a custom page view's heading is said once while any other place keeps its title and count.
  
  Compatibility: ops and primitives — unchanged. Snapshot and log formats — unchanged. The wire — unchanged. The declaration and check finding codes, derived tool names and input schemas — unchanged. The JavaScript API — additive: `WorkerViewManifest.heading` (`"own"`), and `PageTitle`'s `unseen`. What Graview Cloud does: takes this build; lets a view's manifest carry `heading: "own"` through propose_view and the views guide, and a view that draws its own heading says it.
- e30703c: A view's place fits a phone. On Graview Cloud at 390 pixels, a view with something wider than the screen in it — an SVG 640 wide in a box that scrolls sideways, a row of tabs — widened the page: the region a view draws in (`[data-worker-view-place]`) was a grid item at its content's min-content width, so the home grew to 674 pixels and a race page to 1,040 in a 350 column, and the page clipped on the right. The region is now `min-width: 0`, so what is wide scrolls inside the view and the page never widens (Roadmap f-view-place-min-width). `bleed: true` took the gutter back on the home and a record's page but did nothing on a view titled as a place; a place now draws a view that bleeds edge to edge on a phone as they do (f-bleed-on-places). And where a view that bleeds did not draw — refused, past its limits, or not fetched yet — what is drawn in its stead ran edge to edge too; it is now marked (`data-worker-view-fallback`, laid out as if it were not there) and the bleed passes it by, so the app's own page keeps its gutters (f-fallback-lens-loses-gutter). The graview-view-drawing skill says a place bleeds too. `pnpm verify address` asks four more things, at 390 and 1440: a view's wide content never widens the page on a phone, on a place and a record's page; a place declared bleed draws edge to edge; a fallback page keeps its gutters; and, as before, the home and a record's view that bleed span the phone. The pages face alone carries 0.6 KB more, minified (0.1 KB gzipped), and the guest host 0.4 KB more when a view is registered, under raises (`scripts/budget-raises/views-on-phones-and-place-chrome-*.json`) shared with the next two changes.
  
  Compatibility: ops and primitives — unchanged. Snapshot and log formats — unchanged. The wire — unchanged. The declaration and check finding codes, derived tool names and input schemas — unchanged. The JavaScript API — unchanged; a view drawn in a worker view's stead stands in a `div[data-worker-view-fallback]` with `display: contents`. What Graview Cloud does: takes this build; a view's `contain: inline-size` workaround is no longer needed, and `describe_place` may say a place's view bleeds where its manifest says `bleed: true`, which now holds on a place as on the home and a record.
- 642145e: A visitor's form loads light (FR-201). Graview Cloud's form page — `@graview/embed/form` over the app its host compiled, read with `appFrom` from `@graview/core/compiled`, and the store the visitor is served (`openRemote`) — loaded 668 KB up front with React, more than the whole app shell: the derived form imported its controls, and the form its theme, from `@graview/primitives/pages`, the barrel every routed page draws from, and a bundler places in what a page loads first every module its imports reach that anything in the bundle uses. Through the barrel came the bar, the lists, the arranging, the framework's views of a kind and, through them, the document's blocks, view checker and expression parser. Now they import from `@graview/primitives/form`, a new entry with what a form draws with and nothing else (`Files`, `LinkedPicker`, `RepeatPicker`, `WhenInput` and `themeBaseCss`). Cloud's form page loads 308,835 bytes of Graview first (113,728 gzipped), from 468,438 (178,330): about 509 KB with React, from 668. What is left of the document is what builds and runs a compiled app (`document/compiled`, the evaluator and the template): an act the page applies before the server answers is judged as there. The form alone loads 235,371 bytes (86,563 gzipped), from 390,538. "A visitor's form alone" is lowered to that with its raises (226,800 / 83,600 base, from 380,000 / 146,000), and "a visitor's form handed a compiled app" is a budget of its own at 311,000 / 114,800, held by the embed's budget test under 400 KB and under 60% of the pages face. `pnpm verify address` opens the form over a compiled app read with `appFrom`, and it refuses an answer that does not fit and lands one that does (`aVisitorsFormOverACompiledAppRefusesAndAnswersAsBefore`). The bundle measure and `verify-address` now build with React's automatic runtime, as every package's tsconfig says: esbuild does not read it for a `.tsx` its source plugin resolves, and the form drew with a `React` that was not there. `capabilities().shipped` gains FR-201.
  
  Compatibility: ops, stored formats, the wire, the declaration and derived tools are unchanged; `capabilities().shipped` gains FR-201. Additive on `@graview/primitives` (the entry `./form`). What Graview Cloud does: nothing in its code; its form page's 680 KB lint budget (`workers/cloud/lint/no-studio.test.ts`) can come down to about 520 KB once it builds on this.
- e30703c: A record's "Seen in" names each place once. Threat Watch, on Graview Cloud at 390 pixels: a threat's page said "Seen in" ten places, each followed by its own ↗ link to the scene — twenty links, five wrapped lines and twenty tab stops before the record's own view, and an arrow that read as a link out of the app (Roadmap f-seen-in-phone-length). Each place is now one link, to its page; the record's own "In the scene" above it says where it stands there. On a phone, past four places, the first three are said and a button says how many more ("7 more"), and opens them with the keyboard on the first it opened; at a desk every place is said. `pnpm verify address` asks, at 390 and 1440, that Seen in names each place once, folding on a phone and not at a desk.
  
  Compatibility: ops and primitives — unchanged. Snapshot and log formats — unchanged. The wire — unchanged. The declaration and check finding codes, derived tool names and input schemas — unchanged. The JavaScript API — unchanged. What Graview Cloud does: takes this build; nothing to change.
