## 0.1.52

### Patch Changes

- 1a65f86: A list is exported as it is shown (Roadmap f-view-export-csv). A kind's list and a lens that lists records — a calendar, a timeline, a coverage, a board, a status board — offer a quiet, short "↓ CSV" press named "Export CSV" — beside a kind's heading, after adding one, and at the end of a lens's arranging line, and the place menu offers it too: either writes the records the place keeps, in its order, with its filter, its words and its groups — a group drawn shut included, since shutting one is how it is drawn — a "Group" column first when it is grouped, then each record's name, then the fields its page reads, computed ones too, each said as the page says it (money in the app's currency, a day in its locale, a choice as declared), headed by the fields' labels. Never more than the seat sees: the rows are the records the seat is served, so a field its sight keeps is in no row, and a field in no row is no column. The file is named for the place and the day ("Questions — 2026-10-23.csv"), with what a file system refuses replaced; the browser downloads it, or a host that saves files itself passes `onExport(csv, filename)` to the provider, `PagesApp`'s context or `mount`. Whoever may change the declaration is offered "Turn off export" in the place menu, and "Turn on export" where it is off: `export: false` on a lens (`LensDeclaration.export`) or on a kind's list (`pages.lists.<kind>.export`) offers it to nobody, set by the edit `set-lens-export` (`{ title, on?, export }`, on a kind's list by its kind or plural; refused on a lens that lists no records), read by a lens as it is drawn and by a list from the declaration, said by `diffDocuments` and `graview describe`, and written by `toDocument` only when it is off. `graview check` warns of an `export` that is not true or false (`lens-export-not-a-boolean`, `pages-list-export-not-a-boolean`) and of one on a lens that lists no records (`lens-export-lists-no-records`). The list page draws its rows from `shownList` on a new `@graview/core/shown` — the horizon unless the past is asked for, a rank worked out over what the seat sees, the declared groups in order with the closed ones shut — and the export writes the same rows, so the two cannot disagree. The writer is on a new `@graview/core/csv`, fetched with the first press: `toCsv` (RFC 4180 quoting, CRLF, a byte-order mark so a spreadsheet reads accents), every cell that a spreadsheet would run as a formula — starting with `=`, `+`, `-`, `@`, a tab or a carriage return — written with an apostrophe in front (`csvSafe`), `listCsvRows` and `listCsv`, and `csvFileName`. The studio's lens takes `export`, and its change is said as `set-lens-export`. The graview-lists skill says how. A hosted page carries 0.1 KB more up front (the provider's `onExport`), under raises of the hosted page's budgets and the pages face alone's (`scripts/budget-raises/a-list-is-exported-as-it-is-shown-*.json`; the pages face's is mostly `shownList`, which the list now draws with), a visitor's form alone carries 6 bytes more and needs none, and the scene face's limit before it draws moves to 996 KB. Core's tests hold that the writer quotes, guards and is read back by `parseCsv`, that the rows are the page's order and groups, a shut group's rows among them, that a field the seat's sight keeps is no column, and that the switch is edited, checked, carried, written and said; embed's, that Export CSV is on a list's line and in the place menu, hands the host the rows the page draws, and is offered to nobody where it is off, with the owner's switch.
  
  Compatibility: ops and primitives — unchanged. Snapshot and log formats — unchanged; `KeptViews.lens` may be handed `set-lens-export`. The wire — additive: `/graview/views` takes `{ lens: { op: "set-lens-export", title, on?, export } }`. The declaration and check finding codes — additive: `export` on a lens and on `pages.lists.<kind>`, the edit `set-lens-export`, and the findings `lens-export-not-a-boolean`, `lens-export-lists-no-records` and `pages-list-export-not-a-boolean`. Derived tool names and input schemas — unchanged. The JavaScript API — additive: `@graview/core/shown` (`shownList`) and `@graview/core/csv` (`toCsv`, `csvCell`, `csvSafe`, `CSV_BOM`, `listCsvRows`, `listCsv`, `csvFileName`); `LensDeclaration.export`, `KindList.export`, and the `set-lens-export` member of `LensTidyEdit`; `OnExport` and the provider's `onExport`; `PageContext.onExport`; `FrameOptions.onExport`; `ArrangeBarProps.end`, `ArrangingOptions.exportable`, and `ExportPress`, `useShownListExport`, `shownListSaid`, `exportShownList`, `PlaceExportContext` and their keys on `@graview/primitives`; `saveShownList`, `switchExport`, `ShownListSaid` and `ListWords` on `@graview/core/csv`, where a list hands in how a record is said (`labelOf`, `readableFields`, `withComputed`) so the module fetched with the press imports none of them. What Graview Cloud does: takes this build; a host that saves files (or a guest frame that may not download) passes `onExport` to `mount`, and its views route passes `set-lens-export` to the declaration as it does `set-lens-hidden`.

## 0.1.51

### Patch Changes

- 0956b46: Typing is said in words: the fifth phase of collaborative text. A person writing a paragraph sends a run of typing every word, and each was a row of its own; now a run is said as what it wrote, where and how much — "Ada wrote in Kitchen's notes (+42 −3)" — and one person's consecutive runs into one field are one thing. A keystroke's own sentence (`type-text`'s intent) says "Wrote in Kitchen's notes (+5 −1)". The timeline folds them into one tick (`ticksOf`: `batches`, every gesture's batch, and `typed`, the field and the counts; "Restore to here" stops before or after a run, not inside it), Activity into one row with one undo that takes the run back whole (`foldDigests`' row says `typed`, and `typedRowWords` on `@graview/core/tidying` says it), and `historyInWords(store, { me?, limit?, context? })` on `@graview/core/history` is a history newest first in sentences — what an agent's history answer says — each run of typing with the spans it put in and took out (`typedSpans`), never the field's text. `typedRun`, `typingWords`, `typingSentence` and `typedCounts` are there too. A notification says a field typed into as what was put in and taken out (`MatchedRecord.fields[].typed`: `{ inserted, removed, spans }`, with no `before` or `after`) rather than the whole text twice, and is told once, when the typist stops: `holdTypingRuns(hook, { quietMs })` on `@graview/core/subscriptions` holds a match that is a run of typing and joins the same person's next run into the same field for the same subscription into it — one match, every batch (`SubscriptionMatch.batches`), the counts added — until `TYPING_QUIET_MS` (5 s) pass with no more, or another match for that subscription comes, which is told after it. `watchSubscriptions` (`typingQuietMs`) and `@graview/ship`'s handler (`typingQuietMs` beside `onSubscriptionMatch`) hold through it, and tell what they hold when they stop or close. The app as it stood partway through a run folds the text typed so far (`pastOf`), and an agent reads a field typed into whole, as before. `PagesApp` takes `typing` (and `context.typing`), handed to the provider it puts under the routes, so a host that draws the pages alone, with no embed around them, edits a field typed together live; a short string stays a whole value with a Save, which the graview-pages skill says why. The scene face fetches 1.5 KB more before it draws (Activity's fold and its words), and its limit before it draws moves to 995 KB; the pages face alone carries 0.2 KB more, under a raise (`scripts/budget-raises/typing-is-said-in-words-pages-face.json`). Core's tests hold that a run is said with its counts, that one person's runs are one tick and another's breaks it, that the history in words says spans and never the text, that Activity folds a run, that the past partway through a run holds the text so far, that a notification says spans, and that a run is told once its typist stops and before what comes after it; tools', that an agent reads the field whole and its history in sentences; pages', that the pages alone edit live when handed `typing`.
  
  Compatibility: ops and primitives — unchanged; a `type-text` op's intent reads "Wrote in …" where it read "Type in …", and an intent is display only. Snapshot and log formats — unchanged. The wire — unchanged. The declaration and check finding codes, derived tool names and input schemas — unchanged. The JavaScript API — additive: `PlaybackTick.batches` and `.typed`; `historyInWords`, `ChangeSaid`, `typedRun`, `typedSpans`, `typingWords`, `typingSentence`, `typedCounts` and `TypedRun` on `@graview/core/history`; the `typed` row of `foldDigests`, `TypedRow` and `typedRowWords` on `@graview/core/tidying`; `holdTypingRuns`, `HeldTyping`, `TYPING_QUIET_MS` and `WatchSubscriptionsOptions.typingQuietMs` on `@graview/core/subscriptions`; `SubscriptionMatch.batches` and `MatchedRecord.fields[].typed`, where a spliced field used to carry its whole text as `before` and `after` (since collaborative text's phase 2, never released); `typingQuietMs` on the store handler's options; `PagesAppProps.typing` and `PageContext.typing`; `UndoTurn` takes several batches. What Graview Cloud does: takes this build; its `history` tool answers with `historyInWords` (or says a tick's `typed` with `typingWords`), and its notifications are told a run of typing once — a host that matches commits itself puts `holdTypingRuns` between `matchSubscriptions` and its delivery, and reads `fields[].typed` for a field typed into.

## 0.1.50

### Patch Changes

- 5edb2e7: The Suggested tray offers Not now beside Accept and Decline. It hides a suggestion from the person who pressed it until tomorrow, and keeps that as a `Dismissal` in the host's store of the person's own state (`dismissals`: `{ list, dismiss, onChange? }`, a `DismissalStore`, on `GraviewProvider`, `mount` and `PagesApp`). Without one, the tray keeps it in this browser: localStorage where the page may use it, else the page's memory. The log says nothing of it, so the suggestion still waits for everyone else and the app's own actor is held to nothing; Decline still answers for everyone. What the button does is said to a screen reader, and when a row settles the keyboard moves to the next row, or to Activity, never to `<body>`. Given what the app's own actor did while the person was away (`whileAway`: `{ tidied, waiting? }`, which `whileAway()` from `@graview/tools/tidy` returns), the tray says "One suggestion waiting · tidied 12 things while you were away", and it still draws when nothing waits but something was done. A seat gets the same answer as a tool: `withNotNow(runtime, dismissals)` from `@graview/tools` lists `dismiss_suggestion` (with `until`, an ISO time, if the person names one) and leaves what the person dismissed out of `list_suggestions`. The chat and the agent seat wrap their runtimes with it. It wraps the runtime instead of living in it, so the names Graview Cloud's worker imports stay at 1_999_652 bytes of their 2 MB. Activity folds everything one run of the app's own actor applied (`Lineage.run`) into one row, wherever a person's change fell between its batches, and says a run made overnight that ended today as "Tidied 12 things overnight". Where an actor gave no run, consecutive batches fold as before. A hosted page carries 0.1 KB more up front for it (105 bytes, 51 gzipped), and the scene face 480 more before it draws.
  
  Compatibility: additive. `dismissals` and `whileAway` are optional props, a host that passes neither gets the tray in this browser, and `dismiss_suggestion` is listed only by a runtime wrapped with `withNotNow`. The digest row a run makes is new: Activity rows for an actor that gives no `run` fold as before, and `ActivityRow`'s digest gains an optional `run`. Ops, stored formats, the wire, the document format and the tool schemas a bare `createToolRuntime` lists are unchanged.

## 0.1.49

### Patch Changes

- 9dab8bb: Every form asks in one order, whatever declared its act. 0.1.48 made a document's creating act ask for what names the record first; "Change the threat" still asked in the kind's field order, and an app written in TypeScript asked in its `z.object`'s key order, so a code app's "Add a threat" asked Aliases and CVSS before the name it must be given. Now one rule orders them all (`askedArguments`, `askedFieldsOf` and `inAskedOrder` on `@graview/core`, `askedFormFields` on `@graview/core/forms`): the fields the kind's label reads, in the order it reads them; then every other field the record cannot be made without (required, no default — in the schema or in a compiled document's `defaults`); then the rest; each run in the order the kind's page names them, then as declared. A TypeScript kind's label is a function, so it is asked which fields it says — handed a record whose every field marks its own name — and one that cannot be read that way falls back to a field called `name`, `title` or `label`. Only the arguments that are a field of the kind the act makes (else the one kind it changes) move, and only among their own places: a record to link to, the subject and an argument an act names for itself stay where the act put them. A derived `edit-<kind>` is built in this order, its `writes` and its sentence too: an edit is the form that made the record again, filled with what the record holds, so each field is where the person found it the first time — and what the record cannot be without still matters most when changing it, so "required first" holds for an edit as well, though every field of an edit may be left as it is. A TypeScript act's own input is left as declared; what asks reorders it: the pages face's form, a visitor's form that names no `fields`, the seat's described form (`describe_place`), the strip's open questions, the questions a decision is asked, the seat model's list of acts, the studio's offer form, `graview docs` and an agent's tool, whose `properties` are listed in the order a person is asked (`mutationToolSchema` takes the order as a second argument). `core`'s tests find a document's derived edit and a TypeScript app's creating form, edit, visitor form, described form and tool asking the name first, a label's fields read off a label function, a page's order kept, and an act's own input unchanged; `tools`' find the strip and an agent's tool asking a TypeScript act's name first. A hosted page carries 1.6 KB more up front, and so does one handed a compiled app — the store derives the edit in this order and the strip asks in it, so both are on every page — under raises of those budgets (`scripts/budget-raises/every-form-asks-in-one-order-*.json`), with the pages face alone (1.7 KB) and a visitor's form alone (1.5 KB).
  
  Compatibility: additive in the API — `askedArguments`, `askedFieldsOf` and `inAskedOrder` on `@graview/core`, `askedFormFields` on `@graview/core/forms`, and `mutationToolSchema`'s optional second argument. A behavior change in what is asked: a derived edit's input, `writes` and description list fields in the asked order, and a TypeScript app's forms, strip questions and agent tool schemas list arguments in it. Arguments are named, so every call is taken as before. A tool surface's hash is taken over sorted keys, so a property's place does not move it, but a derived edit's description now lists its fields in the asked order, so a surface with a derived edit whose fields were declared in another order hashes differently once. The declaration, the wire and the stored formats are unchanged. What Graview Cloud does: nothing — its forms ask the name first once it takes this version.

## 0.1.48

### Patch Changes

- 5b30150: A lens kept off the lists is kept off a record's "Seen in" and a kind's "Also as" too, and `add-lens` refused a taken title without saying whose it was. Threat Watch, made in Graview Cloud, was given a board per choice field and a calendar per date it never asked for; its author hid them with `set-lens-hidden` and drew their own. A record's page still said "Seen in:" every hidden board, first, ahead of the pictures the app meant to show, and the kind's list said them under "Also as:". Both now pass over a hidden place, which is still a page at its address, reached by a link or `navigate`. And `add-lens` with the title "Remediations by audience" answered only "there is already a lens called…", for a lens nobody could see on the page: it now names the lens that holds the title — what draws it and over what, and that it is kept off the lists, which does not free its title or its address — and its fix says `"replace": "<that title>"` draws the new one in its place. A lens drawn in the place of a hidden one stays hidden, as it did; the sentence `add-lens` says now says so, and that `set-lens-hidden` with `"hidden": false` shows it. `describeApp` (`graview describe`) marks a hidden lens among the lenses that draw.
  
  `pages`' tests find a record's page saying "Seen in" the listed picture and not the hidden one, saying nothing when its only pictures are hidden, the kind's list leaving the hidden one out of "Also as", and the hidden picture still a page; `core`'s find the refusal naming the lens, its lens and kind, and its being hidden, the replace taking a hidden lens's title and saying it stays hidden until shown, and `describeApp` marking it. `graview-shipped-lens` says so.
  
  Compatibility: a behavior change on the routed face only: a record's "Seen in" and a kind's "Also as" leave out lenses marked hidden. `add-lens`'s refusal of a taken title and its sentence for a replaced hidden lens read differently; what it refuses and applies is unchanged. The API, the declaration, the wire, the stored formats and the derived tools are unchanged.

## 0.1.47

### Patch Changes

- 7478654: A drawn view's accessibility is audited (Roadmap f-view-a11y-audit). `auditView(root)` from `@graview/guest/audit`, a new entry no page loads, holds what a worker view drew — its shadow root on a page, or a headless run's tree — to what a person on a keyboard or with a screen reader meets, and says each problem as a repair: the rule, its WCAG 2.2 criterion, how bad, where (a selector from the nearest `data-key`), a one-line fix, and `automatic` when the framework is the one to fix it rather than the view. It judges the drawing as the host draws it, so a link, a press and a control the view drew itself, which the host gives the keyboard, are no problem, and a `<div>` the view only hears a click on is. Eight rules run wherever the drawing is: `keyboard` (2.1.1), `role`, `name`, `toggle-state` and `nested-interactive` (4.1.2), `img-alt` and `svg-name` (1.1.1), and `scroll-region` (2.1.1). The ring the keyboard shows (`focus-visible`, 2.4.7 and 1.4.11) and text `contrast` (1.4.3) run only in a browser that lays the page out, and say how many elements they measured; anywhere else they are listed as skipped with why, never as passed, as is a keyboard trap (2.1.2), which needs keys pressed. `runWorkerViewHeadless(…, { audit: true })` audits what a run drew, with the elements its view listens on for a click — the view's runtime now says which those are — and `graview view check … --audit` prints it and fails on a problem the view must fix.
  
  A place's frame that scrolls its picture inside it (a lens, or a view with `flows: false`), and a draft view's frame, now take the keyboard and are named for the place: `role="group"` with the place's title, so the arrows scroll it and a screen reader says what it holds. A group rather than a region, because the view drawn in it is a region of that name already, and two landmarks of one name fail axe's `landmark-unique`.
  
  `guest`'s tests flag a `<div>` with only a click handler and pass one that says `role="button"`, pass an `<a data-record>` and a card bound to a record, flag a button with no name, a field with no label, a toggle with no state, a control inside another and a card that holds a button, an image with no alt and an SVG that is `role="img"` with no name, say which rules ran and which did not, audit Threat Watch headless with the clicks its view hears, and run `graview view check --audit` to its exit code. `pnpm verify a11y` draws Threat Watch's home and the guest sandbox's offers views on the pages face and audits each in the browser, light and dark: the host's ring and the views' words measure (15 rings and 23 words on Threat Watch), faint words and a ring taken away laid on the same page are found, the audit on the page says what the headless run says, the framework's markup around each view passes the audit, a framed place is a box named for it that Tab reaches, Tab goes into each view and out again, and what axe finds inside a view the audit finds too. `graview-view-links` says how to run it. The view's runtime is 162 bytes larger (48 gzipped), under raises of its budget and of the guest host's that inlines it (`scripts/budget-raises/a-drawn-views-accessibility-is-audited-runtime.json`, `…-open-kit.json`); nothing a hosted page loads up front grew.
  
  Compatibility: additive for a host — `@graview/guest/audit` is a new entry, `runWorkerViewHeadless`'s `audit` and `graview view check`'s `--audit` are optional, and a headless run's transcript may carry `clicks`; a framed place's scrolling box is now a tab stop named for the place. The wire, the stored formats, the declaration and the derived tools are unchanged.
- fbb080a: A place is said drawn once its lens has drawn, so `graview shots`, `graview preview`'s pages and a host waiting on `data-graview-drawn` or `onDrawn` picture the lens rather than its page's head over nothing. Threat Watch's What to do place — a blocks lens declared in the document — was photographed as its title, "8 remediations." and "All remediations as a list", with the rest of the page blank: the lens's module had arrived, so nothing was said to be on its way, but React reveals a part that suspended in a commit it holds back up to 300 ms after the page around it first drew, and the page had been still long enough to be said settled (about 310 ms after load, the rows drawn at about 380). Now each part that suspends on a watched place — a lazy module's part, and a component behind `lazyPart` — is waited for until it has drawn there, or said it did not arrive; a place left before such a part drew does not hold up the next one. The place is said drawn about 240 ms after its lens draws, and a move between places still settles in about 300 ms. A hosted page carries 0.9 KB more up front for it (0.3 KB gzipped; the compiled page too), and both budgets are raised by that.
  
  Compatibility: ops, stored formats, the wire, the declaration, check findings and derived tools are unchanged. New on `@graview/react` and `@graview/react/provider`: `PartsWatch`, `PartsWatchContext`, `createPartsWatch`, `partsWatchMoved`, `partsWaiting` and `lazyPart` (`React.lazy` whose part is waited for until it has drawn). `data-graview-drawn` and `onDrawn` keep their meaning and come later only where a part was still to be drawn. What Graview Cloud does: nothing — describe_place's pictures of a declared lens's place show the lens without a wait of its own.
- f85abad: A worker view's link in an embed carries the host's own address, or none, and a focus ring at the edge of a view's region is drawn whole. In an embed whose host keeps its own addresses — memory routing, a chat's widget, an article — an `<a data-record>` carried the routed face's base and path as its `href` (`/tasks/t1`), so a Cmd-click opened a page of the host's site that may not exist. The embed now takes `hrefOf(path)`, the host's address for a page of the app, and a view's links, the bar's places and the Scene's Open carry what it returns; under memory routing with none given they carry no `href`, and a view's link is a link by its role and a tab stop, as on the scene, followed in the app on a click, a tap and Enter. Under address routing they carry the face's own address unless the host says otherwise. `PagesApp` takes the same `hrefOf`, or `false` for no address; `PageDoor.href` may answer none, and the Scene's Open is then a link by its role. And a worker view's region clips what the view draws to its box, which cut the ring of a control flush with its edge in half: while something in it has the keyboard the region lets the ring's reach — its 2px and its 2px offset — paint past its edge (`overflow-clip-margin`, in Chromium and Firefox) and nothing else, still contained, isolated and clipped; in WebKit, which has no `overflow-clip-margin`, a control found within that reach of the edge as it takes the keyboard draws its ring inside its own edge. A Scene card's body clips only its top and foot, and lets the ring past them while the keyboard is in it.
  
  `embed`'s tests find a view's link given no address under memory routing, the host's under `hrefOf`, and the face's own under address routing; `guest`'s find the region letting the ring past while the keyboard is in it and, in an engine without `overflow-clip-margin`, a control at the edge marked to draw its ring inside. In the taps transport, in Chromium, WebKit and Firefox, Threat Watch is mounted with the host's addresses (`/?at=<path>`): its `<a data-record>` carries the host's address, a Cmd- or Ctrl-click opens the host's page there on the threat's page, and a plain click opens it in the app; mounted with none, the link carries no `href`, is a link by its role and a tab stop, and Enter opens the threat's page; and a link flush with the region's left edge, given the keyboard, shows all four sides of its ring. `graview-embed` and `graview-view-links` say so. A hosted page carries 44 bytes more up front (0.0 KB more up front, 47 gzipped; 44 / 60 on the page handed a compiled app), and the guest host drawing a worker view on the open kit 647 bytes more (235 gzipped), under raises of those budgets (`scripts/budget-raises/a-views-link-carries-the-hosts-address-*.json`, `a-views-ring-is-whole-at-its-regions-edge-open-kit.json`).
  
  Compatibility: additive for a host — the embed's and `PagesApp`'s `hrefOf` are optional; under memory routing an embed's view links and the Scene's Open carry no `href` unless the host gives `hrefOf`, where they carried the face's own path; under address routing nothing changes. `PageDoor.href` may return `undefined`. The wire, the stored formats, the declaration and the derived tools are unchanged.
- 6f9bf30: A worker view's links are real links. An `<a data-record>` or `<a data-place>` was followed on a press but was no link to the browser: no address on hover, nothing in a new tab on Cmd- or Ctrl-click, nothing to copy — the host gave it a `role="link"` and a `tabindex` in place of an `href`. Now on the routed pages the host gives each the address the face goes to as its `href`, with the face's base, filter and section (`/a/<app>/threats/threat%3A7`, `/places/by-day?filter=day%3Afri`), opened in this tab: a plain click, a tap and Enter are followed in the app as before, and a click with Cmd, Ctrl, Shift or Alt, or a middle click, is the browser's — a new tab, a new window — and never also taken by the face around the view. With an address, the link needs no role or tab stop of the host's, and they are taken back; on the scene, which has no addresses, a link keeps them. The view still never writes an app's address itself: an `href` it gives is a link out, judged as before, and an `<a>` with one is left as the open kit drew it. A link out alone says it opens a new tab. `GoTo` (`@graview/react/provider`) takes an optional `href(to, also)`, the address going there arrives at, and the routed face's gives it (`/a/<app>` joined on, as a listed record's link does); a host that mounts a worker view itself gives `hrefOf` on `mountWorkerView`, or none.
  
  `guest`'s tests give a record link and a place link their addresses with no role, tab stop or target, follow a plain click and leave Cmd, Ctrl, Shift and middle clicks unprevented, unfollowed and unheard by the face, give no address to a link that goes nowhere or on a face with none, and put the open kit's target back when a link stops naming a record. `pages`' tests read the routed face's `href` for a record, a list narrowed and to a section, and a lens under `basename="/a/hall"`, the address the face then arrives at, and none for what it does not have. In the taps transport, in Chromium, WebKit and Firefox, Threat Watch's `<a data-record>` carries `/threats/threat%3Aphishing`, a Cmd- or Ctrl-click opens a new tab there and leaves the app on its home, and a plain click opens the threat's page at that address. `graview-view-links` says so. The guest host drawing a worker view on the open kit carries 4.1 KB more for the three changes together (4_094 bytes, 1_381 gzipped), and the guest host before a worker is drawn 207 bytes more (36 gzipped), under raises of those budgets (`scripts/budget-raises/a-views-keyboard-and-real-links-open-kit.json`, `…-guest-host.json`); nothing a hosted page loads up front grew.
  
  Compatibility: additive for a host — `GoTo.href` and `mountWorkerView`'s `hrefOf` are optional, and a host that gives neither draws links as before; on the routed pages a view's `<a data-record>` and `<a data-place>` now carry an `href` and no `role` or `tabindex`. The wire, the stored formats, the declaration and the derived tools are unchanged.

## 0.1.46

### Patch Changes

- 15f234f: A titled worker view's place flows with the page, and a brand may keep the line under the app's name off the home without blanking the description. Every view opened at `/places/<slug>` was drawn in a frame `min(72vh, 760px)` tall with a border that scrolled inside itself, under the page's own scroll — on a phone, a box that traps the thumb — while the home's view and a place of blocks flowed. `registerWorkerView` now marks the view it registers `flows`, as a place of blocks is, and its place is drawn in the page's column as long as the view draws; a calendar, a board, the columns and the other pictures keep their frame. `graview-worker-view` says a view's place flows with the page and needs no height to fill one. And with a home view the routed face drew the document's `description` above it, so a view with its own hero said everything twice, and the only way to stop it was to delete the description, which took it out of the app's listings too. A document's `brand.subtitle: false` now keeps the line off the home — over a home view, under a home of blocks and on the derived home alike — and the description stays the app's: the brand's `subtitle`, the name's hover on the bar, its listings. `set-brand` writes it (`subtitle: false`; `true` or `null` draws the line again) and says so in words, the diff of a change says it the same way, the compiled brand carries `subtitleDrawn: false`, and `describePlace` leaves the line out of the home's masthead. Left out, the line is drawn as it always was. `graview-brand` documents the key. `guest`'s tests find a titled view's place marked to flow and drawn with no height and nothing scrolling inside it, and `pages`' find a calendar, a board and the columns keeping their frame; `verify-address` opens a kept view's place and the board at 1440 and 390 in Chromium, WebKit and Firefox, and finds no box between the view and the page that scrolls while the board's frame does (aTitledViewsPlaceFlowsWithThePageWhileABoardKeepsItsFrame). `core`'s tests find the key validated as false and refused as anything else, written by `set-brand` and taken back by `true` and `null` to the very document, and the masthead without the line; `pages`' find the home drawing no line with it and the line without it, the description still the name's hover. A hosted page carries 0.0 KB more up front (3 bytes, 1 gzipped) and the guest host registering a worker view 6 (1), under raises of those budgets by that (`scripts/budget-raises/a-views-place-flows-*.json`).
  
  Compatibility: additive in the declaration — `brand.subtitle`, optional and only `false`; a document without it draws as it did, and a build that predates it refuses a document that carries it. Additive in the API: `Brand.subtitleDrawn` and the `set-brand` edit's `subtitle`. A titled worker view's place no longer scrolls inside itself; the wire, the stored formats and the derived tools are unchanged.
- 99d25eb: Built-in views look designed: one type scale, one left edge and one heading per page on the place header, blocks lenses, grouped row and card lists, kind lists and record pages (Roadmap f-auto-view-type-polish). Threat Watch's By type page stacked two headings — the place's title and count, then the lens's own larger headline saying the count again — inset its rows 16 pixels past the title's edge, ruled each group label with a one-sided accent, drew 69 row titles in the display face, said "high · resolved · supply-chain · disclosed May 18, 2026" as one string, and pressed its arrow against "All threats as a list→". Now a blocks lens that opens with a headline hands it to its page, which says it as the line under the title in place of the count and draws it once; on its page the lens stands in the page's own column, with no inset of its own; a group's heading is its label and how many it holds, in the text face, over the rows' own hairline; a record's name in a row or on a card is the text face at medium or semibold weight and normal tracking, never the display face; a card's or a row's line of facts with a choice field named bare in it draws each choice as a small chip and the rest as quiet words after them, in the order written, and a kind's list draws its glance's choices the same way; an arrow after a link (→, ↗) stands a space from its words and is not underlined; a place's header, its first block, its blocks and its groups are spaced on a 4px scale, given as tokens (`--graview-space-1` … `--graview-space-7`: 4, 8, 12, 16, 24, 32, 48); a page's title steps one step down the scale on a phone; and on a desk wide enough for a margin, a kind's list hangs its choose boxes in that margin so its names start on the title's edge. A hosted page carries 0.3 KB more up front for it (the compiled page too), and their budgets are raised by that; the pages face alone by what it measured.
  
  Compatibility: ops, stored formats, the wire and derived tools are unchanged; the declaration is unchanged and no check finding is added. New: a resolved text block on a card or a row carries `runs` (each fact, a choice marked by its `field`) where one of its facts is a choice field named bare; describe_place says such a line as it is drawn, a chip a space from what is beside it ("High Contained Supply chain disclosed May 18, 2026") and a grouped list's group carries its `count`, said in the text as "Supply-chain compromises (6):"; the theme writes `--graview-space-1` … `--graview-space-7`; `PlacePageHead` on `@graview/primitives/pages`. What Graview Cloud does: nothing to keep Threat Watch's v19 workaround — a lens may open with a headline again, and its page says it once, under the title.

## 0.1.45

### Patch Changes

- 7069188: A form for a visitor: one act of an app opened to people who never join the workspace — an RSVP, a sign-up — drawn alone, run as a visitor the host mints per answer, its answer landing as a record and nothing else of the app shown. Roadmap f-public-forms, filed by Graview Cloud: "The pieces are in place (people records, link roles, the files route); what is left is a form page per kind for a visitor with no account, a minted author per submission ('form:<token>'), abuse limits, and the plan gate." The framework's half is here; abuse limits, the plan gate and minting the token stay the host's.
  
  The declaration says the form: `forms.<name>` in a document, `forms` on `defineApp` — `act` (one that makes a record or acts on none), `title`, `intro`, `fields` (the arguments asked, in order; unsaid, every one a visitor could fill), `say` (what the visitor reads under "Received — thank you."), `submit` and `role` (`visitor`, unsaid). It is not a second permission system: a visitor is a principal holding that role, so the policy says what they may do and see as it says it for anybody — a grant lets the role run the act, and a sight that never names it keeps every record from it. `formVisitor(app, "rsvp", "form:<token>")` (`@graview/core/forms`) mints `{ kind: "human", id, name: "a visitor via the RSVP form", roles: ["visitor"] }`, so Activity names each answer for the form and never by its token (`nameOfAuthor` says "a visitor via a form" for a `form:` id that carries no name); `visitorForm(app, name)` resolves the act, the fields and the words; `formReceived` is what is said after. `graview check` holds a form to what a visitor can do: `form-unknown-act`, `form-act-on-a-record`, `form-without-sight` (no policy, or one that says nothing of who sees what, so the visitor would be served every record), `form-act-not-granted`, `form-field-unknown`, `form-omits-required` and `form-field-unfillable` (a required picker over records the visitor is not served, or a field their sight keeps from them) are errors; `form-visitor-sees` (a sight serves the visitor a kind — to everybody, or by name where no picker asks for it) and `form-visitor-may-more` (the role may run acts no form opens) are warnings.
  
  `@graview/embed/form`'s `mount(el, { app, form: "rsvp", principal: "form:<token>", remote })` draws the form alone — no bar, no lists, no record, no seat — over the store as the visitor is served it, runs the act as them, says it was received in the declaration's words with the keyboard on what it said, and tells `onSubmitted`; `principal` is the minted id or the visitor whole, and a form the declaration has not got is refused by its name. `@graview/pages/visitor-form`'s `VisitorFormPage` is the same for a React host. The derived form asks the arguments `only` names in that order. `graview preview` serves each form at `/forms/<name>`, a visitor minted per page and handed only what that visitor is served, and the page's head no longer preloads the preview seat's document there. The store, the act and the served seat needed nothing new: the visitor's seat is served nothing, its log withholds every record, and an id it guesses is refused as missing.
  
  `core`'s tests read the form from a document and give it back, resolve its fields and words, mint the visitor, run the act as them and find the record on the whole store and its author named for the form, refuse another act, serve the seat nothing before and after its answer (its answer and its log name no other record, and a guessed id is told the same as an absent one), and say every finding; `pages`' draw the RSVP alone, submit as the visitor and say it was received; `embed`'s mount it and answer it. `pnpm verify preview` opens `/forms/rsvp` over a seeded RSVP at 390 and 1440 in Chromium, WebKit and Firefox: the title and its four fields, no bar and no link, nothing sideways, the guest on the store as "a visitor via the RSVP form" with a visitor minted per page, the keyboard on "Received", and none of the records already there drawn on the page or in anything the server sent it. The `graview-visitor-forms` skill says the declaration, the grant and the mount, and `graview-permissions` points at it. A visitor's form alone is a new bundle budget, set at 373_310 / 143_118 (380_000 / 146_000), with none of the bar, the notices or the first look (the frame's store and host scheme are a module of their own, `frame-light.ts`). A hosted page carries 0.2 KB more up front (232 bytes, 87 gzipped: the document's `forms` shape and "a visitor via a form"), and the compiled page 0.1 KB (88 bytes, 45 gzipped), under a raise of each budget by that (`scripts/budget-raises/a-form-for-a-visitor-a-hosted-page*.json`); the pages face alone 93 bytes over its budget minified, none gzipped, under a raise (`a-form-for-a-visitor-pages-face.json`).
  
  Compatibility: additive for the declaration and the document format — `forms` is a new optional key, which a build before this refuses in a document as a key it does not know; the `form-*` check codes are new. Additive in the API: `@graview/embed/form` (`mount`, `FormEmbed`, `FormEmbedOptions`, `FormEmbedHandle`), `@graview/pages/visitor-form` (`VisitorFormPage`), `formVisitor`, `visitorForm`, `formReceived`, `visitorSeesKind`, `pickersIn`, `VISITOR_ROLE`, `VisitorForm` on `@graview/core/forms`, `VisitorFormDeclaration` and `GraviewApp.forms`, `themeBaseCss` on `@graview/primitives/pages`. The wire, the stored formats and the derived tools are unchanged: a visitor is a seat like any other, said in the seat headers a host already believes.
- 0ffa768: A worker view may be drawn edge to edge on a phone. Threat Watch, on Graview Cloud, put a hero at the head of its home and of each record page, and at 390 pixels the page's column stood 20px of gutter either side of it: 40 pixels of 390 the view could not reach, and its `margin: 0 -14px` was clipped at its box's edge, cutting the hero's rounded corners rather than widening it. A manifest now says `bleed: true` (`WorkerViewManifest.bleed`, which `checkManifest` takes as true or left out), and where the pages face draws that view — the home's body, a record's page — on a face 680 pixels wide or less, its box takes the gutter back, less the device's safe areas (`env(safe-area-inset-left)` and `-right`), so it is the phone's width; at a desk it is the column's width, as every view is. Nothing else moves: a view without it is drawn inside the gutter as before, and what any view draws past its box is still clipped there. `registerWorkerView` marks the view (`markBleeds`, `bleeds` and `BLEEDS` beside `markReplacesPage` on `@graview/react` and `/provider`), the pages draw it in `Bleed`, the column's sides are `var(--graview-page-gutter, 20px)` so the gutter and what a view takes back are one number, and the column says `data-graview-page-main`. `graview-view-drawing` says where a view's box is and what `bleed` changes, and `graview-worker-view` names the key. `guest`'s tests find a view that bleeds drawn in a box that takes the gutter back on a phone and any other view as it was, and the key refused as anything but true; `verify-address` mounts the tasks as Cloud does with a home view and a record view that bleed, and at 390 each spans the viewport less its safe areas with nothing scrolling sideways, at 1440 the column's width (aViewThatBleedsSpansAPhoneLessItsSafeAreasAndTheColumnAtADesk). The pages face carries 722 bytes more (191 gzipped), a hosted page 0.2 KB more up front (186 bytes, 49 and 37 gzipped, handed a compiled app or not), the guest host registering a worker view 288 (70) and drawing one on the open kit 122 (22), under raises of those budgets by that (`scripts/budget-raises/a-view-that-bleeds-*.json`).
  
  Compatibility: additive in the manifest — `bleed`, optional; a manifest without it draws as it did, and a build that predates it passes it over and draws the view inside the gutter. Additive in the API: `markBleeds`, `bleeds`, `BLEEDS`, `Bleed`'s attribute `data-graview-bleed` and the column's `data-graview-page-main`. The declaration, the wire, the stored formats and the derived tools are unchanged.
- 8ab4fa2: The keyboard goes to what an act asks, a record's acts are one stop for Tab, and the scene's offer to take a change back says ⌘Z. The journeys held each job's keyboard cost to twice the pointer's or named why, and four reasons were left from 0.1.40: relate and repair on the pages reached the field an act asks for by Tab, undo on the scene went to Activity at the far end of the bar, and the discography's refused job crossed every district before the Songs. `DerivedForm` takes `autoFocus`: drawn with it, the keyboard goes to the first field that must be answered and is not — past a note nobody was asked for and what the record already holds — else the first field, else its button, and only while the keyboard has gone nowhere else since (a date's editor that is still arriving is waited for). The derived record page's acts open their forms with it, where they focused the form's first box a frame after the press and, on a slow page, nothing; and `Repairs` opens a repair's question with it, where the keyboard stayed on the repair. The derived record's acts are one Tab stop (`useRoving`, a `toolbar` named "What can be done"): the arrows go along them and a letter to the next that begins with it, where each was a stop and the tenth ten Tabs from the first; "Skip to what can be done" lands on the act that holds the stop. On the scene, a change's offer says on its Take back that ⌘Z or Ctrl+Z does the same (`aria-keyshortcuts`, and in its title), from a new `keys` on `NoticeAction` that any notice's act may say. todo's, rota's and the discography's own record designs do the same with their acts and forms. And Find, going into a kind from the keyboard, lands on the first control of its arranging line that is drawn: on a phone the line folds Sort into "Arrange", the keys went to the folded Sort, which takes none, and stayed in the box, so the next key typed into it. `pages`' tests open an act and find the keyboard in the choice it asks, past an optional note; find the acts one stop, a letter moving to the act; take the keyboard into a form drawn with `autoFocus` and leave it where it is without; and open a repair's question with the keyboard in it. `primitives`' find ⌘Z said on the scene's Take back, and Find landing past a folded Sort. The journeys press ⌘Z where a page says it takes a change back, go to a kind's district by name from the keyboard (as narrowing does) for a refused act, and answer a form a page holds open rather than pressing its button first: relate on the pages is 8 keys for the pointer's 4 on todo and rota where it was 10 and 14, undo on the scene one key for two where it was 8–10, a refused act on the scene four for two (the discography, where it was twelve) and four for four or five (rota, where it was nine), and those reasons leave `KEYBOARD_WHY`. What stays is said for what it is: the garden's plot page draws every act's form open (11 for 3), two of a song's acts begin with P (9 for 4, where it was 16), the problems are a page of their own across the bar (repair on the pages 9–15, two keys fewer where a repair asks), and a record chosen from the address is skipped to before its acts' key (3 for 1). The pages face alone carries 0.8 KB more (819 bytes, 261 gzipped), under a raise of its budget by that (`scripts/budget-raises/the-keyboard-goes-to-what-an-act-asks-pages-face.json`).
  
  Compatibility: additive in the API — `DerivedFormProps.autoFocus` and `NoticeAction.keys`. A behavior change on the derived record page: its acts are one Tab stop moved through by the arrows and letters, and an act's form opened takes the keyboard to the field it asks for rather than its first box; a repair's question, opened, takes the keyboard. The declaration, the wire, the stored formats and the derived tools are unchanged.

## 0.1.44

### Patch Changes

- 10ea092: A view the host keeps is a place in the nav, and a kind kept off the places leaves the Pages drop-down. Roadmap q-custom-views-in-nav, filed from the hosted En Dash Social Media Playbook: its "To do" is a script view a chat drew and the host keeps by its manifest, and it showed only as an "Also as" link on the tasks list — `arrange-pages` refused it in `order` ('"to-do" is not a kind this app has') and in `first` ('"To do" is not a place, a kind or "home"'), since the declaration cannot know a host's views. The declaration now names one as `view:<its manifest name>` in `pages.order`, `pages.first` and `pages.primary`. Where the host hands its views' manifests to the edit (`EditOptions.views`, `HostView`: `name`, `title`, `attach`), `arrange-pages` takes the view's title or name and writes `view:<name>` (`order: ["To do"]` is `["view:to-do"]`), and refuses a name the host does not keep, naming the ones it does; unsaid, it takes `view:<name>` on the host's word and says "(once the host registers it)". `graview check` and the checked `compileDocument` (`CheckOptions.views`, `CompileOptions.views`) say a `view:` name no view registered here has is a note, `pages-view-not-drawn-here` — not drawn here yet, it stands where it is named once the host registers it — and, told the host's views, a name none of them is a warning, `pages-view-unknown`. On the page, the bar's places list a registered view `pages.order` names among the Lists at its place in the order (`arrangedEntries`), standing on a wide bar unless `pages.primary` says others; `pages.first` opens on it (`openingOf` reads `view:<name>` by the registration's name); the home's gallery puts it first. `placesOf(app, { views })` lists the host's titled pictures of a kind, each with `view: <name>`, where the order names them and `first` where `pages.first` does — what a host lists when an app is opened, and the places a drawn view's `data-place` is judged against.
  
  And a kind `pages.hide` names really leaves the Pages drop-down: it was dropped from the home only and folded into More, so the playbook's eleven reference lists made the drop-down "insanely long". It is listed only while it is the place you are on, so the bar still says where you are, and it still opens at its address, in Find and from wherever it is linked. `set-lens-hidden` on a kind's name or plural (`title: "source"`) writes `pages.hide` where no lens has that title, refused where `pages.primary` stands the kind; the place menu's Hide on a kind's list, for whoever may change the declaration, hands the host's `keptViews.lens` that edit, and the drop-down's Hidden lists each kept-off list with Show. `arrange-pages` and the preview of a change say "the places and the front page leave off …". `core`'s tests name a view by title and by `view:<name>`, with and without the host's views, refuse the rest in words, check the note and the warning, list and open the view, and hide and show a kind's list; `pages`' render the drop-down with the view first among the lists and without the hidden kinds, and the hidden list at its address; `embed`'s hide a list from the place menu and show one from Hidden. `pnpm verify address` mounts the tasks as Cloud's shell does with a script view registered as `to-do` and `pages: { order: ["view:to-do", "task"], first: "view:to-do", hide: ["person"] }`: the bare address opens on it, it stands first after Home, and the people are not listed and still open at `/people`. A hosted page carries 0.9 KB more up front (903 bytes, about 340 gzipped; the compiled page the same), the bar's arranged list and the hidden kinds' Show, under a raise of each budget by that (`scripts/budget-raises/a-kept-view-is-a-place-in-the-nav-*.json`); the pages face alone 1.4 KB more (1_479, 572 gzipped), and the scene face's before-draw line is 964 KB.
  
  Compatibility: additive for the declaration — `pages.order`, `pages.first` and `pages.primary` may name `view:<name>`, which a build before 0.1.44 reads as a kind or place it does not have and passes over (`graview check` warned of it), and `arrange-pages` takes a title or `view:<name>` in `order` where it took only kinds. A behavior change for `pages.hide`: a kind it names is kept off the bar's places as well as the home. `set-lens-hidden` takes a kind where no lens has the title, where it refused. Additive in the API: `HostView`, `arrangedEntries`, `viewReference`, `ArrangedEntry`, `EditOptions.views`, `CompileOptions.views`, `CheckOptions` and `checkApp`'s second argument, `placesOf`'s `views`, `AppPlace.view`, `BarPlace.hidden`; `arrangementFindings` takes `{ views }` where it took a list of places. The wire, the stored formats and the derived tools are unchanged.

## 0.1.42

### Patch Changes

- a5bbda0: A brand's logo has one for each scheme. Nick, building the TrueNorth recon app: the brand took an emoji or one mark, a client's real logo (a JPG on white) had nowhere else to go, and on a dark theme it sat as a white slab — the app drew it under `filter: invert(1) hue-rotate(180deg)` and `mix-blend-mode: screen` of its own. `brand.logo` is still a string, or now `{ light, dark? }`: the light one in the light scheme, the dark one in the dark, the light one there too where there is none. Whether a light logo is opaque on white cannot be known from it, so inverting it is asked for rather than guessed: `{ light, darkFallback: "invert" }` draws it inverted and screened onto the ground in the dark scheme alone, and beside a `dark` one it is never used, which `graview check` says (`brand-logo`, a warning at `brand.logo.darkFallback`). In a document the same object takes `alt` (`{ "src", "alt" }` and a string still mean one logo), each mark is judged as the one was (`brand-mark` at `brand.logo.light` or `brand.logo.dark`), and `set-brand` takes `logo: { light, dark }` and says "The logo gets one of its own for the dark scheme." (or "The logo is drawn inverted in the dark scheme.", "The logo is the same in both schemes."), as the preview of a change does. A declared app's logo is checked too now: a pair with no light one, a fallback other than `"invert"` (`brand-logo`), and a mark that could act (`brand-mark`). Contrast checks are on the colors, as before.
  
  The default shell's bar and a design's own draw it with `AppMark` (`@graview/primitives`, handed the brand on `context.brand`): both marks are in the page, each `data-graview-scheme-only="light"` or `"dark"`, and the theme's sheet for the scheme the page is in hides the other's — so the logo switches with the scheme however the page chose it, with no hook — and a light one the brand asked to invert carries `data-graview-logo-fallback="invert"`, which only the dark sheet styles. `logoOf(brand)` and `logoFor(brand, scheme)` (`{ src, invert }`) on `@graview/core` are for a design that draws its own image. The static page's masthead does the same; a worker view is handed the logo its scheme draws. `graview-logo`, a skill of its own, says all of it: `graview-brand` gives its marks to it, split along that seam rather than shaved. A hosted page carries 1.5 KB more up front for it (1_541 bytes, 509 gzipped; the compiled page 1.4 KB, 1_461 / 482), under a raise of each budget by that; the scene face before it draws 1_541 bytes more, the pages face alone 982 / 337, the embed without the studio 987 / 318, every face 4_241 / 1_439, the embed with the studio 4_161 / 1_421, and the guest host 80 bytes before a worker is drawn and on the open kit, each under a raise of its budget by what it measured.
  
  Compatibility: ops, stored formats, the wire and derived tools are unchanged. The declaration: `brand.logo` gains a third form, `{ light, dark?, alt?, darkFallback?: "invert" }`, and every document that compiled still compiles the same; the TypeScript `Brand.logo` is `string | BrandLogo`, so code that read it as a string reads `logoOf(brand)?.light`. Check finding codes: new `brand-logo` (an error for a declared app's pair with no light one or a fallback it does not know, a warning for a fallback beside a dark one), and `brand-mark` now also at `brand.logo.light` and `brand.logo.dark`, and for a declared app's logo. New on `@graview/core`: `logoOf`, `logoFor` and the type `BrandLogo`. The `set-brand` edit takes `logo: { light, dark?, alt?, darkFallback? }`. What Graview Cloud does: `add_image` each file as now, then `{"op": "set-brand", "logo": {"light": "/graview/assets/<sha>.jpg", "dark": "/graview/assets/<sha>.png"}}` (or `"darkFallback": "invert"` with a light one alone, when the person says the logo is a picture on white); a page that draws its own masthead uses `AppMark` or `logoFor`, and a stylesheet it writes itself for a scheme hides `[data-graview-scheme-only]` of the other.
- 4fe0969: A page says where you are. Nick, from building a product face with its own shell over the pages: "people got caught in the derived pages and didn't know where they were". On a step's page nothing said "Plan › Steps › Consolidate", no tab lit, and the app rebuilt a trail by matching `pluralSlug` against the path and reading the record's label. Each derived list, record and picture now opens with a trail: a `nav` called "You are here", the section the kind's pages are in, the kind's list as a link, and the page itself last with `aria-current="page"`, said to a screen reader and kept out of sight because the heading under it says it. It stands on one line at a phone's width, a long step cut short rather than wrapped, and its hrefs carry the face's base. It takes the place of the record page's kind link above the name; a list with no section has nothing before its heading and draws none. `registry.section(kinds, label, { path })` names the section ("Plan" over change plans and their steps; without a `path` the trail says it and links nowhere) and `registry.sectionOf(kind)` reads it back. A design that draws its own shell asks `usePlaceHere()`: `{ as: "home" | "list" | "record" | "place" | "page", kind, id, label, section, trail: [{ label, href, path, current }] }`, to light its own tab and draw its own title. A bar outside the face's router, told the path by `steering` or `onNavigate`, reads `placeHere(context, path, { registry, base })`. A shell that places `<PageTrail />` holds THE trail, and the derived pages leave theirs to it; `surface("shell", Shell, { without: ["trail"] })` goes without. Seedbed's garden puts plots and plantings in "The garden", and its own plot page places `<PageTrail />` where its eyebrow was. The product-design rung of `graview-pages` is a skill of its own now, `graview-pages-design`, which teaches the section, the hook and the trail. A hosted page carries nothing more up front; the pages face before it draws is about 3 KB more, and the pages face alone, every face and the embed with the studio handed in are raised by what they measured (3_330 / 1_219, 3_288 / 1_195 and 3_299 / 1_207). `verify-pages` holds that a record's page says where it is, its trail's link goes to the kind's list under `/pages`, and a planting's page says "The garden › Plantings", at 390 and 1440, in Chromium, WebKit and Firefox.
  
  Compatibility: ops, stored formats, the wire, the declaration and derived tools are unchanged. Additive on the pages' API (`PageTrail`, `usePlaceHere`, `placeHere`, `PlaceHere`, `PlaceStep`, `PageSection`, `registry.section`, `registry.sectionOf`) and on `FaceControl` (`"trail"`). On the markup, the record page's kind link above its name is now the list step of `nav[aria-label="You are here"]` (`[data-graview-trail]`), and a list or a picture in a named section opens with one.
- 6809e5a: Undo and Find stand at a design bar's own height. Nick, building a product face with its own bar (the TrueNorth recon app): placed in it, `<PageUndo>` said "Take back “…the whole act's words…”" at whatever width that took and pushed Find to a sliver, and both carried inline styles the design could beat only with `!important` — the app replaced Undo's words with a CSS `::after`. `<PageUndo compact />` is the arrow and "Undo", with what it takes back as its accessible name and in its title; uncompact, its sentence is cut at 18rem (`--graview-undo-widest`) and still whole in its title. Both take `className` (on Undo's button, on Find's form; its box is `.graview-find-box`) and `size` — `"sm"` (30 px, the bar's tools), `"md"` (36 px), or a height as a number of pixels or a CSS length, said as `--graview-control-height` — and what they look like is now rules behind `:where` a design's one class outweighs, not a style attribute. Placed nowhere, each stands as before: Find at 30 px, the way back at 36. `graview-pages-design` says so: graview-pages had no room left under its limit, so its last rung — a product design over every surface — is a skill of its own, split along its seam, with Find and the way back in the design's bar beside it; 27 skills now. The pages face alone carries 0.5 KB more up front (482 bytes, 192 gzipped) and the embed with the studio the same (482 / 187), every face 1.4 KB in all (1_390 / 576), each under a raise of its budget by that; a hosted page is unchanged.
  
  Compatibility: ops, stored formats, the wire, the declaration and derived tools are unchanged. New on `@graview/pages`: `compact`, `className` and `size` on `PageUndo`, `className` and `size` on `PageFind`, and the types `ControlSize` and `UndoLook`. The placed way back's markup is additive: `.graview-undo` on its button, `.graview-undo-place` on its box, `data-compact` and `data-size` where said; Find's form is `.graview-find` and its box `.graview-find-box`, with no style attribute on either. A shell that placed `PageUndo` sees a long sentence cut at 18rem where it stood uncut.

## 0.1.40

### Patch Changes

- 752da2b: `spatialHref(id, sceneHref = "/")` takes the scene's address, as `placeHref` already did. It answered `/#focus=<id>` whatever the host, so a host whose scene lives at `/scene/places/overview` could not link a page of its own to a record there. Now `spatialHref("plot:north-bed", "/scene/places/overview")` is `/scene/places/overview#focus=plot%3Anorth-bed`, and unsaid it is `/` as before. A project `graview create` writes passes its PagesApp context's `sceneHref` to it. The `@graview/pages` README and the `graview-pages` skill say to set `sceneHref` in the PagesApp context when the scene is not at `/`, and pass it on.
  
  Compatibility: ops, stored formats, the wire, the declaration and derived tools are unchanged; `spatialHref(id)` answers what it did.
- c3b7ab9: The keyboard reaches a long list's records, a chosen card's acts and the problems in few presses. Walks XI and XII counted what each job costs from the keyboard against the pointer (`pnpm verify journeys`): relating two records on the scene took 31 to 49 presses where the pointer took 3 to 5, the gauntlet's repair 46 against 7, choosing three of its talks 52 against 5, and a list's first box was up to 24 Tabs from its heading. The bar's first stops are now ways past it, unseen until the keyboard is on one (`SkipLinks`, WCAG 2.4.1): on the scene "Skip to <what is chosen>", whose card then opens its acts on its key, "Skip to the picture" and, while a rule is broken, "Skip to the 2 rules that are broken", which opens them; on the pages "Skip to the content" — a list's records where the page is a list — and, on a record's page, "Skip to what can be done", whoever designed the page. A list's own heading is followed by "Skip to the 30 tasks", onto the boxes' one stop, and "Skip to arranging them", onto "Filter". A menu of acts is one Tab stop (`useRoving`): the arrows go down it, Home and End to its ends, a letter to the next act whose words begin with it, and an act's pin is the arrow to its right, out of Tab's way. An ask's choices are one stop the same way, its filter's arrow down goes into them, and an ask opened by its act's own press takes the keyboard, on its filter where the choices are many. `focusInto`, `SkipLinks`, `useRoving`, `rovingItems`, `ROVING` and `ROVING_ITEM` are on `@graview/primitives` and its `/pages`, `SceneSkips` on the main entry, and `AppBar` takes `skips`. Across every example's jobs but making one, the keyboard's presses fell from 1,995 to 1,103 — relating on the scene to 6 to 9, a repair on the scene to 5 to 9, choosing three to 16 to 18; the journeys hold the keyboard to twice the pointer's presses or say why not (`theKeyboardCostsAtMostTwiceThePointer`), and a long list's first box to its own skip (`theKeyboardSkipsIntoALongList`). A hosted page carries 0.0 KB more up front, 12 bytes, the bar's slot for the skips (the compiled page too); the pages face, every face and the embed with the studio are raised by what they measured.
  
  Compatibility: ops, stored formats, the wire, the declaration and derived tools are unchanged. The bar's first Tab stops are its skips, so a host that walks the bar by Tab from the top of the page reaches the app's mark one or two presses later; the pins in a menu of acts are reached by the arrow from their act, not by Tab.

## 0.1.39

### Patch Changes

- 99ed9e7: A record a lens draws as a link answers the right-click menu on a page mounted under a base, and a new tab keeps the base (FR-189). On a page mounted with `basePath` (`/a/<app>`), a board's card and a blocks lens's card or row were `<a href="/features/live-documents">`, the base dropped, so the menu read them as a link somewhere else: "Open in a new tab" and nothing more, no Open, no acts, no "Move to ›" — and with the Move button gone in 0.1.38, a phone could not move a card at all — while the new tab opened outside the app. Now every record a list draws as a link (a board's card, a blocks lens's cards and rows, grouped or not, a home's lists) links to its address under the router's base (`/a/<app>/features/live-documents`), the menu reads the record an item is listed as (`data-graview-listed`) wherever in it the press lands, its own stretched link included — a link the card draws to somewhere else stays that link — and "Open in a new tab" opens the record's address under the base. On a status board a finger resting on a card opens its menu, with Move to ›, rather than choosing it; on a phone that menu offers "Choose several", which chooses the card and begins choosing as the long press did, and while several are chosen a tap and a long press choose as before (a card is `data-graview-choose` only then). `capabilities().shipped` gains FR-189. The pages face alone grows 218 / 81 bytes (minified / gzipped), every face 679 / 250 and the embed with the studio handed in 218 / 97, each raised in `scripts/budget-raises/`; a hosted page's first load holds to its budget unraised.
  
  Compatibility: ops, stored formats, the wire, the declaration and derived tools are unchanged; `capabilities().shipped` lists FR-189. What Graview Cloud does: nothing — a page mounted with `basePath` draws its cards' links under the base and its menu answers them; a host that read a card's `href` as basename-relative now reads it with the base on.
- 3bfdf76: The page says when a place has drawn and settled (FR-188). A host picturing a place headless waited on a guess — its opening frame gone, the network quiet, then 600 ms — and a font, an image or a worker view that came later than that was pictured half drawn. Now once the place the reader is on has drawn and settled — its face here, every part drawn on it arrived or said it did not, the fonts and images loaded, each worker view drawn or stopped (what stands in its place drawn), and nothing moving for 200 ms — the embed's root wears `data-graview-drawn`, its value the place's path, and `mount`'s `onDrawn(place)` is told the place as `where()` says it (`EmbedWhere`: face, path, stop). A move to another page, another stop on the scene or another face takes the attribute off in the commit that moves, and the next place puts it back as it settles; a place that never stops moving is said drawn after ten seconds all the same. `DRAWN_ATTRIBUTE` names it on `@graview/embed`, and `graview shots` waits for it before each picture where it waited 0.7 s, or 1.8 s on the scene. A worker view's region says `data-worker-view-drawn` once it has drawn what it was last shown with no picture still being fetched for it; `partsArriving()` on `@graview/react` counts the parts drawn and still on their way, and `partArriving(load)` counts a loader of one's own. `onDrawn` was told the face once it was on the page (FR-57); that is `onFaceDrawn` on `<Embed>` now, and `handle.drawn()` still waits for it. A hosted page carries 2.3 KB more up front for it (the compiled page too), and their budgets are raised by that, and the pages face, the embed, every face and the open kit's host by what they measured. `capabilities().shipped` gains FR-188.
  
  Compatibility: ops, stored formats, the wire, the declaration and derived tools are unchanged; `capabilities().shipped` lists FR-188. `mount`'s `onDrawn` is handed the place (`EmbedWhere`) where it was handed the face, and is called once the place settles rather than when the face is first committed; a React host that wanted the old call passes `onFaceDrawn` to `<Embed>`. What Graview Cloud does: in `pictures.ts`, wait for `#graview-app [data-graview-drawn]` (or its value, the address pictured) in place of the opening's absence, network idle and the 600 ms settle.
- 099209c: The pages alone say when a page has drawn, and open on a draft view, as the whole embed does (FR-188, FR-187). `@graview/embed/pages` — a chat's widget, a phone's page — took neither `onDrawn` nor `draftView`, so a host picturing a proposal there guessed when it had drawn and could not draw the proposal at all. Now its `mount` takes `onDrawn(place)` and its root wears `data-graview-drawn` (the page's path) once the page has drawn and settled, off in the commit that moves; and it takes `draftView`, drawn where the manifest attaches and marked "Draft — <title> · Not kept", kept nowhere, the worker's host fetched before the pages draw and only when a draft is handed. Both come from the same code as the whole embed's: what watches the place is one hook (`drawn.ts`), and the draft's registration one module (`draft.ts`). `DRAWN_ATTRIBUTE` and the `EmbedDraftView` and `EmbedWhere` types are on `@graview/embed/pages` too. And the pages alone go where `handle.setPath` sends them when they were mounted without a `path`: the routed face took the first path a host handed after it opened for the one it began at, and stayed. The pages face alone measures 3.5 KB more (1.7 KB gzipped) for them, and its budget is raised by that; nothing of it is in a hosted page.
  
  Compatibility: ops, stored formats, the wire, the declaration and derived tools are unchanged. New on `@graview/embed/pages`: `onDrawn` and `draftView` on `mount` and `<PagesEmbed>`, `setDraftView` on its handle, `DRAWN_ATTRIBUTE`, and the types `EmbedDraftView` and `EmbedWhere`. `PagesApp` follows its `path` prop from any value, not only from the one it was first drawn with. What Graview Cloud does: a widget on `@graview/embed/pages` waits for `[data-graview-drawn]` as the hosted page does, and passes `draftView` there too.

## 0.1.38

### Patch Changes

- 28a63eb: A status board moves a card without a button under it, and the shipped lenses take search, filter and column options (Roadmap f-board-move-without-buttons, f-lens-search-filter). A Move… button under every card doubled a board's height, and the Recon app's Actions board could not be searched without a hand-drawn view that could not drag. A card is now dragged, carried by the keyboard — Space picks it up, the arrows carry it between and along the columns, Space drops it and Escape puts it back, each step said in a live region ("Public forms, picked up. Planned, 2 of 16.") — or moved from its own menu's "Move to ›", which lists every other column: the ones this seat may move it to, and the withheld ones struck through with the reason a press would meet (`columnChoices` on `@graview/core/describe`). The card stays one stop for Tab and says how it moves. `add-lens` and a declared or TypeScript `columns` lens take `options`: `search` (the fields Find looks in), `filters` (fields and relations standing as chips), `move` (the Move button back), `collapsed` and `hidden` (columns, by value), and `cap` (the newest cards a column keeps, with "Show N more"). A board of more than twenty cards shows Find without being asked; a smaller one shows it when `search` names its fields. A narrowed card moves as before. A calendar and a coverage take `search` and `filters` too. `graview check` says an option the kind cannot honor as `lens-option`, and `add-lens` refuses it. Up front a hosted page carries 0.7 KB more (722 bytes, 0.6 KB handed a compiled app), the options a declared lens is held to.
  
  Compatibility: a declaration gains the `columns` lens's `search`, `filters`, `move`, `collapsed`, `cap` and `hidden` options and the calendar's and coverage's `search` and `filters`, and `graview check` gains the warning `lens-option`; every declaration that compiled still compiles, and ops, stored formats, the wire and derived tools are unchanged.
- 91f5168: A view is hidden, shown, deleted and restored from the place menu. Views pile up as chats draw them, and the person annoyed by the clutter is standing at the places drop-down: now the place you are on in the bar, and each place in its drop-down, open the Pages menu — a right-click, Shift+F10 or the menu key, a finger resting on it — with Hide and Delete for a view. Hide takes it off the places, the scene's places and the home's gallery for everyone, still at its address; the drop-down's foot lists it under Hidden with Show. Delete takes it out of every face and lists it under Recently deleted with Restore, and the day it is gone for good, thirty days on; deleting the place you are on takes you to its kind's own place, else home, on Pages and the scene alike. A script view a chat drew is the host's: `mount`'s new `keptViews` (and the whole-page `Shell`'s, and `GraviewProvider`'s) (`KeptViews` from `@graview/core`: `current()`, `onChange`, `hide`, `show`, `remove`, `restore`, and `lens`) is where the page asks, and `current().may` says what this viewer may — the views a chat drew for an owner or an editor, the declaration's lenses for the owner. A declared lens is hidden with `set-lens-hidden` and deleted with `remove-lens`, handed to `lens` as a change to the declaration the host writes with its rollback. `registerWorkerView` registers a view under its manifest's `name` and `registerDeclaredLenses` marks a lens `declared`, which the menu reads; a view the app's own code registers is offered neither. A served store answers `/graview/views` (`createStoreHandler`'s `views` and `mayTidyViews`, `remoteViews` from `@graview/ship/browser` for the page), `graview serve` keeps what was tidied in `views.json` beside the store, `graview preview` writes a declared lens's change into the document it holds, and `devViews()` from `@graview/ship/dev` is the dev server's door. `memoryViews` and `placeTidying` (whether this viewer may tidy a place, and how) are on `@graview/core`, and `useTidiedViews` — the registry as the host's tidying leaves it — on `@graview/react`. A page carries only what hears the gesture and says the drop-down's foot has something to list: which place, whether a finger rested, what a press on the menu's rows does, the toast, the foot and the tidying are fetched on first use, and the registry's tidying only once the host lists something hidden or deleted. A hosted page carries 1.7 KB more up front for it (the compiled page too); their budgets are raised by that, and the pages face, the embed, every face and the guest host by what they measured.
  
  Compatibility: ops, stored records, the declaration and derived tools are unchanged. The wire: a new route, `/graview/views` (GET what is hidden and recently deleted and what the asking seat may do; POST `{ hide | show | remove | restore: name }` or `{ lens: set-lens-hidden | remove-lens }`), and `createStoreHandler` takes `views` and `mayTidyViews`; every other route answers as before. New: `KeptViews`, `KeptViewsState`, `KeptViewsMay`, `RemovedView`, `LensTidyEdit`, `memoryViews`, `placeTidying`, `NO_KEPT_VIEWS`, `REMOVED_VIEW_DAYS` and `removedLensName` on `@graview/core`; `ViewMeta`, `ViewRegistration` and `Place` take `name` and `declared`; `keptViews` on the embed, the pages embed, `Shell` and `GraviewProvider`, `GraviewContextValue.keptViews`, `KeptViewsProvider`, `useTidiedViews` and `PLACE_MENU_EVENT` on `@graview/react`; `ViewTidyingSections`, `useViewTidyingSection`, `TidyGroups`, `useTidied`, `placeAfter`, `useKeptViewsState`, `placeGesture`, `openPlaceMenu`, `placeAim` and `PLACE_MENU_EVENT` on `@graview/primitives`; `answerViews`, `keepsTheApp`, `remoteViews`, `fileViews` and `VIEWS_PATH` on `@graview/ship`, `devViews` on `@graview/ship/dev`. What Graview Cloud does: keep each app's hidden view names and its deleted views (with the definition and its versions, thirty days, then gone), answer `KeptViews` for the viewer with `may` from their share (owner and editor: views; owner: lenses), write a lens's edit as a change with its usual undo, keep registering a deleted view's definition while it is kept so Restore brings it back in place, and pass the object as `keptViews` to `mount`.
- 5bd37dc: The first load asks for nothing it does not draw again. The place menu's tidying had put a round trip and six requests in front of the first paint: the preview's page asked `/graview/views` only after the document came, and waited for the answer before it drew; the frame fetched what tidies the registry though nothing was hidden; and the routed face's first chunks carried what a press on Hide or Delete does, the drop-down's foot and the gesture's reading, while the bar's panes carried the hover chips' switch. Now `graview preview` hands the views with the document, as it hands the store's state, and `remoteViews` starts from them (`state`) without asking; `useTidiedViews` fetches `tidyViews` the first time the host lists something hidden or deleted; the menu's rows decide what they offer as the menu opens and fetch what a press does while the pointer rests on them; the foot is fetched when the drop-down opens with something to list, the gesture's reading with the bar's panes; and the person's menu fetches the hover chips' switch only where the host offers chips. On a slow phone the roadmap is useful in 3.5 s where it took 4.1 and interactive in 5.3 s where it took 5.9, on 75 requests where it took 80, as main measured it before the batch; the tasks are useful in 3.4 s where they took 4.1.
  
  `tidyViews` is no longer on `@graview/react`'s main entry, which put it in every chunk that imports that entry: a host hands `keptViews` and `useTidiedViews` does the rest. The rule for whether a viewer may tidy a place is `placeTidying` on `@graview/core` (it was `tidyingOf` on `@graview/primitives`, a name core's suggestions already use), and `PLACE_MENU_EVENT` is on `@graview/react/provider` beside the menu it opens, still re-exported by `@graview/primitives`; `useKeptViews` is gone from `@graview/primitives`. The routed face alone loads 5.4 KB (2.0 KB gzipped) less before it draws; up front, a hosted page carries 0.3 KB more for the frame following the host's views itself, and the place menu's raises are measured again.
  
  Compatibility: the preview's `/graview/document` carries `views`, the answer `/graview/views` gives its seat; `remoteViews` takes an optional `state`. Ops, stored records, the declaration and derived tools are unchanged.
