## 0.1.37

### Patch Changes

- cf691f4: A record can hold a video. A photo gallery built on Graview Cloud with three iPhone clips could show only a play glyph over a still: no field took a video, the host's files took none, and a view may not draw `<video>`. A document now says `{"type": "video"}` (or `{"type": "list", "of": "video"}`), a declaration `videoField()`, and the value is the `FileValue` the host's upload answered — `{ href, name, type, bytes, width?, height?, caption? }` — with what a player wants before it loads a byte: `poster` (a picture on the host), `duration` in seconds and `captions` (a WebVTT file on the host), each a path on the host or a length above 0, or refused. A video field takes only `video/*`, a picture field no video. The editor asks for videos where the host's `kinds` take `video/*` (`accept="video/*"`, "Choose a video", "This host doesn't take videos" where they do not) and sends one as it was picked: making a phone's HEVC `.mov` an MP4 every browser plays, its poster and its length are the host's to answer. The face plays a record's video — its poster first, the browser's own controls, `preload="metadata"`, captions where the value has them, sized from the value — drawn and fetched where the host serves files (`fileSrc`), its length read from what the player loaded where the value does not say it; a card or a row shows its poster (or its first frame) with a play glyph and its length. A record's facts, an image block and a headless run say it as "a 45-second video" (`videoWords`); the act's form and its argument's shape say `video: true`. A worker view whose manifest says `pictures` places `<graview-video data-record data-field>` and the host plays that record's video in it, from the records the view was shown, never handing the view a path or drawing it a `<video>`; and a viewer's press on a `data-lightbox` element opens the host's full-screen viewer for a picture or a video. The dev server's files door takes videos and serves them a range at a time. The `graview-view-drawing`, `graview-worker-view` and `graview-node-kind` skills say so. A hosted page carries 0.6 KB more up front for a video field's schema; the player, the card and the view's player are behind the doors already there, and the guest host's first load grows by under 1 KB for the press and the door.
  
  Compatibility: ops and stored records are unchanged; a stored file value is read as before, and a value may now also carry `poster`, `duration` and `captions`. The declaration takes the field type `video` and `of: "video"`; a declaration that compiled still compiles. Derived tool input schemas for a file argument list the optional `poster`, `duration` and `captions`, and a video field's form control and argument shape say `video: true`. The guest wire is unchanged in shape: a video on a record is handed to a view that asks for `pictures` as its facts (`GuestFile.duration` beside `width`, `height` and `caption`) where it was handed its name, type and size. The open kit draws one element more, `graview-video`. `DEV_FILES_KINDS` takes `video/*`.
- d689fbc: A record page of a project's own draws its facts as the derived one does. The page `graview create` writes drew every fact as text: a festival's trailer was its file name, and a date was changed only through "Change the film", where the derived record page plays a video, shows a picture where it is kept, links a phone and edits each value where it stands. `RecordField` from `@graview/pages` is one fact as the derived page draws it, the derived page draws with it, and the scaffold's record page does too.
  
  Compatibility: no surface changes; `@graview/pages` exports `RecordField` and `RecordFieldProps`.
- 7d6f521: Datetimes are said in the app's time zone, and a template can say a time of day. An app declares its zone once, `brand.timeZone` (an IANA name, "America/New_York") on `defineApp`'s brand or a document's `brand`, and every instant kept with a zone (`2026-10-16T14:47:00Z`, `…-04:00`) is said on that clock: in lists, cards, record pages, a rule's sentence, an act's sentence and its echo to an agent, Activity's digest, the static page and the date editor, which reads and keeps an instant on the same clock. A person's zone wins over it where a host passes one — `timeZone` on `GraviewProvider`, the embed and the pages face, beside `locale` — and each reader's own clock is said when neither says, as before. A moment kept without a zone is still the wall time written. Describe templates take `{at | time}` ("10:47 AM") and `{at | datetime}` ("Oct 16, 2026, 10:47 AM EDT"), and a field block takes `as: "time"` and `as: "datetime"`; `{at | date}` and `relative` count an instant's day on the app's clock. An agent's act given `2026-10-16T10:47:00-04:00` echoed "2:47 PM", the server's UTC; a tool runtime handed the app now says it in the app's zone, and a host answering away from any page says its own words in it with `sayingIn({ appTimeZone }, run)`. A view is handed the zone rather than formatted strings: every props push carries `locale` and `timeZone`, for `Intl.DateTimeFormat(props.locale, { timeZone: props.timeZone, … })`, so no view hard-codes a zone again. `graview check` and the document's checker refuse a zone the runtime does not know, or an offset, by name (`brand-time-zone`); `set-brand` sets one (`timeZone`, null clears it), and a diff says "Times are said in America/New_York." `describeApp` says the zone. The `graview-brand`, `graview-node-kind` and `graview-worker-view` skills say so. A hosted page carries 2.0 KB more up front for it (the compiled page too), and the guest host about 0.5 KB; their budgets are raised by that.
  
  Compatibility: ops and stored records are unchanged; the declaration takes an optional `brand.timeZone`, and a declaration without it says every time as before. The check finding `brand-time-zone` is new. The guest wire's props carry `locale` and `timeZone`. `Money` (a block's and a template's `money`) takes `timeZone`; `readableFields` takes `timeZone`; `momentAsRead`, `timeAsRead` and `whenAsRead` take a zone after the locale. `sayIn` takes `readerTimeZone` and `appTimeZone` beside `reader` and `app`. A derived edit's sentence and Activity's digest say an instant's day and time on the page's zone, where they said them on the runtime's clock.
- f9e81c1: Two ways to add one beside a list's heading wrap apart, never one over the other. Each press is a 28-pixel target that takes 4 pixels back from the line above and below it, so on a phone, where the presses wrap, the second row stood 8 pixels over the first: Cloud's vendor list at 390 wide had "+Add a vendor to this category" over "+Add a vendor", and a press on one landed on the other. The rows now stand 8 pixels apart.
  
  Compatibility: no surface changes.

## 0.1.34

### Patch Changes

- f48b7d1: A panel drawn from the right-click menu opens in the face it was asked on (FR-184). On Pages, "Draw a panel" did nothing: the menu at the pointer was drawn outside the routed face's way of going (`GoToContext`, which Pages gives by address), so the inline AI went the scene's way, set the seat's draft with nowhere on the page to show it, and the draft waited for the scene. The routed face now gives its way of going to everything it draws, the menu and the quick ask's card included, so "Draw a panel", a panel the menu suggests and a panel or a place taken from the quick ask's card land at `/~draft` with "Keep as a lens" and "Discard", or at the place's page, and never switch face. Over the scene a drawn panel is framed on the scene's surface — the panel's color, an edge, rounded corners, a little in from the picture's edges — instead of a bare list on the ground, and the panel beside the map (a district's records, a record's links) is put away while it stands and comes back when it is kept or discarded. An ask the menu makes stays in the menu until what it asked for lands: "Asking…" while it is on its way, then the menu is put away as the ghost, the panel or the page arrives; an ask that fails is said in the menu, where the person who asked is looking, as well as in Activity, and the quick ask's card says a failed offer on the card. `capabilities().shipped` gains FR-184.
  
  Compatibility: ops, stored formats, the wire, the declaration and derived tools are unchanged; `capabilities().shipped` lists FR-184. What Graview Cloud does: nothing — a host that mounts the pages face gets the panel at `/~draft` on the next version.
- 8f09e52: A reader who may do nothing is told how to get in, not shown every act struck through (FR-183). A signed-out visitor on a public app right-clicked an era and the menu said "Nothing you may do with an era — 7 actions withheld" over seven disabled buttons, each with the policy's sentence; the list's foot said "~~Add an era~~ — Not permitted: …"; nothing said how to get the right, and "Notify me…" was offered to `anonymous` and kept a subscription for nobody. A host now says what a signed-out reader could gain: `signIn: { label, run, roles? }`, handed only while the reader is a visitor, on `mount` from `@graview/embed` and `@graview/embed/pages`, on `GraviewProvider` (which the Shell draws under, and a provider inside another takes from the one around it) and on the pages' context. Where every act withheld from the reader is one a member could take — one some role could, or one of `roles`, the roles signing in gives, when the host names them — the context menu and the Inspector on either face, the list's foot and a record's acts say `label` as one press that calls `run` (`data-testid="sign-in"`), in place of the struck acts; the face's own rows (Open, Show in the scene) stay. With `signIn` given, "Notify me" is not offered on the menu, a record's page or after an act, and the seat's chat keeps no subscription. A member lacking one role is still told each act they may not take and who could (FR-16), and so is a visitor where one act is one no role can take. `signInInstead(withheld, signIn)` and the `SignIn` type are exported from `@graview/core`, the same judgment for a page of an app's own. A hosted page, compiled or not, carries 0.2 KB more up front for it, under a raise of 0.3 KB more up front on each of the two budgets. The embed's README and the graview-embed skill say the option. `capabilities().shipped` gains FR-183.
  
  Compatibility: ops, stored formats, the declaration and derived tools are unchanged. The wire is unchanged; `capabilities().shipped` lists FR-183. What Graview Cloud does: pass `signIn: { label: "Sign in to make changes", run }` to `mount` while nobody is signed in, and drop its interim `visitorMenuSections` row and the CSS hiding `[data-testid=withheld]`, `[data-testid=no-affordances]` and `[data-menu-section=notify]`.
- 55ab33c: The right-click menu is ready before the click (FR-186). The menu derived its acts and sections as it opened, fetched its AI section behind Suspense, and asked for suggestions afresh every time, so it filled in while the reader watched: "Thinking…", then rows arriving. Now when the pointer rests on a record, a card, a row or a kind for 150 ms — in the scene and on the routed face; a pass-over across a list does nothing — or the keyboard lands on one (so Shift+F10 opens at once), or a finger goes down on one (so a long press does), the menu that would open there is got ready: the pane and its act forms fetched, its acts derived and kept, and each section's new `prepare` called, which fetches the inline AI's chunk and the sheet "Notify me…" opens. Nothing is fetched before that, so a hosted page's first load is unchanged. The acts derived for a target are kept per store, by the selection, the seat, the providers, the pins and the focus, until the graph changes (`prepareAffordances` and `AffordancesAsked` from `@graview/react`; `useAffordances` reads the same kept derivation). Suggestions are kept per target, by the seat, the declaration and the host's `ai`, until the record changes (for a list, a lens or a place, until the graph does): a menu opened again draws what was suggested in its first frame and asks nothing, and a failure is not kept. Hovering asks no model, ever. A host's own menu section may give `prepare` too (`MenuSection.prepare`); `useMenuPrepare()` from `@graview/react/drawing` calls every section's for a target, and the routed face's `Inspector` takes `aimAt`, what a menu opened at an element would be about. `capabilities().shipped` gains FR-186.
  
  Compatibility: ops, stored formats, the wire, the declaration and derived tools are unchanged; `capabilities().shipped` lists FR-186. What Graview Cloud does: nothing for its menus to be ready before the click; a section it adds through `menuSections` that fetches what it draws can say so in `prepare`.

## 0.1.32

### Patch Changes

- 0a822b7: A kind's list offers adding one beside its heading, not at its end. Since 0.1.30 following a place puts the keyboard on the new page's heading, so a screen reader says where it arrived; "Add a task" stood after the last row, and from the heading the keyboard went back through the whole bar to reach it, so making a record on the pages from the keyboard cost seven to nine presses more (a task 21 then 28, a talk 21 then 30, a song 17 then 26). Each creating act's press now stands beside the list's title, the next stop after the heading, and its form opens under the list's head, above the rows: making a task takes 13 presses, a talk 14, a song 10. A closed form takes no room, so the first record stands where it stood; the form still stands open where there is nothing yet, Escape still puts it away and gives the keyboard back to its press, and an empty list says the first one "starts above".

## 0.1.31

### Patch Changes

- 4784ba8: A date and a date and time are given as people give them (Roadmap f-date-inputs). Every place a person gives one — a record's value changed in place on its page or its card, an act's form on the pages, the scene's ask — draws one editor (`WhenInput`, on `@graview/primitives` and `/pages`): a text box that reads "tomorrow 3pm", "Fri 10:30", "17 Oct", "in 2 weeks", "next Mon" or "10/17" (in the reader's order of digits), says back in words the day it read and the zone it reads it in, and refuses words that name no day rather than guessing, so the form it is in will not send them. Beside it a button (or Alt with the down arrow) opens a month to pick from, a grid the keyboard walks — arrows, Page Up and Page Down, Home and End, Enter, Escape back to the button — with each day named in full; it sits under the box, so on a phone it never runs off the screen. The editor and its month are fetched when first asked for, and a date that can be changed fetches its editor while the page is idle. A value opened to change is chosen whole, so what is typed replaces it.
  
  A document's `datetime` was asked for as a bare text box that wanted `2026-10-17T15:00:00Z`; `describeArg` now reads it, and `z.iso.datetime()`, as a date with a time that names an instant (`{ type: "date", time: true, instant: true }`, and `instant` on a form's date field). Such a moment is typed on the reader's clock and kept in UTC with its `Z`; a field whose own pattern takes a bare `YYYY-MM-DDTHH:MM` keeps the wall time as written. An instant is said on the reader's clock with its zone ("Oct 17, 2026, 3:00 PM EDT", `momentAsRead`), and a calendar places it on the reader's day and hour rather than UTC's.
  
  An end moves with its start: where a kind (or an act) has a start and an end — its `start` and `end` roles, else fields named for them (`starts`/`ends`, `from`/`until`, …) — a start moved in place carries the end as far, in one change with one undo; in a form or the scene's ask, a first start puts an empty end an hour after it, and a start moved carries the end it had. `@graview/core/words` gains `readWhenTyped`, `storedOfWall`, `wallOfStored`, `wallNow`, `namesInstant`, `zoneSaid`, `endAfterStartMoved` and `startAndEndOf`. `pnpm verify date-inputs` holds it on both faces and a 390-pixel phone.
  
  Nothing up front changes on a hosted page but 42 bytes, and with the rest of 0.1.31 merged the page is 0.1 KB more up front (raised in `scripts/budget-raises/dates-and-the-quick-ask-together-*.json`); the scene face before it draws is 7.2 KB larger (954,856 bytes), and the embed's budgets are raised in `scripts/budget-raises/`.
  
  Compatibility: derived tool names and input schemas — no tool name or input schema changes; a form's date field (`ScalarField`) gains an optional `instant`, and a document's `datetime` argument describes itself as a date with a time instead of as text, so a form and the scene ask for it with the date editor.
- ed1a54c: Nothing stands over a control the watch found covered on its first run (W-224). From altitude a district's showings hang from the foot of its name as drawn, measured rather than guessed, so they no longer cover its problem count. The sign to a person somewhere else stands down the right edge below Up and the key, clear of a panel docked over the map. On a phone the app's name gives its room before the faces switch stands over it. The city keeps the zoom's corner clear as it keeps Up's, and where a sheet stands over the ask field the foot is the taller of the two rather than both. The timeline over the scene says its height as a sheet does, so the city is fitted above it. A sign at the top edge steps past the picture's trail ("Reset view"). A focused card ends above the row of districts under it at every text size, so "+3 more" is pressable. A row or card stretched over by its record's link stops that link where a list of its own records begins, so a package's offers and the offers answering what was heard no longer stand over it. And a view the seat drew over the scene puts the picture under it away (inert) while it stands, so Up, the key and the zoom are not pressed through it.
  
  It adds 0.2 KB more up front to a hosted page (178 bytes, 63 gzipped), and its raises are filed in `scripts/budget-raises/`.
  
  Compatibility: no surface changes; `useEndsAboveItsList` is new on `@graview/primitives` and its `/pages` entry.
- ff88015: A person can ask to be told when something happens in an app, and the host is told when it does (Roadmap f-notify-subscriptions). A subscription is `{ who, app, when }`, where `when` is an act running (any, one by name, and/or one on a record), a record of a kind added, a kind's records or one record changed, or a record reaching a condition written in the app's own rule language (`done == true`, `amount > 100`); it is told only of changes somebody else made unless it says `alsoMine`. `matchSubscriptions(store, ops, subscriptions)` on the new `@graview/core/subscriptions` judges a commit batch by batch as each subscriber's seat sees it — a change to a record they cannot see never matches, a field kept from them is neither said nor read by a condition, a change withheld from them is said as "Someone" — and answers at most one `SubscriptionMatch` per batch per subscription, with who made it, its sentence, the acts it ran and each record it touched with the fields it changed. `watchSubscriptions(store, { subscriptions, onSubscriptionMatch })` calls a host's hook on every commit; `subscriptionWords` says a subscription in words and `subscriptionProblem` says why one could never be told anything. The shapes and `memorySubscriptions()` are on the main entry.
  
  `@graview/ship`'s handler keeps them (`subscriptions`, a `SubscriptionStore` of the host's; in memory when unsaid), serves each seat its own at `/graview/subscriptions`, and calls `onSubscriptionMatch` once per matching batch; `remoteSubscriptions` is the page's half, `fileSubscriptions` the file `graview serve` keeps beside the store and prints each match from. A chat subscribes its person with three new tools, `subscribe`, `unsubscribe` and `list_subscriptions`, listed where the host keeps subscriptions (`ToolRuntimeOptions.subscriptions`, `createMcpHttpHandler`'s, and always under `graview mcp`, which shares the file with `graview serve`).
  
  On both faces the context menu offers "Notify me…" on a record, a kind's list and an act (a menu target now says the `act` it was opened on), a record's page offers "Notify me when this changes", and an act's form when it is done offers "Notify me when someone else does this": each opens one sheet already filled in, so from a right-click a person subscribes in two presses. What a person is told of is listed in Activity, each with one press to stop. The provider keeps them where its host says (`subscriptions`), else in the page.
  
  It adds 0.8 KB more up front to a hosted page (864 bytes, 330 gzipped), and its raises are filed in `scripts/budget-raises/`.
  
  Compatibility: the wire: a new route, `/graview/subscriptions` (GET the seat's own; POST `{ add: { when, alsoMine? } }` or `{ remove: id }`), and `createStoreHandler` takes `subscriptions` and `onSubscriptionMatch`; every other route answers as before. Derived tool names and input schemas: unchanged for a runtime given no `subscriptions`; given one, `subscribe`, `unsubscribe` and `list_subscriptions` are listed beside the read tools, and an act named any of them is listed as `<name>_2`. New: `@graview/core/subscriptions`, `Subscription`, `SubscriptionWhen`, `SubscriptionMatch`, `SubscriptionStore`, `OnSubscriptionMatch`, `memorySubscriptions` and `subscriberOf` on `@graview/core`, `MenuTarget.act`, `PointerMenu.act`, `GraviewContextValue.subscriptions`, and `NotifySections`, `NotifyDoor`, `NotifyButton`, `NotifiedDoor`, `useNotifySection`, `useNotifiedCount` and `askToNotify` on `@graview/primitives`.
- 6c92669: Pages honors the app's setting for the AI it offers in place. An embed mounted with `seat: "hidden"` drew no ask field on the scene and one on every page anyway: the routed face drew its own without reading the setting. In this release the ask field itself gives way to the quick ask (see "The quick ask replaces the Ask box"), and its setting, `quickAsk`, is read by both faces alike: the embed hands it to the scene and to every page, and `handle.setQuickAsk(on)` changes it on whichever face is open, on a phone and a desk, without mounting again.
  
  Compatibility: no surface changes beyond those the quick ask's changeset names.
- f7269b7: The first load and a page switch wait on less, and are timed (Roadmap f-perf-first-load, from "too slow to load initially and between pages"). `pnpm verify first-load` serves Graview Cloud's shell as `graview preview` builds it over a roadmap the Graview Roadmap's size and a small team's tasks, at a desk and on a slow phone, and CI holds its medians to a budget; docs/first-load.md has the numbers before and after. On the phone the roadmap is interactive in 5.2 s where it took 7.6, the tasks in 3.4 s where they took 5.1; at a desk the scene draws 37 ms after a press where it took 343.
  
  A press for the scene fetches it first and keeps the page drawn meanwhile, for at most 150 ms, rather than suspending into a placeholder that React then holds for 300 ms; at a desk the other face is fetched once the page is idle, and the bar's switch fetches a face as a hand or the keyboard reaches for it (`BarFace.reach`). A picture on the home is drawn as it comes within half a screen of the window, so a phone no longer fetches and lays out every lens before it answers a press. `openRemote` takes the store's first `state` when the host already sent it, and opens on it without asking; `graview preview` hands it with the document, and its page opens without waiting for the socket's welcome. `graview serve` and `graview preview` send their answers gzipped to a browser that takes them, and the preview's page names the chunks it needs in its head and asks for every chunk a door needs as the door is opened, rather than a level at a time.
  
  It adds 1.0 KB more up front to a hosted page (1,042 bytes, 405 gzipped), and its raises are filed in `scripts/budget-raises/`.
  
  Compatibility: the wire: a served answer is gzipped when the request accepts gzip (`content-encoding: gzip`, `vary: Accept-Encoding`), and the preview's `/graview/document` carries `state`, the answer `/graview/state` gives its seat; `openRemote` takes an optional `state`, and `RemoteState` is new on `@graview/ship`, `/browser` and `/runtime`. `BarFace` takes an optional `reach`.
- 294f181: The inline AI picks from what the app already knows, previews before it keeps, and leaves an entry in Activity (Roadmap f-ai-candidate-catalog, f-activity-ai-entries). A host that hands the seat a model now gets an AI section in the context menu on both faces: a few suggestions the model picked for what was right-clicked, "Fill in with AI" and "Draw a panel". The model never invents: `catalogOf(store, target, { principal })` on the new `@graview/core/catalog` derives, for a record, a selection, a field, a list or a lens, an act or a place, the candidates with an id and a line each — the acts the viewer may run there, the panels its kind can be drawn as, the places and records next to it, the values its fields may take — and `aiContextOf` the compact state a model reads beside them (where the viewer is, the target, the selection, the records one link away, what was done lately, a digest of the declaration cached per declaration), both under the viewer's sight: nothing they may not see or run is ever offered or said.
  
  `HostAi` gains two provider slots: `fast`, a typed-decision model in Jev's shape (`{ model, decide }`; `jevFast()` fills it with Jev), and `thinking`, a language model (`{ model, think({ system, prompt, state, catalog }) }`); unsaid, `decide` and `complete` serve them. Every call reports its tier, model, tokens and cost to the interaction and to `ai.meter`. `@graview/tools/inline-ai` holds the engine (`suggestHere`, `fillHere`, `panelHere`), the ghost (`ghostOf`, `acceptGhost`) and stub providers (`stubFast`, `stubThinking`) for tests and a dev app; the todo example takes them at `?ai=stub`. A proposed change waits as a ghost with Accept, Accept some and Dismiss, is kept as one batch whose ops carry lineage `interaction` and come through `ai:<interaction>`, and is taken back with one undo; a panel is drawn where the seat draws a view, and "Keep as a lens" makes it a real one.
  
  Each interaction is one entry in Activity (`AiInteraction`: who, when, the target, what was asked, offered and taken or dismissed, the batches it made with their undo, the models and their cost), and the change it made is drawn in it rather than again beside it. Activity filters All, Changes or AI and "Just mine"; a record's page lists what AI did there (`RecordInteractionsDoor`); an entry can ask again or show its panel again. A host keeps them with `ai.interactions` (`{ load, keep }`). `ai: { off: true }` hides every AI part on both faces — the menu's section, the ghost, the ask field, the entries and the filter — rather than disabling it.
  
  It adds 1.3 KB more up front to a hosted page (1,305 bytes, 460 gzipped), and its raises are filed in `scripts/budget-raises/`.
  
  Compatibility: ops and primitives: an op's `lineage` may carry `interaction`, the id of the AI interaction it came from; a reader that does not know it reads the lineage as before. New: `@graview/core/catalog`, `@graview/tools/inline-ai`, `HostAi.off`, `.fast`, `.thinking`, `.interactions` and `.meter`, `GraviewContextValue.aiDesk`, `GoTo.draft`, and `GhostDoor`, `InlineAiSections`, `useInlineAiSection` and `RecordInteractionsDoor` on `@graview/primitives`.
- 9a5e067: The quick ask replaces the Ask box (Roadmap f-quick-ask-framework). No face draws a field at its foot that grows into a chat thread any more: `SeatField`, its panel and Pages' `PageAsk` are gone, and so are their settings, the Shell's `ask` and `chat`, a routed face's `context.ask`, the embed's `seat` option and `handle.setAskField`. In their place, where the host's `ai` gives a model and does not say `off`, ⌘K (Ctrl+K) opens a command bar beside what the reader is on — the selected card on the scene, the record or list a page is about — that completes the acts on it, "Show …" for a panel the catalog can draw of it, its places and its records. Words that complete nothing are asked, and the answer is a card beside the record or the list: a few sentences, the places, records and panels that act on it, and the changes it proposes as a ghost on the card, accepted all or some as one batch with its one undo. A follow-up is asked on the card and replaces its answer; it is not a thread. The context menu's "Ask about this…", Find's last row and the search page's "Ask: ‘…’" open the same card, Activity keeps each ask as an AI interaction (`ask.kind` `"ask"`, with its `answer`) and "Ask again" asks it on the card again. Where the quick ask is drawn ⌘K is its and `/` stays Find's — Pages' Find hears `/` too, and the bar says `/` over its box; where it is not, both are Find's.
  
  `quickAsk` (a boolean, on by default) on `Shell`, a routed face's context and `mount`/`<Embed>`, and `handle.setQuickAsk(on)`, say whether it is drawn; `quickAskOf(setting)` reads a setting a host kept from the Ask box — `"field"` is on, `"hidden"` is off — and the embed reads a kept string the same way. The answer is the host's: `HostAi.answer` (`{ model, answer({ said, target, targetLabel, state, catalog, follows }) → { text, pick?, usage? } }`) answers it, else the Thinking slot, else the Fast slot's pick among the candidates; `answerHere` on `@graview/tools/inline-ai` is the engine, and `stubThinking` answers it for a test. A provider drawn inside another now takes the outer one's `ai` unless given its own, so an embed's routed face draws the inline AI and the quick ask too.
  
  The conversation stays the app's (`seatTalk`, the responders, `ai.turn`): no face draws it, and `ChatPanel` is there for a product that places one in its own layout. The strip the scene kept clear at its foot for the field stays, always, as the foot's (where the zoom, the way back, a notice and a proposal stand), so the city keeps its fit; `useSeatDrawn` and the conversation's `open`, `side`, `drawn`, `setOpen` and `setSide` went with the field. The four harnesses that existed for the box (`chat`, `phone-seat`, `seat-guide`, `seat-goes-and-draws`) are retired, and `quick-ask` holds the new surface on both faces in three engines.
  
  It adds 0.1 KB more up front to a hosted page (100 bytes, 60 gzipped, about the 19 its first load grew by and a margin), and its raises are filed in `scripts/budget-raises/`.
  
  Compatibility: no surface changes. Removed: `SeatField`, `SeatFieldProps`, `SeatStart`, `SEAT_WIDTH`, `SEAT_PHONE_BELOW`, `PageAsk`, `useSeatDrawn`, `SeatSide`, the Shell's `ask` and `chat`, `PageContext.ask`, `EmbedOptions.seat` and `EmbedHandle.setAskField`. New: `QuickAskDoor`, `openQuickAsk`, `useQuickAskDrawn`, `quickAskOf`, `QuickAskOpening` on `@graview/primitives`, `PageQuickAsk` on `@graview/pages`, `quickAsk` on `ShellProps`, `PageContext` and `EmbedOptions`, `EmbedHandle.setQuickAsk`, `HostAi.answer` with `AnswerProvider`, `AnswerRequest` and `AnswerReply`, `answerHere`, `answerOf` and `QuickAsk` on `@graview/tools/inline-ai`, `AiInteraction.answer`, and `HeldGhost.onCard`.

## 0.1.30

### Patch Changes

- e46f304: A broken rule leads with what it says. On a record's page, a rule's box read its expression ("number of blocks with phase = now 2 ≠ 0") under the rule's title, and the sentence its author wrote in `says` was only a tooltip. The sentence leads now, the rule's title under it, and the expression it was judged by is a detail one press away ("How it is judged"). A rule that wrote no sentence still says its shape, as before; the problems page draws the same box.
  
  Compatibility: no surface changes.
- 6cbecc0: A card says its kind's glance and no more, the bar names a place once, and a drawn home stays Home beside `pages.first` (the same review app). A finding card read "Critical · Security · High" — the third its confidence, though a severity can be high too — and still did after `confidence` was taken out of the kind's `glance`: the glance chose the order and the rest of the kind followed it. A glance a kind declares is now said exactly, and a choice another field shown beside it shares says its field ("Confidence: high", "Likelihood: low"), on a card, a row, a describe line and a table alike. A default card no longer draws a long or Markdown text as a chip cut after a couple of dozen characters: it says the text's start on a line of its own under the chips. With a home declared and `pages.first: "This week"`, the app opened on This week, and a press on Home from the scene drew the pages at their first entry and went to This week again, so the home the author drew was nowhere: `pages.first` now picks where a new visit lands, once, and after that `/` is the home on both faces. `graview check` says so when both are set (`pages-first-beside-home`, a note). And a kind whose plural is "root causes" beside a blocks place titled "Root causes" put two "Root causes" on the bar: the place keeps the name and stands, as `pages.first` and `pages.primary` already read the word, and the kind's list folds into More as "All root causes", still at `/root-causes`; `graview check` warns of it (`pages-place-named-as-a-list`). The graview-pages and graview-lists skills teach each. With the template fixes beside them, a hosted page carries 1.2 KB more up front (and 0.6 KB more up front handed a compiled app).
  
  Compatibility: ops, stored records and wire messages are unchanged. New check finding codes `pages-first-beside-home` (a note) and `pages-place-named-as-a-list` (a warning). `readableFields` with `glance: true` says only a kind's declared `display.glance` when it has one, and a choice's `alone` may carry its field's label. `barPlaces` labels a kind's list "All <plural>" and folds it when a place has its name (`allLabel` from `@graview/core`). `@graview/react/provider` exports `hasLanded(views)` and `markLanded(views)`, which the pages and the embed read `pages.first` once by. A default card's prose is `p[data-graview-excerpt="<field>"]`.
- dc5a737: A new app can point a person at its own controls once. A host that wanted to introduce the switch between the scene and the pages, the problems count, Activity and the Ask box could only do it by their selectors, which are not a surface it may hold the framework to, so its tour broke the first time the bar was drawn differently. `firstLook` on `mount` and `<Embed>`, the whole-page `Shell` and `PagesApp` runs a short sequence of hints the framework anchors to its own controls: one at a time, beside the control each names and never over it, a non-modal dialog the keyboard moves into and is said as it opens, with Next, Done on the last, Skip and Escape; a press anywhere else ends it, and the keyboard goes back where it was. A hint whose control is not drawn — no Ask box, nothing yet in Activity, the switch gone from a phone's bar while Find is open — is skipped, and it works on either face of an embed, kept across a change of face. It is shown once per viewer: the host says whether this viewer has seen it and is told when it is over; with no host seam, the browser remembers it in `localStorage` for the app. A host adds hints of its own after the framework's by naming one of the framework's controls. The hints are fetched only when a first look is shown: a hosted page's first load is about 1.1 KB more (0.4 KB gzipped), inside its budget, and every face 7.6 KB more minified.
  
  Compatibility: additive. New: `firstLook?: FirstLook` on `FrameOptions` (so `mount`, `<Embed>` and `@graview/embed/pages`' `mount`), `ShellProps` and `PagesAppProps`, where `FirstLook = boolean | { seen?: boolean | (() => boolean); onDone?: (how: "finished" | "dismissed") => void; steps?: readonly { anchor: FirstLookAnchor; title: string; words: string }[] }` and `FirstLookAnchor` is one of `FIRST_LOOK_ANCHORS`: `"faces"`, `"problems"`, `"activity"`, `"ask"`, `"find"`, `"profile"`, `"home"`. `FIRST_LOOK_ANCHORS`, `FirstLookDoor`, `firstLookKey` and the types `FirstLook`, `FirstLookAnchor`, `FirstLookEnd`, `FirstLookOptions`, `FirstLookStep` and `FirstLookDoorProps` from `@graview/primitives`, `/frame` and `/pages`; `FIRST_LOOK_ANCHORS` and those types from `@graview/embed`. Off unless given: an app that passes nothing draws and loads what it did. Ops, stored formats, the wire, the document format, the compiled format, check finding codes and derived tool names and schemas are unchanged. What Graview Cloud does: pass `firstLook: { seen: () => <the viewer has seen it>, onDone: () => <record that they have> }` to `mount` for a new app, and name an anchor rather than a selector in any hint of its own; `true` remembers it in the browser instead.
- 37f2b3b: A kind's list opens in the order and groups its declaration says (Nick, prioritizing the Graview Roadmap in a Graview app). Only a blocks lens could sort and group; a kind's own list, `/requests`, could not be told to, so done and declined requests read among the new ones. `pages.lists.<kind>` now takes, beside its `filters`, a `sort` — a field, a computed field (a rank the kind works out) or `label`, lowest first unless `"desc"`, with a `then` for ties — and a `group`: a choice or yes-or-no field, an `order` its groups come in, and the `closed` ones, drawn last and shut. A closed group is a disclosure: its heading is a button saying "done · 12 requests", expanded or not, the rows it controls drawn once it opens; it stands open when the list is narrowed by words or by the very field it groups, and `?open=done` keeps it open, so an opened group is a link too. A person still sorts or groups the list another way from its arranging line, and the address says so; a declared order keeps its tiebreak whenever the list is in it. `graview check` refuses a list sorted by what the kind does not have, grouped by a field that is not a choice or a yes or no, or ordering or closing a value that is not one of its choices, each in a sentence at its path (`pages-list-arrangement`, an error). `arrange-pages` sets them and says what it did (the list of requests opens grouped by "status", "done" and "declined" closed at the end), as `diffDocuments` does; a field renamed is followed, and a field removed, a computed field no longer worked out or a choice dropped lets go of what named it, said. `describeApp` says how each declared list opens. The graview-lists skill teaches it.
  
  Compatibility: ops, stored records and wire messages are unchanged. The document format takes `pages.lists.<kind>.sort` (`"<key>"` or `{ "by", "direction"?: "asc" | "desc", "then"?: "<key>" | { "by", "direction"? } }`) and `pages.lists.<kind>.group` (`"<field>"` or `{ "by", "order"?: [...], "closed"?: [...] }`), optional within `graview-document@1`; a build that predates them refuses a document that says them. `arrange-pages` takes `sort: { "<kind>": … | null }` and `group: { "<kind>": … | null }`, and setting a kind's `filters` no longer drops what else its list says. New check finding code `pages-list-arrangement` (an error). `DocumentDiff`'s sentences may say "The feature list opens sorted by "rank", highest first, then "name", A to Z." `PagesArrangement.lists` is `Record<string, KindList>` (`KindList`, `KindListSort`, `KindListGroup` from `@graview/core`); `Sort` may carry `then`, and `ArrangeContext` takes `read` for a computed field's value. `@graview/core/arrange` exports `listOpening`, `listSort`, `listGroup`, `orderListGroups`, `kindListFindings`, `listFieldsOf` and `withWorkedOut`. A kind's list page reads `?open=<value>,…`; a closed group's section is `[data-testid="list-group"][data-closed="shut" | "open"]` and its button `[data-testid="list-group-toggle"]`.
- 5b8ac1f: A lens can be kept off the lists (Roadmap f-hidden-lenses). A kind could be left off the home, but a lens could not be left off anything, so an app whose custom home links to lenses as targets ended with a long page list of them. A lens marked `hidden: true` is still a place at its address — a link, a view's `navigate` and `/places/<as>` reach it, and `placesOf` lists it with `hidden: true` — but the bar's places, the scene's tabs (unless it is the one shown), the page list and the home's gallery do not offer it, and it never becomes what its kind draws when an address names no picture. In a document `set-lens-hidden` sets it — `{ "op": "set-lens-hidden", "title": "Building now", "hidden": true }`, with `on` where two lenses share a title — and says so ("The lens "Building now" is kept off the bar and the page list; a link or navigate still reaches it.", and "… is listed on the bar and the page list again."), as `diffDocuments` does; a lens drawn anew in a hidden one's place (`add-lens` with `replace`) stays hidden, and a lens `pages.primary` stands on the bar is refused, in a sentence, until it is taken out of `primary`. `graview check` says a `hidden` that is not true or false (`lens-hidden-not-a-boolean`, a warning), a hidden lens with no title to be a place (`lens-hidden-untitled`, a note), and `pages.primary` naming a hidden lens (`pages-primary-hidden`, a warning). In TypeScript a lens says `hidden: true` beside its `title`, and a registration `views.register(kind, cell, View, { title, hidden: true })`. The studio reads a hidden lens as hidden and keeps one off the lists, or lists it again, with its own act, said to the document as `set-lens-hidden`. The graview-pages and graview-studio skills teach it.
  
  Compatibility: ops, stored records and the wire are unchanged. The document format takes `lenses[].hidden` (a boolean, optional within `graview-document@1`); a build that predates it draws the lens listed. New edit op `set-lens-hidden` (`title`, `on?`, `hidden`); `EDIT_OPS` lists it. New check finding codes `lens-hidden-not-a-boolean` (a warning), `lens-hidden-untitled` (a note) and `pages-primary-hidden` (a warning). `DocumentDiff`'s sentences may say "The lens "…" is kept off the bar and the page list; a link or navigate still reaches it." `LensDeclaration.hidden`, `DrawnLens.hidden`, `Place.hidden`, `ViewMeta.hidden`, `ViewRegistration.hidden` and `AppPlace.hidden` (now a lens's too) are `boolean | undefined`. The studio's meta-schema gives `lens` a `hidden` field and the act `set-lens-hidden` (`id`, `hidden`).
- e46f304: A place may leave off the forms that begin its kind. Every place's page ended with the create form for the kind it stands on, which on a page that is read, not written (a summary, a report) is a form nobody came for. A lens says `"create": false` and its page offers none; the kind's list and the bar still do. A per-place key rather than a form folded behind a button, because the author knows which places are read: a button would still put an act on every summary, and a place that is written keeps its form open as before. The document's shape types it (`lenses[].create`, a boolean, in `document.schema.json`), `graview check` warns at `lenses.<i>.create` when it is not one (`lens-create`), `add-lens` takes it ("its page offers no form to add one"), and `DrawnLens.create` carries it to whoever draws the place.
  
  Compatibility: the declaration and check finding codes: a lens takes a new key, `create`, a boolean; a document that does not say it is unchanged, and one that says something other than true or false is refused by the document's shape, or warned of (`lens-create`) in a TypeScript declaration. The `add-lens` edit takes `create`. Ops, stored formats, the wire and derived tool schemas are unchanged.
- 37745ff: A relation can go to a kind in another app, and the linked record is read there as whoever is looking (FR-171, the full relation). 0.1.29 named another app; this names a record in it. A relation's `to` may be `"@<app address>/<kind>"` — `"forHouse": { "to": ["@nick/house/house"], "cardinality": "one" }` — and `linkedRecordField("@nick/house/house")` says the same in a declaration, which `toDocument` writes back as the relation. Linking picks a record there and never touches the other app: the record that links keeps which one under the relation's name, as `"house:h1"` (or a list), so its derived edit sets it, undoes it, logs it and keeps it from a seat a sight keeps it from like any value. `graview check` says in sentences what such a relation cannot be — `linked-mixed`, `linked-apps`, `linked-target`, `linked-inverse`, `linked-append-only`, `act-linked`, `rule-linked` — with the checker, so a page that compiles without it carries none of their words. The host reads the far side beside `resolveApp`: `linkedRecords` on `mount`, `<Embed>`, `GraviewProvider` and the routed face answers `resolve(ref, viewer)` with the record's label, describe line, card and the fields that viewer may see, `{ denied: true }` or `null`, `find(search, viewer)` with the records that viewer may link, and `open(ref, viewer)`, optional, the seam a press on a linked record opens it through. The record's page and the scene's record panel draw it with its own card from its home app, and a document card's `{ "field": "forHouse" }` names it; a viewer the host refuses sees "linked record (no access)" and nothing of it. "Change" beside it, a form and the scene's ask pick another from what the host lists. The rule language walks it by name, `forHouse.listPrice`, in a computed field, a block or a rule, as the viewer: a far record it cannot read reads as nothing and is said — a computed value says why it has none, a rule that would not hold without it cannot be judged — and never thrown; the page reads again as each answer arrives (`store.reread()`). When the far value changes, the host calls `recordLinkedChange(store, { ref, fields, app })` and the reading app's history keeps an op authored by the other app on each record that links it — "Buy it outright: the linked record changed in House — list price" — naming what changed and none of its values. `describe_app` lists each relation under "Linked from other apps" with how to link one, the derived edit's tool says its argument is a record of that app as `"<kind>:<id>"`, and a seat finds one with `find_linked`, listed only for an app with such a relation. `@graview/core/linked` holds `linkedRecordsOver`, the host over stores held in memory, read through each far store's `seenBy(viewer)`, which the tests read two compiled documents through and a host holding both apps can use as it is; `graview-linked-records` is the skill that teaches it. A hosted page's first load is 5 107 bytes larger (550 088 over the vendors document, 544 981 on main), 4 648 handed a compiled app, both under their budgets; what draws a linked record is a chunk fetched with the first one drawn, and the embed's budgets are raised by files of their own: the pages face 4 585 / 1 704 bytes minified / gzipped, every face 20 162 / 7 423, the studio handed in 13 618 / 4 936.
  
  Compatibility: additive. The document format gains a relation target `"@<app address>/<kind>"` in `kinds.<kind>.edges.<name>.to` (`document.schema.json`'s `edges.to` items match a kind name or that target), its value kept on the linking record as `"<kind>:<id>"` or a list of them, and `graview-compiled@1` carries it; a build that predates it refuses such a target by path. The check gains `linked-mixed`, `linked-target`, `linked-apps`, `linked-inverse`, `linked-append-only`, `act-linked` and `rule-linked`. `mount`, `<Embed>`, `GraviewProvider` and the routed face's context gain an optional `linkedRecords: { resolve(ref: { address, kind, id }, viewer): Promise<{ address, kind, id, label, describe?, card?, fields, href?, app? } | { denied: true } | null>; find({ address, kinds, query, limit }, viewer): Promise<LinkedRecord[] | { denied: true }>; open?(ref, viewer): void }`. Ops and primitives are unchanged: a far change is an ordinary op appended with author `{ kind: "system", id: "app:<address>", name }`, `via: "app:<address>"`, the linking records in `reads` and no primitives. `ReadableField` gains `linked`, a form gains the control `linked`, a document card's resolved field block gains `linked`, and the derived `edit-<kind>` of an app with such a relation describes its argument with `"x-graview-linked"`; an app with none has the same tools as before. What a host does: read `capabilities().shipped` for FR-171 before writing such a relation, pass `linkedRecords` to have a linked record drawn and walked, and call `recordLinkedChange` when a far value changes.
- 84ad32f: A record's change starts from what the record holds. "Change the feature" opened with "—" in every field, on a record page and beside the act in the scene, so a person changing one fact could not see the others and a screen reader heard "Stage, —, pop-up button". Every field of the derived edit now opens on the record's value — a text, Markdown as written, a choice, money as it is typed ("12,500" beside its "$"), a date and how it repeats, a link, an app, and a record in another app — and only what was changed is sent, so saving one field leaves the rest as they were and the change says only that field. In the scene, how a date repeats is asked with the picker the pages ask with, never as a box to type in, and a choice the record holds is marked among the others (`aria-current`).
  
  Compatibility: no surface changes. `@graview/core/forms` exports `editStart(mutation, graph, args)`, the record's values for the fields its derived edit asks, and `changedArgs(args, start)`, what a form that started there sends. Derived tool names and input schemas are unchanged.
- c4a953d: What a screen reader is told, stop by stop. `verify-screen-reader` walks the Roadmap and the todo example through the accessibility tree Chromium and WebKit expose — the bar, the Pages and Scene switch, a list, a record page, a columns lens, a blocks lens, an act's form, the Ask box, and a change from someone else in another tab — and writes what a reader would hear to `docs/screen-reader-walk.md`. What it found is fixed where it was drawn:
  
  - A place followed from the bar puts the keyboard on the new page's heading. The bar's link stays on the page, so the keyboard stayed on it and the page under it changed in silence; only a person typing (the Find box, the seat, a field) keeps the keyboard where it is.
  - A change somebody else makes is said aloud as it lands, politely, with who made it: "Ben Okafor: Change Billing across apps: stage → …". Their ops only, never this reader's own or an agent's acting for them, and never one withheld from this seat; the keyboard stays where it was.
  - A card in a list or on a board says its name once, first, as the way to it. The link stretched over the card comes before it in the page, and the card's own title in the same words is drawn and not said again (`NamedByItsLink`).
  - A value changed in place says which fact it is: "Stage: Shipped", never "Shipped, button". A heading's words are said as they are (`EditableValue`'s new `said`).
  - A district's way in is named in words from altitude ("See the features in here"), never by its arrow alone.
  - A record's act takes the keyboard into the form it opens, and Escape puts the form away and gives the keyboard back to the act.
  - The arrow beside a picture a record is seen in is named by the picture ("Roadmap, in the scene"), not "↗"; the arrows after "In the scene", "as a list", "Connections" and "Sort and filter" are drawn and not said; a flagged row says "Breaks a rule:" before its name rather than "⚠"; a required field's star is drawn and not said ("Name", required — not "Name star"); and the separators between related places and in the arrange bar are no longer read as words of their own.
  
  Bundles: every face is about 2.6 kB larger minified (0.9 kB gzipped) and the embed with the studio handed in about 1.7 kB (0.5 kB), raised in `scripts/budget-raises/a-screen-reader-is-told-each-stop-*.json`; a hosted page's first load and the embed without the studio are unchanged.
  
  Landed beside graview.dev's scene fixes and a kind's list in its declared order, the three together carry 0.3 KB more up front on a hosted page.
  
  Compatibility: no surface changes. `EditableValue` takes an optional `said`, and its button's accessible name is now the fact and its value; a test that finds it by its value alone finds it by `data-graview-field` instead.
- 93770ac: A static page wears a product's own design (Nick, making graview.dev a Graview app). `renderPlace` from `@graview/pages/static` wrote the derived page or nothing, so a product whose site is its records could print a record only in the framework's look, while the routed face has always taken a design as a registry of pages. It now takes `design`: `records[kind](record, site)` writes a record's page in the product's own markup, and `document(page)` writes the document around whatever a page says — handed its title, language, scheme, the app's stylesheet and masthead, the page's markup, whether it was found, and the record it is about. A design reads only what the seat may see: a `StaticRecord` is the record's fields as that seat reads them and `related(edge, direction?)`, the records it relates to by one relation in the order they were related, and `StaticSite` gives every record of a kind, one by id, and an address under the base; a kind kept from the seat is not there. A writer that answers `undefined` leaves the derived page, and a page with no design is written exactly as before. graview.dev renders every page it serves this way, from its own declaration (`apps/site/app.json`) and the records written out of the repository. The graview-lists skill says how.
  
  Compatibility: ops, stored records, wire messages, the declaration and tool names are unchanged. `@graview/pages/static` exports the types `StaticDesign`, `StaticDocument`, `StaticRecord` and `StaticSite`, and `RenderPlaceOptions` takes `design`.
- 5b8ac1f: A view's `navigate` goes to a kind's list or a lens narrowed, and to a section of a page (Roadmap f-navigate-filters). It took a place's slug and nothing else, so a custom home could not open "features where stage is building" or jump to a heading, and every filtered link needed a lens of its own: the Graview Roadmap's home needed six. Now `graview.navigate({ place: "features", where: { stage: "building" }, section: "Shipping soon" })` goes to the features' list narrowed, at the heading that says "Shipping soon"; `place` is a lens's slug first, then a kind's list by its plural or its name, and a link says the same with `<a data-place="features" data-filter="stage:building" data-section="shipping-soon">`. The filter is held to what the kind can be filtered by — the offers the list's own arranging line makes — and written in the words that line already writes, so the routed face's address is `/features?filter=stage%3Abuilding#shipping-soon` and can be shared: opened from a link it lands on the list narrowed, the section scrolled to and given the keyboard. A lens is gone to narrowed the same way (`/places/<as>?filter=`, the records it is handed narrowed and said so over it, "Only those where stage is building. Show all"); a lens drawn from blocks lists what its own blocks say. On the scene a list is the kind's stop narrowed (`in.filter`, which its arranging line reads) and a lens its picture narrowed; a stop is a picture, not a page of headings, so the scene leaves a section unsought. A field the kind does not have, a choice it does not offer, a filter on one record or a section that names nothing goes nowhere and says why to the view's console and the host's problems ("The filter on "features" goes nowhere: Features have no field "stag" to filter by; they filter by name, stage, area, summary, became, became.status and is."), as a place that is not there already did; a headless run says it too. The graview-worker-view skill teaches it, and `verify-growing` holds it in a browser.
  
  A hosted page's first load carries 0.2 KB more up front for it and for keeping a lens off the lists, raised in `scripts/budget-raises/navigate-filters-and-hidden-lenses-a-hosted-page.json` and `…-a-hosted-page-handed-a-compiled-app.json`; the embed's pages face, the embed with the studio handed in, a worker view's runtime and the guest host's four budgets (the links that judge a navigate, fetched with the first one where a frame is drawn) are raised in their own files beside them.
  
  Compatibility: ops, stored records and the declaration are unchanged. The guest wire takes more on its existing `navigate` message: `{ type: "navigate", nonce, place, where?, section? }` and `{ type: "navigate", nonce, to, section? }`, where `where` is `Record<string, string | number | boolean>` (`GuestWhere`); a host that predates them ignores them and goes unnarrowed. `graview.navigate` and a frame guest's `navigate` take `GuestNavigateTo`: a string, `{ record, section? }`, or `{ place, where?, section? }` (`navigateRequest` from `@graview/guest` spells the message; `GuestNavigateTo` and `GuestWhere` are its types). `GoTo` (`useGoTo`) is now `record(id, also?)`, `place(as, also?)` and a new `list(kind, also?)`, `also` a `GoToAlso`: `{ filter?: string, section?: string }`, the filter in the address's words; a face that provides `GoToContext` writes `list` too. `mountWorkerView`'s `onNavigate` hears an `Arrival` — `{ record, section? }`, `{ place, filter?, section? }` or `{ list, filter?, section? }` — and it and `mountGuestView` take `goTo` (the face's `GoTo`) in its place; `createGuestHost` takes `onArrive` and `onLost`. New subpath `@graview/core/navigate`: `narrowedPath(path, { filter?, section? })` and `sectionSlug(words)`. `PagesApp`'s `onNavigate` path ends with the section it went to (`#shipping-soon`), never a scene's fragment.
- cf85240: An app can name one standard emoji as its page icon and, where it has no logo, its mark. A brand's icon was an SVG or a path, so an app somebody made in a chat had a blank tab and an empty corner of the bar until somebody drew it a picture. `brand.favicon` now also takes `{ emoji: "🏡" }`, in a TypeScript `Brand` and in a document alike: exactly one emoji as the reader sees it (Intl.Segmenter), and one of the standard emoji every emoji set draws (`RGI_Emoji`), so a skin tone, a joined emoji and a flag — 🏡, 👩🏽‍🌾, 🇺🇸 — are each one, and two emoji, letters or nothing are refused in words: `"🏡🏡" cannot be the app's icon, because it is 2 emoji, and a mark is one` (`brand-emoji`, at `brand.favicon.emoji`), from `compileDocument`, `graview check` on a declared app, and `editDocument`. `set-brand` takes `favicon: { emoji }` and says "The app's page icon and mark become 🏡." (or "The app's page icon becomes 🏡." when the app has a logo), and the preview of a changed document says the same sentence. Where the brand has no logo, `AppMark` draws the emoji as text on the app bar of both faces and in an embed, and the routed face's static pages put it in the masthead; with a logo, the logo is the mark and the emoji is only the icon. `faviconHref` makes the icon an SVG with the emoji as text, so a self-hosted app's tab shows it in the reader's own emoji font; a host that wants it alike on every system renders it from a bundled emoji set into icon PNGs and serves those instead, as Graview Cloud does. New from `@graview/core`: `emojiProblem`, `emojiMark`, `emojiOf`, `emojiSvg` and the `FaviconSpec` type. The graview-brand skill says it in two lines. The every-face and studio-handed-in budgets are raised by 2_385 / 795 and 2_409 / 920 bytes (scripts/budget-raises/an-emoji-as-the-apps-mark-every-face.json, scripts/budget-raises/an-emoji-as-the-apps-mark-studio.json); a hosted page's first load stays within its budget.
  
  Compatibility: the document format's `brand.favicon` gains a second form, `{ "emoji": "<one standard emoji>" }`, within `graview-document@1`; a document with a string `favicon` compiles to the same app. The TypeScript `Brand.favicon` is now `FaviconSpec` (`string | { emoji: string }`), so code reading it as a string narrows first. `set-brand`'s `favicon` takes the same object. Check finding codes gain `brand-emoji`. Ops, stored formats, the wire and tool names are unchanged. What Graview Cloud does: render `brand.favicon.emoji` from its bundled emoji set into the icon PNGs it serves, and serve those rather than the framework's text SVG.
- c70440c: A person can scrub back through an app's history and watch it being built (Roadmap f-playback). `pastOf(store, seq)` and `pastOf(store, time)`, on the new `@graview/core/past`, are the app as it stood then — its records, its links, its log and its problems — read, never changed: it offers no act, declared or derived, `permits` says "Nothing changes while you look back.", and every change asked of it is refused with a `PastError`. A seek folds from the nearest point it can, an epoch or a point kept in memory for that store every `pastEvery` ops (64) as an earlier seek passed it, so scrubbing never folds the whole log twice; a store reopened with its log but not the epoch its seed began (a page's browser adapter keeps none) works back from the graph it holds with each op's inverse, so the past begins at the seed rather than at nothing. `pastOf` asked of a seat's view of a store answers under that seat's sight, so a seat sees the past only as it could see it then. `seqAt(store, time)` says where a time falls, and `pastAt(store, when, host)` is the past under the declaration a host says the moment was made under (`declarationAt`). The past is an entry of its own, apart from the store, because a page needs none of it until somebody looks back: the provider fetches it then. `@graview/core/history`, fetched with the timeline, holds what the timeline reads around it: `ticksOf(store, { me, declarations })` is a tick per gesture marked by who made it (`you`, `person`, `agent`, `app`), with a bigger tick where the declaration changed and the sentence it said, from an epoch's `change` or a host's `DeclarationMark`; `declarationSentence` ("Added a Budget kind"), `makerOf`, `makerWords` and `restoreBatches`.
  
  On both faces, every row of Activity has "Show the app as it was here": the face draws the app as it stood just after that change, from the one store every surface reads, with a timeline over the scene and at the head of the routed face's page — "As of" the moment, what was done there and by whom, a tick per change, Play at 1× to 8×, and Back to now. While the past is shown nothing is offered to change it: no act, no undo on Activity, no ask field, no way back after an act, and a design's own control that asks `permits` is disabled. "Restore to here" takes back every change since as one undo, itself undone as one, offered only when this seat may make every one of those changes and the log lets them go together. An undo whose target also came after the earliest op being taken back no longer blocks it, since the two leave the graph as if neither was made.
  
  What the log does not hold is the host's: `GraviewProvider`, `mount` and `PageContext` take `history: { declarations, viewAt }`, the declaration changes it knows (each with the declaration the past is drawn under from its seq) and `viewAt(name, seq)`, a script view's code at a moment, `null` where the view did not exist then. `HostFiles.kept(href)` says whether the host still keeps a file's bytes, and one it has let go of is drawn as "removed later", as is a picture that does not load while somebody looks back. `verify playback` drives it on the todo example in three engines. Up front, the hosted page carries only the seam the faces read the moment from and a store's refusal of a change to the past; the fold, its kept points and the timeline are fetched when somebody first looks back (what the 0.1.30 wave adds up front together is said in `.changeset/the-0-1-30-wave-leaves-the-past-and-the-suggestions-off-the-first-load.md`). The every-face, pages-face, studio and registering-a-worker-view budgets grow by 13_243 / 5_442, 4_630 / 1_928, 5_790 / 2_173 and 482 / 154 bytes in their raise files.
  
  Compatibility: ops and primitives fold as before (`OperationLog.fold` lands each op through the new `foldOp`, unchanged); `checkUndo` no longer counts as blocking a live undo whose target is itself after the earliest op being undone, so an undo that was refused with "Include batch …" over such a pair now runs, and nothing it was allowed before is refused. New API: the `@graview/core/past` entry — `pastOf(store, when: number | string | Date, options?: { declaration?: PastDeclaration }): Store`, `pastAt(store, when, host?: HistoryHost): Store`, `seqAt(store, when: string | Date): number` and `declarationAt(marks, seq)` — and `Store.past`, `Store.pastEvery`, `StoreOptions.pastEvery`, `PastError`, `PastPoint`, `PastDeclaration`, the `@graview/core/history` entry (`ticksOf`, `declarationSentence`, `makerOf`, `makerWords`, `restoreBatches`), `HostFiles.kept`, `history` on `GraviewProvider`, `FrameOptions` and `PageContext`, `usePlayback`, `useLiveStore`, `useOwnPlayback`, `PlaybackContext`, `PastBar` and `prefetchTimeline`.
- c3564eb: The app's own actor suggests what it is not sure of, is held to what people said, and its run of changes is one row of Activity. A background actor that links and prunes records on its own ("Graview, tidying") could apply a change with its lineage (FR-172) and nothing else: what it was unsure of had nowhere to wait, a person could not say "leave this one alone", an app could not say "never auto-link Vendors", and a pass of thirty links buried a person's own changes in Activity. An author may now say `automated: true` (a `system` or `agent` author: the app's own actor; `isAutomated`).
  
  A suggestion that waits is `suggest(store, calls, { author, lineage, via?, intent? })`, on `@graview/core/tidying`: the calls are judged as `applyAll` would judge them for that author, on a rehearsal, and none of it is applied — the log keeps one op that `suggests` them, with no primitives and its lineage, so it persists, travels and is withheld from a seat exactly as any op is. The same suggestion asked again answers the one that waits, and one that would change nothing is not suggested. `suggestions(store, principal?)` lists what waits, under the seat's sight (or the seat whose view `store` is); `acceptSuggestion(store, id, options)` applies the calls as the person, carrying the suggestion's lineage with `lineage.suggestion` naming it; `declineSuggestion(store, id, options)` is an undo of the suggestion, which keeps its lineage as `undoneLineage`. `declined(store, calls)` answers the op that declined these calls, or any one of them — a declined suggestion, or an actor's confident change a person took back — and the store refuses the app's own actor the same change or suggestion again, in a sentence ("Graview, tidying may not make this again: a person declined it on 2026-10-14."). The log is where suggestions live, rather than a seam a host fills, because accepting and declining are then a `call` and an `undo` like any other, and a host persists nothing new. Both faces show what waits in a quiet Suggested tray beside Activity on the bar — what it would do, who suggested it, its basis and how sure, Accept and Decline — fetched, with what reads which suggestions wait, only once the log holds one, so a hosted page carries up front only what holds the actor to what people said (what the 0.1.30 wave adds up front together is said in `.changeset/the-0-1-30-wave-leaves-the-past-and-the-suggestions-off-the-first-load.md`).
  
  Leave this one alone: every kind is given `leave-alone-<kind>` ("Leave this one alone") and `allow-tidying-<kind>` ("Allow tidying again"), ordinary undoable acts that set and clear `$leftAlone` on the record as archive sets `$archived`, offered on the record's page and the scene's record card beside archive, and said at the head of the record's page and in its card. `isLeftAlone(store, id)` reads it. The store refuses the app's own actor any change that touches a record left alone — a patch, a removal, a link at either end — and a suggestion about one, naming the record. A declaration says what is never tidied: `tidying: "never" | "no-links"` on `defineNode` (a document's kind) and `tidying: "never"` on a relation; the actor is refused a change to a kind never tidied, a link to or from a kind never linked, and a link of a relation never tidied, each in a sentence, and may still suggest them for a person to decide. The document edit `set-tidying` (`{ kind, relation?, tidying: "never" | "no-links" | "allowed" }`) says it in a sentence, `diffDocuments` says the same sentence, and `graview check` refuses a grant naming a leave-alone act of a kind never tidied (`grant-act`) and warns of a relation that says what its kind already says (`tidying-redundant`).
  
  A digest in Activity: consecutive batches by one automated author fold into one row, "Graview, tidying Tidied 6 things today", which opens to each change with its own lineage and its own undo; a person's change and a chat seat's never fold, and a suggestion and its declining are the tray's, not rows. The agent's tools gain `list_suggestions` (the actor's basis handed over as somebody else's words), `accept_suggestion` and `decline_suggestion`. `@graview/core/tidying` makes, settles and reads it all for a host and a face: `suggest`, `suggestions`, `acceptSuggestion`, `declineSuggestion`, `declined`, `suggestionsIn`, `declinesIn`, `declinedIn`, `suggestionKey`, `tidyingOf`, `tidyingRefusal`, `foldDigests`, `digestWords`, `isSuggestionBatch` and `LEFT_ALONE`. The embed's budgets for the pages face alone, every face and the studio handed in are raised by what they grew (`scripts/budget-raises/the-apps-own-actor-*.json`). The graview-lists skill teaches it.
  
  Compatibility: ops gain two optional fields, `suggests` (on an op with no primitives, which folds to nothing) and, on a lineage, `suggestion`; authors gain the optional `automated`; a record may carry `$leftAlone: true`, written by an ordinary `patch-node` — additive within op format 1, and a log folds to the same graph on an earlier version. The wire is unchanged: accepting is a `call` whose lineage names the suggestion, declining an `undo`. The document format gains the optional `kinds.<kind>.tidying` and `kinds.<kind>.edges.<relation>.tidying`, the edit `set-tidying`, and the check finding `tidying-redundant` (a warning); `grant-act` also names a leave-alone act of a kind never tidied. Derived tools: every kind gains `leave-alone-<kind>` and `allow-tidying-<kind>` (`{ id }`) unless it is never tidied, and the agent's surface gains `list_suggestions`, `accept_suggestion` and `decline_suggestion` (`{ suggestion }`). New APIs: `isLeftAlone`, `isAutomated` and the type `Suggestion` on `@graview/core`, and `@graview/core/tidying` with `suggest(store, calls, options: SuggestOptions): Operation | undefined`, `suggestions(store, principal?): readonly Suggestion[]`, `acceptSuggestion(store, id, options?): ApplyResult`, `declineSuggestion(store, id, options?): ApplyResult`, `declined(store, calls): Operation | undefined` and `SuggestOptions`. Stored formats are unchanged.
- 489ff27: The routed face's last line clears the ask field. The page's main kept room at its end for the field standing over the foot, but the line after it, "Remembered in this browser · Start fresh", was the page's very end and stood under the field on a phone however far the page scrolled. It keeps the same room.
  
  Compatibility: no surface changes.
- eb3bdb7: A right-click on the pages opens the scene's context menu. It was the scene's alone, so on a list, a record's page or a picture's page a right-click met the browser's menu and the acts were a scroll away. It is the same menu now — the same pane, the same acts `@graview/tools` derives, narrowed by the seat, with what is withheld said the way the scene says it — opened on what the gesture landed on: a list's row, a record's page, a card or a link to a record, a lens's mark, all about that record, with "Open", "Open in a new tab" and "Show in the scene" beside its acts; a value on a record's page, with "Change “Due”" first, which opens that value's own edit in place; a picture's ground, about its place, headed with its title and offering what its kind offers ("Add a task"); and the page's own ground, with the list's kind's acts and Find. Shift+F10 and the context-menu key open it on what has the keyboard, and a finger resting on the page opens it on a phone (on a list that chooses, resting on a row still chooses it). The keyboard goes into the menu and back where it was opened; Escape closes it.
  
  The browser keeps its own menu in a text field or editable text, over text somebody selected, and whenever Shift is held with the right-click — the way to the browser's "Open link in new tab", which the menu also offers itself. A design's own value takes part by carrying `data-graview-field` beside `data-graview-editable`, and its own row by being a link to the record or carrying `data-graview-record`.
  
  A host adds a section of its own to the menu on either face: `menuSections` on `GraviewProvider`, on `embed()` and on the pages' context, each a function told what the menu was opened on (`MenuTarget`: `face`, `on`, `about`, `field`, `place`, `href`, `element`) that answers a `MenuSection` — rows (`items`: `{ id, label, run, hint? }`) or what it draws itself (`render(close)`), before the acts (`at: "start"`) or after them — or nothing. A face adds its own with `MenuSections` from `@graview/react/drawing`. `graview-desk` teaches the seam, and `graview-lists` how a design's own rows and values take part. The menu and its pane stay off a page's first load: they are fetched when the page is first idle, or at the first right-click, and a hosted page's first load carries nothing more for them. The embed's pages face alone carries 3.7 KB more in all (the menu's pane, now fetched there too), every face 0.5 KB and the embed with the studio handed in 5.0 KB, raised in `scripts/budget-raises/the-pages-open-the-scenes-context-menu-*.json`.
  
  Compatibility: no surface changes. New on the provider, `embed()` and `PageContext`: `menuSections` (the context menu's section seam). New on `PointerMenu`: `about`, `face`, `field`, `place`, `href`, `title` and `returnTo`, all optional. `Inspector` takes `openActs`. The `acts` popover is drawn on the pages too.
- 88c84c3: On the routed face the person's button names the seat the app offered them under, not its principal id. The face read the app's `seats` for its history and never handed them to the provider it draws the bar under, so the button said "u-4f1c9a — you, your seat and your settings". The provider is handed them, and a seat taken from the person's menu is the face's principal until the app hands it another, so the menu and the pages sit in one seat.
  
  Compatibility: no surface changes.
