## 0.1.29

### Patch Changes

- 7966f5d: A change says what it was based on, and a link made less than sure is drawn as such until a person keeps it (FR-172). An author said who moved and nothing said why one change was made or how sure its maker was, so a background actor that links and prunes on its own ("Graview, tidying") could not show its reasons or be told apart from a person's work. An op may now carry `lineage: { basis, confidence?, model? }` — given as `ApplyOptions.lineage` to `apply`, `applyAll`, `applyPrimitives` and `undo`, or on an `AppendOp` — checked before anything is kept (a basis of 1–500 characters, a confidence from 0 to 1, a model of 1–120) and kept in the log, on the batch and on the wire. On the op rather than its author, because an author is the same for every change a seat makes and a basis is this change's own. It is said where the change is: "based on “both name 12 Harbor Street” · 60% sure · jev-1" on a record page's history line and in the scene's recent changes, and a record page says what the record was made on. A link whose making op was less than sure, by anyone but a person, is provisional: the scene draws it in short dashes and quieter, its basis on hover and in its acts when it is pressed or long-pressed, and a record page marks it "unsure", the mark saying the basis and offering "Keep this link". Keeping it is `keep-link` (`KEEP_LINK`), an act every store answers to and no list offers, which takes the link away and puts it back as the person's op; any person's op that takes the link away does too, and undoing the keep makes it provisional again. An undo of a change with a lineage keeps it as `undoneLineage`, so a host can read "do not make this again" — a merge (FR-173) as much as a link. `@graview/core/provenance` is the one answer every face and host reads — `linkProvenance(store, edge)`, `isProvisional`, `provisionalLinks`, `provenanceWords` — fetched with what draws a link, so the hosted page's first load stays under its budget. Nothing in the framework decides what to link.
  
  Compatibility: ops gain two optional fields, `lineage` and `undoneLineage`, additive within op format 1: an op without them reads and folds as before, and a build that predates them keeps them on an op it receives. `Batch` gains an optional `lineage`. The wire's `call` and `undo`, on the socket and over `POST /graview/ops`, may carry `lineage`; a change whose lineage is not one is refused as `invalid`, and a server that predates it ignores the field. Every store answers to `keep-link` (`{ kind, from, to }`), which no list of acts — `allMutations`, `permittedMutations`, an agent's tools — offers; an app that declares its own `keep-link` keeps it. Stored formats, the document format, check finding codes and derived tool names and input schemas are unchanged. `capabilities().shipped` gains FR-172. What a host does: a background actor passes `lineage` with each change it makes, reads `linkProvenance` or `provisionalLinks` for what a person has not yet kept, and reads an undo's `undoneLineage` for what a person declined.
- 55ec52b: A date can repeat: a weekly practice, a monthly bill or a yearly renewal is one record, not one per occurrence made by hand (the Roadmap's f-recurring-dates). A document says `"repeats": true` on a `date` or `datetime` field; a declaration puts `startsAtRepeat: repeatField()` beside `startsAt`, and `toDocument` writes it as `repeats`. A record keeps its first occurrence in the date and, beside it under the date's name and `Repeat`, how it repeats: `{ "every": "week", "on": ["tue"], "until": "2026-12-15" }` — `every` day, week, month or year, an `interval`, `on` weekdays for a week or days of the month for a month (`-1` the last), `until`, `count` and `except` days — or `null` once it no longer does. The store refuses anything else (`validRepeat`). Occurrences are worked out and never stored (`occurrences`, `nextOccurrence`, `dateOn`): a rule, a template, `today()`'s comparisons, a list's sort, filter and date groups, and a kind's stop read the next occurrence on the day they are judged on, so the tick that turns the day (FR-176) moves it with nothing written. A day a month lacks falls on the month's last (Jan 31 monthly is Feb 28, Feb 29 yearly is Feb 28 off a leap year), a moment's time of day is kept as written so six in the evening stays six across a clock change, and `count` counts skipped days. The calendar draws every occurrence in the days it shows, marked ↻ and "· repeats" and not dragged, and counts a series once, at its next, where it is a glance; a kind's stop opens on a calendar by a series' next occurrence and counts it in "in the next 7 days"; a record's facts, a row and a card say "Every Tuesday, next Oct 27" in the app's locale (`repeatAsRead`, `repeatingDateAsRead` on `@graview/core/words`). Three derived acts on a kind with a date that repeats, permitted as its edit is, each one undo with a sentence: `skip-<kind>` ("Skip this one", `{ id, on }`) adds the day to `except`; `detach-<kind>` ("Change just this one") makes that occurrence a record of its own, with the series' fields, the links it makes and what was changed, and an exception on the series; `split-<kind>` ("Change from here") ends the series the day before and starts a new one that day, `continues` naming the old. A record's page has a "Repeat…" control beside the date: a picker (does not repeat, daily, weekly, monthly, yearly; every how many; which weekdays; the month's day or its last; until) that says the rule back in words as it is chosen, every control labeled, kept by the edit act; a form asks for a repeat with the same picker (`FormField` gains `control: "repeat"`). `editDocument` gains `set-repeats`, the studio offers "Say whether it repeats" and writes `repeatField()` back, a migration moves a repeat with its renamed date, `graview types` writes `<date>Repeat?: Repeat | null`, and an act's tool schema describes it with `"x-graview-repeat": true`. Time zones beyond the one a moment says, and the rest of RFC 5545 (by-week-number, by-set-position, hourly), are left out. A hosted page's first load is 5 797 bytes larger minified, 2 381 gzipped, than the batch it joined (549 531 / 192 258 against 543 734 / 189 877, under its budgets of 550 912 / 192 512), and 5 425 / 2 274 handed a compiled app (507 320 / 177 411 against 509 440 / 177 664): the occurrence math, the repeat's check and the three acts, which a rule and the store need as the page loads; the words, the picker and the calendar's drawing are fetched with what draws them. The embed's every face, the studio handed in and the pages face alone are raised by files of their own. A document's `datetime` field now reads back as a date and time (`fieldSpecOf`), so a kind's stop finds it for its calendar and its dates, and the stop says its date by the field's label ("in the next 7 days by starts"). verify-repeats holds it at desk width in three engines.
  
  Compatibility: additive. The declaration and check finding codes: the document format gains `repeats` on a date or datetime field, within `graview-document@1` and `graview-compiled@1`, the edit `set-repeats` and the finding `repeat-clash`; a build that predates them refuses the key as `unknown-key`. Derived tool names and input schemas: a kind with a date that repeats gains `skip-<kind>`, `detach-<kind>` and `split-<kind>`, and its `edit-<kind>` takes `<date>Repeat`; a kind with none is unchanged. `ReadableField` gains `repeats`, `PlacedEntry` gains `repeats`, `DerivedAct` gains `skip`, `detach` and `split`. Ops and primitives, the stored formats and the wire are unchanged. What a host does: serve 0.1.29 or later before writing `repeats`; a record's repeat travels as any other field value.
- 3cd624d: A lens is listed once, however many kinds it draws. The program's scene bar read "The whole thing · What the talks are about · The timetable · The timetable · A dozen workshops": its one calendar over talks and workshops was registered on each kind, so that either district goes in to it, and every list of places took each registration for a picture of its own. A place now says when it is one picture over several kinds (`Place.over`, given by `ViewMeta.over`, which `registerDeclaredLenses` sets from the lens's bindings), and every list reads it through `onePerPicture`: the scene's bar and its tabs, the Pages bar and its More, the gallery and the pictures' page, Find and `search_graph`, the seat's places, `placesOf` and `placesFrom`, `graview describe`, and the slugs a guest view may link to. A district of either kind still goes in to it, the tab is pressed from either, and choosing it keeps the district in focus when it is one of its kinds. Its page is at `/places/<as>` alone and draws every kind it is over, with each kind's list beside it. Two different lenses that share a title on different kinds are another matter: `graview check` warns `lens-title-repeated`, and the bars and the gallery say whose — "The timetable · talks" (`placeLabel`).
  
  Compatibility: `placesOf` and `placesFrom` list a picture over several kinds once, under the first of its kinds in the arrangement's order, with a new optional `over` naming every kind it is over; its `address` is `/places/<as>` and its `stop` `#view=<as>`, where before each kind had an entry at `/places/<as>?of=<plural>` (those addresses still open the picture). `describe_place` takes the one slug as before. A search's place hit for such a picture comes once. `Place`, `ViewMeta` and `ViewRegistration` gain an optional `over`; `onePerPicture`, `samePicture` and `placeLabel` are new exports of `@graview/core`, and `sharesItsName` no longer counts a picture's own other kinds. Check gains the warning `lens-title-repeated`. Stored formats, the document format and derived tool names and input schemas are unchanged.
  
  The embed's budgets are raised by what this adds (`scripts/budget-raises/a-lens-is-listed-once-*.json`): the pages face alone 617 / 260 bytes, every face 1,325 / 529, the embed with the studio handed in 1,312 / 529, and the guest host on the open kit 77 / 43, minified / gzipped. A hosted page's first load holds to its budget unraised.
- 897c7bb: A list reads well by default. On the Program's Sessions grouped by day, the title stood at forty pixels, every row's name was set bold and wrapped to a third and fourth line on a phone so every row shouted and nothing was secondary, each row said "Sep 25, 2017" under a heading that said "Sep 25, 2017", its date stood at whatever x its length left it, and the related links were underlined heavily in the line's own ink. The routed face now sets its words on one scale, a minor third from the reader's own rem, written by the theme as four custom properties: `--graview-text-small` (0.833rem: a row's date, a count, a relation, a group's heading), `--graview-text-body` (1rem: reading text and a row's name), `--graview-text-heading` (1.2rem: a section) and `--graview-text-title` (1.728rem: a page's title, where it was 30 to 40 pixels by the window's width); the sizes the pages had picked between them, from 0.75rem to 1.4375rem and a title by the window's width, are gone, and the static export reads the same four. A row of a kind's list, and a hit on the search page, is one anatomy: its name at the reading size and a medium weight, two lines at most with the rest on its page (and in its title when it is long), the whole row its link with a hover, a press and a keyboard ring of its own; its glance small and muted in a column of its own on a desk, one column for the whole list so the dates down it start at one x, and under the name where the list is narrower than 36rem. A list's rows are one line or two, so it keeps two heights. A row leaves off its glance the field its group is by (a date grouped by day, unless the row's value has a time the heading does not say) and a choice the list is narrowed to one value of. A group's heading is small and semibold in the body face, its count beside it, and over the framework's own rows stays at the top of the scroll on a desk. A link on a page is in the accent `graview check` holds to 4.5:1 on the ground, the same visited or not, with a hairline underline at half strength. A page's end clears the ask field by its measured height and a phone's home bar. A declared row's title on a list, and the kind stop's heading, header row, small text and names (at a medium weight), read the same scale. The graview-brand skill says how an app moves a step.

The look moves (docs/stability.md, "Not a surface: the look"): four new custom properties, `--graview-text-small`, `-body`, `-heading` and `-title`, which an app's own stylesheet may set to move a step everywhere it is used. A brand that names its own faces and weights keeps them: headings on the routed face no longer set their own family and weight but take the theme's heading rule (`--graview-font-display`, `--graview-weight-display`), so a brand's display weight now wins where 600 was written over it; a group's heading is set in `--graview-font-body` at `--graview-weight-label`. A row of the derived list is `li.graview-row` holding `a.graview-row-title` and `span.graview-row-glance` (a search hit is the same, still `[data-testid="search-hit"]`); a group's or a section's heading is `h2.graview-group-head`; `record-row`, `records`, `list-group`, `list-section` and `list-index` are as they were. Links on the routed face are drawn in `--graview-accent`, where they were the text's own ink.
- 933761a: A Markdown field named in a view never shows its syntax, a row's glance is two lines at most, and a first sentence ends at a code-ish word's stop (FR-180). A declared `{ "text": "{body}" }` block printed the field as written — "## Plan", "**Friday**" — where the record's own page drew it as prose: a text block that is a Markdown field alone is now drawn as Markdown on a page (the resolved block carries `markdown: true` and the text as written) and said by its start in a card or a row; named beside other words, or in a title or a badge, it is said as its words with the syntax taken out, on every face and in `describe_place`. A filter (`{body|excerpt}`, `{body|upper}`) says it as before. The 0.1.28 brief's row ran four lines in the glance column; the glance is now clamped to two, like the row's name. And "capabilities().shipped." was taken for an abbreviation, so an excerpt ran on into the next sentence: only short letters between stops ("e.g.", "U.S.", "Ph.D.") and the usual titles ("Dr.") are abbreviations now, and a word like "graview.dev." ends its sentence.
  
  Compatibility: a resolved text block (`resolveBlocks`, `@graview/core/blocks`) may carry `markdown: true`, and `BlockContext` takes `glance` (set by `blockContextFor` for a card or a row); a block about a field that is not Markdown resolves as before. What a card, a row or a describer says of a Markdown field named bare in a template changes from the text as written to its words. The document format, check finding codes, ops, stored formats, the wire and tool schemas are unchanged.
- dedefed: A near word finds the record, and says it is near (the Roadmap's f-semantic-search). A typo or another form of a word found nothing, and "the florist" did not find Bloom & Co, tagged flowers. Now, when the words find fewer than five records exactly, `search` (`@graview/core/search`) adds the records a near word finds, ranked after every exact hit: one slip in a word of 4–7 letters or two in a longer one ("tulpis" finds Tulips Galore, "flowr" the start of "flowers"), or another form of the word — a plural, -ing, -ed ("planting" finds "planted"). No model is asked for that, and it is fetched with Find as the matcher is: a near pass over 8,000–12,000 records adds a few milliseconds to a keystroke (6–16 ms in all on the gauntlet's and the discography's records). A host may give a ranker for what no spelling finds — `HostAi.near`, or `rank` on `search` — handed the words and only the records the seat may see, answering scores (0–1) and the record's word that was near; Find shows the exact hits at once and the ranker's when they arrive. Find's row for a near hit reads "Near “florist” — tags: flowers", the count says how many are near, and `search_graph` answers `near: true` with `why.matched`, so an agent knows it was not what it asked for. Going to a record by name and the conversation's "things called …" stay exact.
  
  Compatibility: a search's answer may now include near hits — a node hit's optional `near: true`, a `why` with `strength: "near"` and optional `asked`, `matched` and `by` (`"spelling"`, `"stem"`, `"meaning"`), and `SearchResult.later` when a ranker was asked; `search` takes `near` (false for exact hits only) and `rank`. `search_graph`'s name and input schema are unchanged; its description says what a near hit is, so a seat's tool-surface hash moves, and its answer may hold hits with `near: true`. `HostAi` gains `near`, `createToolRuntime` and `createMcpHttpHandler` take `near`, and `@graview/core` exports the types `NearBy`, `NearCandidate`, `NearScore` and `SearchRanker`. A ranker is handed only records the seat sees, past the kinds searched, the horizon and the conditions, so a hidden record's text never reaches a host's model (docs/stability.md). Ops, stored formats, the wire and the document format are unchanged. The hosted page's first load grows by 121 B (70 B gzipped), the provider waiting for a ranker's answer, within its budget; the pages face, every face and the embed with the studio handed in are raised by the near matcher's measured size (`scripts/budget-raises/a-near-word-finds-the-record-*.json`).
- A phone, a place and a length of time are field types of their own. Templates held all three as strings: a phone that was not a link to call, an address that was not a link to a map, and lengths of time nobody could add up. A document says `{ "type": "phone" }`, `{ "type": "place" }` and `{ "type": "duration" }`; a declaration `phoneField()`, `placeField()` and `durationField()`. A phone is kept as it is entered (a form trims it) and refused only when it cannot be one — fewer than three digits, or letters other than an extension's (`PHONE`: digits, spaces, `+`, `-`, `.`, `/`, parentheses, `ext. 12`, `x12`), at most 40 characters; it is said grouped as "(555) 123-4567" for a reader in the US or Canada where it is a North American number and as entered everywhere else, and drawn as a `tel:` link. A place is a line of words, at most 500 characters, drawn as a link that opens the reader's maps app in a tab of its own (`rel="noopener noreferrer"`): maps.apple.com on Apple's platforms, which opens Maps, and Google's universal Maps address elsewhere, which opens the Google Maps app on Android and the map in the browser otherwise — no one address opens every reader's own app (`geo:` does nothing in a desktop browser), and neither needs a key or fetches anything until it is followed. A duration is a whole number of minutes, none less than none, bounded by the field's `min`, `max` and `step` like a number; it is typed as "1h 30m", "90", "1:30" or "1.5 h" (`parseDuration`), said "1 h 30 min" with the locale's figures (`sayDuration`), and sorted and summed as the number it is: a computed field that sums, adds or subtracts durations — `sum(out('rehearsals'), length)`, `planned - spent` — is said as a duration too, as is one a declaration says `as: "duration"`. Each is taken by `editDocument`'s `add-field` and `retype-field` (a duration keeps its range), kept by a migration where the old value reads as one and cleared where it does not, read from a spreadsheet's cell or refused in words, written by `graview types` (a phone and a place as `string`, a duration as `number`), asked for by a form (a `tel` box; a duration in words, sent as minutes), the workbench and a value changed in place, and described to an agent with `"x-graview-type"` (and `"x-graview-unit": "minutes"` for a duration, which a seat sends as 90, never "1h 30m"). A record's page, the scene's record panel and a document card's field block draw the links, each a target of at least 24 pixels; where the value is changed in place, "Call" and "Map" stand beside it. The studio offers, declares and writes back all three. `@graview/core/words` gains `parseDuration`, `sayDuration`, `sayPhone`, `telHref`, `mapsHref`, `fieldSays`, `typedWords`, `typedHref` and `typedFieldOf`. A hosted page's first load is 392 / 168 bytes larger minified / gzipped (360 / 179 handed a compiled app), under its budgets; the embed's every face carries 7 885 / 3 336 more and with the studio handed in 7 242 / 3 150, each raised by a file of its own. verify-field-types holds it at desk width on both faces in three engines.
  
  Compatibility: additive. The document format gains the field types `phone`, `place` and `duration`, and `graview-compiled@1` carries them; a host's build that predates them refuses a document using them by path, and a page that predates them refuses such a compiled app as `compiled-shape` and compiles the document, which refuses the type by path. These carry no FR id, so a host validates a document that uses them against the `document.schema.json` of the oldest build it serves. `ReadableField` gains `href`, `ScalarField` gains `holds`, `ArgShape` gains `holds` (words) and `duration` (a number), and a computed field may say `as: "duration"`. A derived tool's input schema for a declaration with none of these fields is unchanged.
- 8e09c51: A place is rendered to static HTML off the browser (FR-174). `renderPlace(store, declaration, place, { viewer, look, today, base })` on the new entry `@graview/pages/static` answers `{ html, css, title, found }`: a kind's list, a record, a declared place or the home as one self-contained document in the app's look — its brand's tokens, type and shape inlined as CSS, light unless asked or declared otherwise — with no script, no form and nothing to fetch. It says what `describePlace` says the place shows that seat, a record's describe line under its name, its facts in its page's sections and its relations in the declaration's words, each listed record by its card or row (or its glance), and puts each section of a place — a board's column, a roster's week — on a page of its own in print. A record the viewer may not see, a kind kept from them and an address that names nothing are answered alike, `found: false`. The entry imports no React, router or face, so a Worker renders a fixed link — a schedule as a PDF, a roster — on each change without a DOM; nothing on a hosted page imports it. `staticCss(brand, scheme)` is the sheet alone.
  
  `describePlace`'s derived home no longer counts a kind kept from the seat — an administered module's people for a member, a kind the seat sees none of — or a disabled module's, as the routed home never listed them.
  
  Compatibility: additive. `@graview/pages/static` is a new entry, and `capabilities().shipped` gains FR-174. `describe_place` says one line fewer on the home for a seat a kind is kept from.
- 15c033b: A record can be archived and brought back, and two records that are one can be merged (FR-173). Every kind is given three more derived acts beside `edit-<kind>` and `remove-<kind>`: `archive-<kind>` and `unarchive-<kind>` ("Bring it back") take `{ id }`, and `merge-<kind>` takes `{ id, into }`. Archive keeps the record — its fields, its links, its history — and takes it out of lists, the Scene, counts, computed fields and rules: every graph read that lists records (`allNodes`, `nodesOfKind`, `count`, `allEdges`, `edgesOfKind`, `out`, `in`, `neighbors`) reaches the live ones unless it is given `{ archived: true }` or `{ archived: "all" }`, and a read by id (`getNode`, `has`, `outEdges`, `inEdges`) finds a record archived or not. A merge moves the duplicate's links to the kept record and archives the duplicate, its op saying `mergedInto`, both histories kept and undone as one. A host archives with `store.apply({ name: "archive-<kind>", args: { id } })`, and a mutation with `ctx.archiveNode`, `ctx.unarchiveNode` and `ctx.mergeNode`; each goes through the policy, the seat's sight and the log like any act, permitted through the acts that make the kind as a remove is, and a grant may name each on its own. The host decides what to archive; the framework keeps the state.
  
  A rule or a computed field asks for archived records explicitly: `all('task', true)` for the archived ones, `all('task', 'all')` for both, and `archived()` says whether a record is. Search still finds an archived record, marked `archived` and after the live ones, and `is:archived` asks for those alone; the agent's `search_graph` and `get_graph` take `archived` and reach the live records unless asked, and `get_node` says `archived: true`. A lens and a list page take `archived` beside `sort`, `filter`, `group` and `q` (`in.archived=all`, `?archived=true`), a list says "+N archived" beneath it, and an archived record's page says so at its head with "Bring it back" where the seat may. A name finds a live record before an archived one, and an archived one when no live one has it. `editDocument` renames and drops the new acts with their kind, `graview check` knows them, and a kind's JSON Schema says `$archived`. The hosted page carries 1.3 KB more up front, under its budget, which is not raised; the embed's budgets for the pages face alone (gzipped), every face and the studio handed in are raised by what they grew (`scripts/budget-raises/a-record-can-be-archived-and-brought-back-*.json`).
  
  Compatibility: additive to ops and primitives — an archived record is `$archived: true` on the record, written by an ordinary `patch-node`, and a merge's op carries the optional `mergedInto`; a log folds to the same graph on an earlier version, where an archived record reads as live. Derived tools: every kind gains `archive-<kind>`, `unarchive-<kind>` and `merge-<kind>`, and `search_graph` and `get_graph` take an optional `archived`; `get_graph` no longer lists archived records unless asked. The rule language gains `archived()` and a second argument to `all`. `capabilities().shipped` gains FR-173.
- 3b06199: A record can link another app, and the host says what that app is called (FR-171). A relation's `to` names kinds inside one document, so a scenario in one app could not point at a house in another. The first step is a field: `{ "type": "app-link" }` in a document, `appLinkField()` in a declaration, holding another app's address — `@owner/app`, an id its host knows it by, or an absolute http(s) address, at most 500 characters (`isAppAddress`). The store refuses anything else as `invalid`, `editDocument`'s `add-field` and `retype-field` take the type, a migration keeps a value that is an address and clears one that is not, a spreadsheet's cell is read as one or refused in words (FR-177), `graview types` writes it as a string said to be another app's address, a form asks for it with "@owner/app or https://…" as its placeholder, and an act's tool schema gives it the address's `pattern` and `"x-graview-app-link": true` so an agent sets an address rather than a name. A host hands `resolveApp(address, viewer)` to `mount`, `<Embed>`, `GraviewProvider` or the routed face's context, answering `{ name, icon?, headline?, href }`, `{ denied: true }` or `null`: the field draws as that app's icon, name and home headline, linked to `href`, on a record's page and in the scene's record panel, and as its icon and name on a list's row and a document card's field block. A viewer the host refuses sees "linked app (no access)" and nothing of the app. Without a resolver, or where the host knows no such app, the address is drawn as it is kept, a link only when it is an absolute http(s) address. The host is asked once per address and viewer, however many records link it, and a throw is taken as `null`. Where an act may write the field, a "Change" control beside the link edits the address. What draws it is a chunk fetched with the first app-link drawn: a hosted page's first load is 509 bytes larger (557 327 bytes over the vendors document, 556 818 on main), 501 handed a compiled app, and the scene before it draws 1 647, all under their budgets; the embed's pages face carries 1 884 / 1 607 bytes more minified / gzipped, every face 7 001 / 3 726, each raised by a file of its own. A relation whose `to` is another app's kind, an expression that walks it and the history of the far value are left for 0.2. `capabilities().shipped` gains FR-171.
  
  Compatibility: additive. The document format gains the field type `app-link`, and `graview-compiled@1` carries it; a build that predates it refuses the type by path, and a page that predates it refuses such a compiled app as `compiled-shape` and compiles the document. `mount`, `<Embed>`, `GraviewProvider` and the routed face's context gain an optional `resolveApp`; `ReadableField` gains `app`, `ScalarField` gains `app`, and a document card's resolved field block gains `app`. A derived tool's input schema for a declaration with no app-link field is unchanged. What a host does: read `capabilities().shipped` for FR-171 before writing an `app-link` field, and pass `resolveApp` to have one drawn as the app rather than as its address.
- df64e2b: A sight hides a field, not only a kind (Roadmap f-field-sight). A role saw a whole record or none of it, so a roster where parents see every player's name and not their phone number could not be said. A sight may now say `hide: ["phone", "medical"]`, or `fields: ["name"]` as an allow list, and sights add up: a field is shown when any sight that shows the seat the record shows it, so `{ roles: ["parent"], kinds: ["player"], own: true }` beside the hiding one gives each parent their own child whole. A field kept from a seat is judged in the seat view, so it never leaves the store for that seat: not on the record it is served (a required one withholds the record, as a hidden required reference does), not in the log — an op that adds or removes such a record, writes such a field or whose sentence read one is withheld, whatever the field held, its primitives served without it — nor in an act's answer, a conflict, a search hit, a name a call resolves, a form, an edit offered in place, a static page or a tool's answer. A computed field and a rule are worked out over what the seat is served, so a phone it may not see reads as none. The seat may not write such a field: an act that would is refused as one given an argument it does not take (`invalid`), the same whatever it held and whatever was written, and an import refuses a column that goes to one as it refuses a column that goes to no field. `graview check` names a field a sight misspells (`sight-names-an-unknown-field`), one a record cannot do without (`sight-hides-a-required-field`) and a label or description that reads a kept field (`sight-hides-a-labeled-field`, a warning). `add-sight` and `remove-sight` take the keys, a field the sight's kinds lack is refused (`sight-field`), and the studio carries, adds, changes and writes them back. Judging a field is `@graview/core/field-sight`, which `@graview/ship`'s server entry loads; a page that holds its whole store (`openStore` from `@graview/ship/browser`, or a `Store` of its own) imports it once, as does a host that serves seats without `@graview/ship`, and a page `openRemote` opened needs none, its host having judged what it sent. Without it, a sight with `hide` or `fields` shows nothing: the store keeps the record rather than serve a field it cannot judge. The new graview-sight skill teaches it; graview-permissions points there.
  
  Compatibility: the declaration gains two optional keys on a sight, `policy.sees[].hide` and `policy.sees[].fields` (field names), and the document schema with them; a declaration without them compiles and serves as before. Four finding codes are new (`sight-names-an-unknown-field`, `sight-hides-a-required-field`, `sight-hides-a-labeled-field`, a document edit's `sight-field`). Ops and the wire are unchanged in shape: a seat with a kept field is served fewer fields and more withheld ops, and an op's `described` may name `<id>#<field>` — a field its sentence read — beside the record ids it named; a build that predates it reads those as ids that name no record. `WIRE_PROTOCOL` does not move. The risk is an older build: a host or a page on a build before this one ignores `hide` and `fields` and SHOWS the field to the role it was hidden from — so a host serves a declaration that says either only on a build that has `@graview/core/field-sight` (`FIELD_SIGHT` on `@graview/core`), and refuses such a declaration otherwise; `capabilities().shipped` names no FR id for it. Stored data is unchanged. docs/stability.md says it under the seat's sight.
- 02bdf28: A place rendered to static HTML is said in the reader's locale (FR-174, FR-178). `renderPlace` landed beside the app's locale and wrote every page `<html lang="en">` with its days said in the declaration's brand, whatever brand it was handed and whatever locale the reader reads in. It takes `locale`, the reader's, as the routed face does: days, times and numbers are said in it, then in the `locale` of the brand the page wears, then en-US, and the document says it as its `lang` (`<html lang="en-GB">`).   Compatibility: additive. `RenderPlaceOptions` gains `locale`; a page rendered without one says `lang="en-US"` where it said `lang="en"`.
- b61f093: Cards you can read at a glance (the Roadmap's f-glanceable-cards). On the Graview Roadmap's "Up next", each card printed its whole thirty-line summary, so a row of cards was a wall of text, every card stretched to the tallest beside it, in a box of its own to scroll under the page's scroll. A long text on a card is now three lines at most and on a row one, cut with an ellipsis; the record's page says it whole. A template says a long text's start on purpose: `{summary|excerpt}` is its first sentence, or about 140 characters cut at a word, and `{summary|excerpt: 80}` says at most 80. Cards side by side keep their own heights. A place drawn from blocks flows with the page, never a box that scrolls inside itself; a list cut by its `limit` says "See all 120 features" on the routed face and goes to the kind's list (or "24 more · all features" when it listed some of them), where the scene still says "and 24 more" of a list without a limit. `graview check` warns where a card or a row puts a `text` field whole in a text block (`view-whole-text`: "feature card shows summary whole; it will be clamped. Use {summary|excerpt} or move it to the page."). `excerpt` is in `@graview/core` too, and the stylesheet the blocks are drawn with is written without the spaces it does not need, to make room for this under the hosted page's budget.
  
  Compatibility: ops, stored formats and wire messages are unchanged. The document format's templates take the formatter `excerpt` (`excerpt: <20–1000>`); a template that names it is refused by an older build. New check finding code `view-whole-text`, a warning. `ResolvedList` carries `total`, how many its source named before the limit. `SpecLinkTo.list` is `SpecLinkTo.more`, a face's way to draw the rest of a list cut short. `SPEC_VIEW_CSS` says the same rules without spaces (`.graview-spec [data-graview-tone="good"]{--graview-spec-tone:var(--graview-good)}`), with `.graview-spec-card .graview-spec-text` clamped to three lines and `.graview-spec-row .graview-spec-text` to one. On the routed face a place drawn from blocks has no fixed height (`[data-testid="place-lens"][data-flows]`). What a host does: a card that should say more than three lines of a text moves it to the page.
- 0b722e4: Days, times and numbers are said in the app's locale, en-US first (FR-178). The eighth walk read "10 Sep 2023" in an app written for the United States: every day was said day first, in an order fixed in code, and no face knew a locale. Now every day, time and figure a person reads is said through `Intl` — a record's facts and its glance, a template's `{due}`, `{due | date}` and a moment's `{at}`, a block's field and figure, a rule's line and a rule's message, a creating act's line, an arrangement's headings ("Week of Aug 24, 2026"), the calendar's titles and labels, a declared timeline's hours, and the seat's lines ("On Thursday, Sep 17: …") — in en-US unless the app says otherwise: "Sep 10, 2023", "3:30 PM", "1,250". A moment (`2023-09-10T15:30`) is said with its time where it was said as it is kept. The app says its locale where it says its currency, `brand.locale` ("en-GB" keeps the day first, "de-DE" writes "1.250"), and a host that knows its reader passes theirs as `locale` on the embed (both faces), `GraviewProvider` and a pages `PageContext`, which wins over the app's. `describeApp` says what the locale makes of a day, a time and a figure; `editDocument`'s `set-brand` and `diffDocuments` say "Days, times, numbers and money are written for de-DE." The framework's own words stay English. `@graview/core` exports `dayAsRead`, `momentAsRead`, `timeAsRead`, `numberAsRead`, `weekdayAsRead`, `dayOfMonthAsRead`, `monthAsRead`, `weekdayName`, `minutesAsRead`, `whenAsRead`, `localeFor`, `sayIn` and `DEFAULT_LOCALE`; each takes an optional locale. `capabilities().shipped` gains FR-178.
  
  Compatibility: ops, stored formats, wire messages, the document format, the compiled format, check finding codes and derived tool schemas are unchanged; `brand.locale` is the key it was, and now says days, times and numbers as well as money. The words a person reads change: a day is said "Aug 28, 2026" where it was said "28 Aug 2026", a moment with its time where it was said as stored, a number in a block or a rule's line with the locale's separators, a week's heading with its day in words, and the seat's day "Thursday, Sep 17" where it was "Thursday 17 Sep". `editDocument`'s sentence for `set-brand`'s `locale` and `diffDocuments`' for a changed locale say "Days, times, numbers and money are written for …"; a changed currency is its own sentence. What a host does: pass the reader's locale as `locale` on `mount`, and match words a test reads in en-US.
- b61f093: Filters that stay usable as an app grows (the Roadmap's f-filters-that-scale). On the Graview Roadmap's Requests, "Filter" opened one long menu listing every record at the far end of every relation — "The feature it became" alone was ninety feature names, wrapping, in a popover that ran off the screen — and a row's "1 vote" was cut off at a desk's right edge while "0 votes" was drawn as a badge. A list's choice fields now stand on its arranging line as chips with counts ("Considering 12"), always in view and on a phone a rail of their own beside "Arrange": one press keeps that value, another value of the same field takes its place, pressed again it lets go, and each counts among the rest of what is asked. What is on is a removable chip beside the count. Filter opens short: every heading starts closed and says how many of its own are on; a box at the top, which stays as the list scrolls, finds any filter or any record at a relation's far end by a few letters; a relation with more than eight records there lists only "anything" and "nothing" and is found by typing, eight matches at most; the list is opaque, at most 520 pixels tall with its own scroll, and a sheet along the foot of a phone, where Filter is now a word of its own beside "Arrange" (which holds the sort and the grouping). A list reaches through a relation to what is at its far end — `became.stage:building`, said "The feature it became · Stage: Building" — and `pages.lists.<kind>.filters` names what a kind's list offers, in order (a field, a relation, `is`, or `<relation>.<field>`); unsaid, a list offers every way but a relation to a kind with more than forty records, which it offers by the far kind's choices instead, and its first two choice fields as chips. `graview check` says a filter the kind cannot be filtered by (`pages-filter-unknown`) and a kind `pages.lists` names that is not there (`pages-kind-unknown`). A count of nothing ("0 votes", "0") is no badge, and a list's glance says a record's prose by its start.
  
  Compatibility: ops, stored formats and wire messages are unchanged. The document format takes `pages.lists` (`{ "<kind>": { "filters": [...] } }`) and `arrange-pages` takes `filters: { "<kind>": [...] | null }`, which it refuses for a key the kind cannot be filtered by; `DocumentDiff`'s sentences may say "The request list filters by status, became.stage.". A filter's grammar takes `<edge>.<field>:<value>`, judged by `conditionHolds` (and so by search's `key:value`). New check finding code `pages-filter-unknown`. `@graview/core/arrange` exports `throughEdges`; `ArrangeOffer.about` may be `"via"`. `@graview/primitives` exports `offeredFilters`, `chipOffers`, `farCount`, `farCounts`, `MANY_AT_THE_FAR_END`, `TYPED_FROM` and `CHIP_FIELDS`; `ArrangeBar` takes `filters`, `chips` and `offers`. On a narrow line "Arrange" (`arrange-all`) holds the sort and the grouping and Filter (`arrange-add`) stands beside it; the Filter list's headings are `[data-testid$="-section"][data-key="<offer key>"]`, closed until pressed, and its box is `-filter-find`. What a host does: a harness that picked a Filter entry by `[data-value]` opens its heading first.
- 93a7636: A text field may be written in Markdown, and every list says a text field by its start (FR-180). Graview Cloud's Roadmap keeps its briefs as Markdown in a text field, and the Briefs list drew each body whole with its syntax showing, and the record page too. A document says `{"type": "text", "format": "markdown"}` and a declaration `markdownField()`; the value is kept as written. The record's page — the routed face, the scene's record, the scaffolded page and a static page from `renderPlace` — draws it as prose: headings demoted under the page's own, paragraphs with their line breaks kept, lists (nested, and tasks as ☐ and ☑), emphasis, code and code blocks, quotations, rules and tables, and links only to `http:`, `https:` and `mailto:`, each `rel="noopener noreferrer nofollow"` in a new tab. No HTML passes through: a tag, a handler or a `javascript:` or `data:` link is its words, an entity its character, and a picture `![…](…)` is its words — a record's pictures are its image fields' (FR-162). The reader is a small one of the framework's own, `@graview/core/markdown` (`markdownBlocks`, `markdownHtml`, `safeHref`), linear in what it reads, fetched with the first Markdown a page draws. Editing it opens the text area, with a "Preview" that shows it as the record will; the form says "Markdown" beside the field.
  
  A row, a card, a describe line, a kind stop's table cell, a search hit and a seat's line about a record say a text field by its start (`excerptOf`, on `@graview/core/words`): its first paragraph, heading or list item, or about 160 characters cut at a word, with an ellipsis where words were left out and Markdown's syntax taken out — "## Heading **bold**" is "Heading bold". A template's `{body|excerpt}` takes out Markdown's commonest marks too, and a derived edit's history line says a long string by its start rather than whole. How many lines each draws is still the surface's clamp. Plain text fields keep their paragraphs and line breaks on the record's page, as before.
  
  The checker refuses `markdown` on any type but `text`, and a number's format on a `text`, as `field-format`. `retype-field` takes `"format": "markdown"` and drops it when left out, saying "brief's body is now written in Markdown" or "is drawn as written, not as Markdown"; `diffDocuments` says the change too. A Markdown field's tool argument carries `"contentMediaType": "text/markdown"` and "Written in Markdown." `graview types` notes it. The studio reads it as a text field that says Markdown (`markdown` on its field node, set by `add-field` and `retype-field`), writes it back as `markdownField()` and hands a document back with `"format": "markdown"` kept. `pnpm verify long-text` now also asks it of the Roadmap's real briefs, on a desk and a phone in both schemes: a Markdown body renders as prose with no syntax showing, a list row says a text field as one excerpt line with no Markdown syntax, and hostile Markdown runs nothing. `capabilities().shipped` gains FR-180.
  
  A hosted page's first load is 171 bytes (0.2 KB) heavier, 114 gzipped: the mark on a compiled text field, the format's name, the template excerpt's marks and the history line's start. Nothing else is up front; the reader and the renderer are fetched with the record that draws one. Fetched all together, every face is 16_333 / 6_776 bytes more and the embed with the studio handed in 5_888 / 2_421, each raised by a file of its own (scripts/budget-raises/markdown-in-a-text-field-every-face.json, -studio.json).
  
  Compatibility: ops, stored formats and the wire are unchanged. The document format gains `"markdown"` among a field's `format` values within `graview-document@1`, refused by a build that predates it, and the checker a finding code, `field-format`. A derived tool's input schema for a Markdown field gains `contentMediaType` and the words "Written in Markdown."; for a declaration without one it is unchanged. A derived edit's intent, which the log keeps, says a string of more than 60 characters by its start. `excerptOf` and `isMarkdownField` are on `@graview/core/words`, `markdownField` on `@graview/core`, and the new entry `@graview/core/markdown`. What Graview Cloud does: offer `"format": "markdown"` on a text field when `capabilities().shipped` lists FR-180, and send `retype-field` with `{"type": "text", "format": "markdown"}` to turn it on.
- 73a9750: A number field marked `"format": "money"` is said as money on every face. Found on a Graview Cloud app: a deal's amount, in an app whose currency is USD, read a bare 10000 on its record page, its list, its board's cards, the scene's record, the kind's table, a static page and its form; only a `{amount | money}` template and a lens block said "$10,000". A document's number field now carries what it is said as on its schema (`markAmount`), and every face that reads a record says it there, in the app's currency (`brand.currency`, or the field's own `"unit": "EUR"`) and the reader's locale (FR-178): money "$10,000", a percent kept as a fraction "13%", a measure with its unit "12.5 kg"; a number shown as a duration (`"format": "duration"`) now means exactly the `duration` type — whole minutes, said "1 h 30 min", typed as "1h 30m" and told to a model as `x-graview-type: "duration"` — and the checker notes that the type says it once. A field named bare in a label, a description, a rule's sentence or a refusal (`{amount}`) is said as `{amount | money}` is, and a change's line says "amount → $12,500". A form asks for an amount as it is said: the pages face's form, a record's edit in place and the strip's prompt show the currency's symbol (or "%", the unit) beside a text box with a suggestion in the reader's figures, and read "$1,250.50", "1.250,50 €" and "15%" as typed (`amountBox`, `amountInBox` and `readAmount` on `@graview/core/words`). An act's declared argument that sets a formatted field and says no format of its own is asked for as the field is. `describeApp` says each amount under "## Amounts", and an act's tool schema marks one with `"x-graview-format"` (`money`, `percent` or `unit`), `"x-graview-currency"` and `"x-graview-unit"`, and a description saying the number is kept plain. `editDocument` gains `set-format` (`{ kind, field, format?, unit? }`, `null` clears either), which changes only how a field is said — a number's `money`, `percent`, `duration` and `unit`, or a text field's `markdown` (FR-180) — and says it in words — "deal's amount is said as money in USD — 10000 reads $10,000; its values are kept." — as `add-field` and `retype-field` now say a format they set, and refuse one on a field it does not fit; `diffDocuments` says a format or a unit that changed. `graview check` warns `money-currency` where a money field has no currency to be said in; `field-format`, one code with FR-180's, says in one sentence a format on a field it does not fit — an error for `markdown`, a warning for a number's format — and notes a number shown as a duration. What a hosted page loads first grows by the mark on a number's schema and the change line's money: 476 bytes gzipped over a document, 301 over a compiled app, under both budgets; what reads an amount, asks for one or says one in words is fetched with the faces and forms that do. The embed's every-face and studio budgets are raised by files of their own (10_726 / 3_888 and 10_092 / 3_734 bytes).
  
  Compatibility: ops, stored formats and the wire are unchanged; stored values are kept as the numbers they were. The document format is unchanged; the edit vocabulary gains `set-format`, which a build that predates it refuses as an edit it does not know, and `add-field` or `retype-field` with a format on a field that is not a number or an integer is now refused where it was taken silently. A new check finding code, `money-currency`, a warning; `field-format` (FR-180) also warns of a number's format on any field that is not a number, where it refused one only on `text`, and notes a number shown as a duration. Derived tool input schemas change for an act whose argument is a number field with a `format` or a `unit`: the property gains `x-graview-format`, `x-graview-currency` or `x-graview-unit` and a description (announced on the conformance kit's `document:vendors`, 0.1.29); tool names are unchanged. The words a person reads change wherever a formatted number was said bare, and a percent field's facts are said to the whole percent, as `{x | percent}` says them.
- 48b777a: What takes a record away waits one press behind the rest on its page (FR-173). Archive and merge drawn beside a record's own acts stood between "Change the song" and its form, and renaming a song from the keyboard took 14 presses where it had taken 10. A record's page now keeps archive, merge and remove behind "Show N more" when there are two or more of them, and the first of them takes the keyboard when shown. An affordance says `setsAside: true` when it is a kind's derived archive or merge, so a design that draws its own acts (the discography's) can do the same. The pages face alone and every face are raised by what this added (`scripts/budget-raises/rename-keeps-its-presses-*.json`).
  
  Compatibility: derived tools unchanged. An `Affordance` gains the optional `setsAside`.
- 952e526: Several records are chosen and acted on at once, as one change. Every act on a page was on one record, so marking six tasks done was six rounds and six undos. A kind's list on the pages, a kind's stop in the scene and a status board now choose records: a box at each row's start (on a desk, shown as the pointer or the keyboard reaches the row and always once one is chosen), Shift for a range, Cmd/Ctrl-A for the list, Space on a row, Escape to put the choice down, and on a phone a long press, after which a tap chooses rather than opens. A bar over the face, fetched with the first one chosen, says "6 tasks chosen" to a screen reader as it changes and offers the acts every chosen record shares — the derivation's own, in its order, with "Move to Declined" for the kind's state field and archive, merge and remove behind More — asks an act's questions once for all of them, and runs it as one batch with one undo, attributed as any act is. A record the act may not run on — the policy, the seat's sight, the act's own refusal — is named with why and stays chosen, and the rest are done: each record is judged first, without being told to anybody, and the batch is all-or-nothing only when the store refuses it whole after that (a served store's server), which the bar says ("None of it was kept"). `actsForSeveral` and `actOnSeveral` are on `@graview/tools/several` (and `@graview/tools`), and a seat does the same with `act_on_several` (`{ act, records, args }`), which answers the batch, the records changed, those already so and those refused. The hosted page carries 33 bytes more up front (22 gzipped): the names the first chunk now exports to the bar's chunk.
  
  Compatibility: derived tool names and input schemas — one tool is added, `act_on_several`, listed for every seat that is not read-only, so the surface's hash moves; every act's tool and every other tool is unchanged. `@graview/tools` gains the `./several` entry; `AnswerArgs` takes an optional `store`.
- 3cb21ac: The home counts in the page's locale, and its pictures link nowhere themselves (the ninth walk, W-198 and W-200). The routed home's headline counted each kind by hand — "14 apiaries, 280 hives, 64 beekeepers, 7540 inspections" — and now says it as `counted` does, "7,540 inspections", as do the count beside each kind and "See all 1,201 hives" under a list cut short. And the gallery's card for a lens is a link, while a status board drawn small in it drew each record's card as a link too: React logged "<a> cannot be a descendant of <a>" on every visit, and a browser's parser takes such markup apart. A picture in a gallery card now draws its records without links.
  
  Compatibility: the words a person reads change where a kind holds a thousand or more; the gallery's pictures, already inert, hold no `<a>`.
- 8da8189: A hosted page's first load is 31 KB lighter, and a change that spends more of it has to say so (FR-179). Graview Cloud's shell measured 562 KB of its 570, the framework's hosted page 573.8 KB of 579.7, and every release had spent a little of it. A bundler places a whole file in a page's first chunk when the first chunk can reach it and any fetched chunk uses it, so a name a barrel the page imports re-exports rode up front as soon as a face, a form or the seat used it. Those names left the barrels for entries of their own: the matcher (`search`, `actsOn`, `touchWeights`, `parseQuery`, `matchNode`, `fold`, `squeeze`, `strengthOf`, `fragmentOf`, `searchableFields`, `describeSearched`) is `@graview/core/search`, handed to the provider by the scene and the Find box that search (`matchWith`, from `@graview/react/provider`); a record read in words and an argument's shape (`readableFields`, `pageSections`, `isLongText`, `describeNode`, `tellApart`, `argShape`, `describeArg`, `argumentWords`, `failureWords`, `viaSaid`) are `@graview/core/words`; an act's answer to its seat, every place an app has and the order things are made in (`answerSeenBy`, `placesOf`, `placesFrom`, `beginning`) are `@graview/core/seat`; judging the arrangement (`arrangementFindings`) is `@graview/core/check`'s; a card's sizes and a line's estimated width (`rosterRows`, `MARQUEE_WIDTH`, `estimateWidth` and the rest) are `@graview/layout/sizes`; and an address's arrangement is read apart from what a kind can be arranged by. A frame guest's host — `mountGuestView`, `createGuestHost`, `createGuestLimiter`, `readAcross`, `readTheme` and the protocol's constants — is `@graview/guest/host/frame`, and `guestView` fetches it as the view is first drawn, the frame's height held meanwhile, asked for again when it did not arrive. The studio's door, the seat's panel and the lenses were already fetched with the face that draws them; a test now holds each of them, the frame guest's host and the matcher off the first load.
  
  `pnpm verify hosted` measures a hosted page at 556 818 bytes up front (543.8 KB, 187.4 KB gzipped), where main measured 589 206 (575.4 KB, 199.9 KB gzipped): 31.6 KB less, 12.5 KB gzipped. Handed a compiled app (FR-123) it is 515 126 bytes (503.1 KB, 173.1 KB gzipped), where main measured 547 516 (534.7 KB, 185.6 KB gzipped): 31.6 KB less. Before a face draws, with what it fetches as it is drawn, the scene is 876.3 KB and the pages 806.2 KB, each smaller than before. The budgets fall to 554.0 KB (191.5 KB gzipped) and 513.5 KB handed a compiled app (177.5 KB gzipped), which keeps 10 KB of room for the five features landing beside this one (FR-171, FR-172, FR-173, FR-176, FR-178, about 8 KB together), and a base is never edited upward again: a change that spends more files a raise in `scripts/budget-raises/` naming `"a hosted page"` or `"a hosted page handed a compiled app"`, the bytes it adds and the changeset that ships it, and that changeset says the kilobytes it adds up front ("1.2 KB"), or the test and the pull request's changeset check fail. `pnpm verify hosted` says the gzipped weight beside the minified, and docs/hosted-page.md carries both. Fetched all together, every face's chunks are 4 063 / 3 665 bytes more for the chunks the moved files split into, the studio handed in 1 816 / 2 926, the pages face alone 411 more gzipped (2 637 fewer minified), the guest host before a worker 282 / 288 and registering a worker view 223 / 639, each raised by a file of its own. `capabilities().shipped` gains FR-179.
  
  Compatibility: ops, stored formats, the wire, the document and compiled formats, check codes and tool names and schemas are unchanged. Breaking for an import: the names above are no longer exported from `@graview/core`, `@graview/layout/view` or `@graview/guest/host`, and are imported from the entries named (their types — `ReadableField`, `FieldSection`, `ArgShape`, `AppPlace`, `Beginning`, `Hit`, `SearchResult` and the rest — stay where they were). What Graview Cloud does: import `placesOf` from `@graview/core/seat`, and `mountGuestView`, `createGuestHost` or `readTheme` from `@graview/guest/host/frame`, if it uses them; its shell's `guestView` import is unchanged and now carries the frame's host only when a guest view is drawn.

## 0.1.26

### Patch Changes

- 26dbe52: A picture or a document is put on a record from the page, through the host's own storage (FR-163). A host hands `mount`, `<Embed>`, `GraviewProvider` or the Shell `files: { upload(file: Blob, name: string): Promise<FileValue>; maxBytes: number; kinds: readonly string[] }` (`HostFiles`), and `PagesApp` takes it in its context; `upload` stores the bytes and answers the value the record keeps. A file field's editor is drawn on every face where the record's values are changed — the scene's record, the routed record page, the routed face's forms and the strip's ask — and a field that holds nothing yet is drawn there too when an act may write it. It takes files picked, a photo taken (`<input capture>`, offered where the pointer is a finger), files dropped and files pasted, several at once for a list. A camera's photograph, and a JPEG or WebP bigger than a page needs, is drawn again in the page no larger than 2,048 pixels before it is sent (`resample`, which `downscale` now uses too); a HEIC the browser can read is sent as a JPEG, one it cannot read is sent as it is where the host takes HEIC and refused in words where it does not. What the host takes is said before anything is sent ("or drop or paste them here · pictures and PDF files, up to 10 MB"), and what it refuses is said in the editor's words: "“big.png” is 14 MB; this host takes files up to 10 MB.", "“quote.pdf” is a PDF file; this host takes pictures.", or the message `upload` threw ("“porch.png” wasn’t kept: the disk is full"). What is kept becomes the ordinary act that sets the field, which the room records and undo takes back, and a list's files may be given a caption, moved earlier and removed. Without `files` the editor says "This host doesn’t take files, so none can be added here." and offers no way to add one.
  
  For development and the harnesses, `@graview/ship/dev` has a door that keeps files in memory and serves them back (`devFiles()`, a Vite plugin, and `devFilesHandler`; `/__graview/files`, same-origin only, never in a build), and `@graview/ship/browser` has `filesAt(url, { maxBytes, kinds })`, the `files` a page hands that sends to such a door, or to a host's route that answers the same. The todo example's tasks take attachments through them under `pnpm dev`. `scripts/verify-files.mjs` drives it on both faces in three engines.
  
  Compatibility: additive. Ops, stored formats, the wire, the document format, the compiled format, check finding codes and tool names and schemas are unchanged by the seam itself. New: `HostFiles` from `@graview/core`; `files` on `FrameOptions` (so `mount` and `<Embed>`), `GraviewProviderProps`, `ShellProps` and `PageContext`; `HostFilesContext` and `useHostFiles` from `@graview/react` and `/provider`; `filesAt` from `@graview/ship` and `/browser`; `devFiles`, `devFilesHandler`, `DEV_FILES_PATH`, `DEV_FILES_KINDS` and `DEV_FILES_MAX_BYTES` from `@graview/ship/dev`. `capabilities().shipped` gains FR-163. What Graview Cloud changes: it passes the `files` its route already answers to `mount`.
- 26dbe52: A form, a field read as a document says it, and what can be done are entries of their own, so a page that has drawn none of them carries none of them. A bundler places a whole file in a page's first chunk when the first chunk can reach it and any chunk uses it, and three did: `formFields` and the rest of a form's reading rode in every hosted page's first load through `@graview/core`, though only a drawn form, the strip's ask and a seat read them; a field read in a document's words (`fieldSpecOf`) rode in it through `@graview/core/document`, though only a face's blocks and the checker read it; and the routed face reached `deriveAffordances` through `@graview/tools`, which carried the chat, the agent's tool surface and zod's JSON Schema into the pages face's first load. Now:
  
  - `formFields`, `formField`, `formComplete`, `formArgs`, `undecidableArguments` and the types `FormField`, `ScalarField` and `UndecidableArgument` are `@graview/core/forms`', and no longer `@graview/core`'s;
  - `fieldSpecOf` is `@graview/core/blocks`', beside `fieldSpecsOf`, and no longer `@graview/core/document`'s; `validateViewSpecs`, the checker's reading of a declaration's view specs, has a module of its own and is still `/document`'s;
  - `@graview/tools/derive` carries `deriveAffordances`, `applyAffordance`, `previewAffordance` and `defaultProviders`, which `@graview/tools` still exports too, and the routed face reads them there.
  
  What that buys, with a record's files (FR-162, FR-163) in the same pages: the hosted page carries 1,609 bytes fewer up front (587,604 where main is 589,213) and 280 fewer handed a compiled app (546,632 where main is 546,912); the pages face alone loads 26,338 bytes fewer first (505,306 where main is 531,644; 10,782 fewer gzipped), and the hosted page's routed face 25.7 KB fewer before it draws (834,347). The embed with the studio handed in loads 4,965 more first (1,571,334; the value's check, the file field's door on every face and the checker reading a field as a document says it), and every face 18,725 more in all, 12,787 of it the files' own chunk, fetched with the first file field drawn; both budgets are raised by that, measured.
  
  Compatibility: breaking for an import, and nothing else. Ops, stored formats, wire messages, the document format, the compiled format, check finding codes and tool names and schemas are unchanged, and so is what each name does. What a host does: import `formFields` and the rest of a form from `@graview/core/forms`, and `fieldSpecOf` from `@graview/core/blocks`; a bundler alias list that names `@graview/core`'s subpaths names `@graview/core/forms` and `@graview/tools/derive` before the bare names (`graview create` writes both). Graview Cloud imports none of the moved names.
- 26dbe52: A record can hold a picture or a document (FR-162). A document's field may be `{"type": "image"}`, `{"type": "file"}`, or a `list` with `"of": "image"` or `"of": "file"`; a declaration says the same with `imageField()` and `fileField()` from `@graview/core` (`photo: imageField().optional()`, `papers: z.array(fileField())`), which `toDocument` writes as those types. The value is what the host stored, `FileValue`: `{ href, name, type, bytes, width?, height?, caption? }`, its `href` a path on the host under its base (`/graview/files/3f9a….jpg`, as Graview Cloud answers, or one relative to the page). A value whose `href` has a scheme of any sort (`javascript:`, `data:`, `https:`), names another host (`//…`), holds a backslash or climbs (`..`, `.`, `%2e`) is refused, by the store, a form, a seat's call and an import alike (`isFileValue`, `safeFileHref`): a record is written by acts, seats and imports as well as by an upload, and a `data:` picture would put the bytes in the log, where they do not belong.
  
  A file field is drawn wherever the record is drawn. On a card in the scene and a row of a list on the routed face, the record's first picture is drawn small; on its page and in the scene's record, its pictures are a gallery, each opening full size in a dialog that gives the keyboard back to the picture it opened from; a document is said by its name, its type and its size, a PDF shown inline and anything else a download. Every picture says what it is: its caption, else its name. A view's blocks gained `{ "image": "photos", "as": "thumb" | "gallery", "label"? }` for a card, a row, a page or a blocks lens; the check refuses one that names no image or file field (`view-image`) and an `as` it does not know (`view-format`). What draws them is fetched with the first file field drawn, never in the first paint.
  
  A file's value is a value like any other: set by an act and said in the history by its names ("Change Fix the roof: photos → porch.jpg, gutter.jpg"), undone, exported and remixed as any field, found by its name and its caption, and written by `graview types` as `FileValue` (or `readonly FileValue[]`). A change that leaves files no record holds says which: removing a record says "Remove Fix the roof and its 3 files", and a migration's plan lists them (`MigrationPlan.files`) and names them in its words. What becomes of the bytes is the host's to decide. An agent's tools describe a file argument as the object it is, and a seat may set one to a value the host gave.
  
  The hosted page carries none of it up front that it did not pay for: 587,604 bytes up front where main is 589,213, and 546,632 handed a compiled app where main is 546,912 (see the changeset that moved the forms and the field reading to entries of their own).
  
  Compatibility: additive. The document format gains the field types `image` and `file`, `of: "image" | "file"` on a list, and the `image` block, within `graview-document@1`; a build that predates them refuses them by path. The compiled format carries such a field within `graview-compiled@1`; a page that predates it refuses the compiled app (`compiled-shape`) and compiles the document, which refuses the type by path. Check finding codes: `view-image` is new, an error only about a block that could not be written before. Tool names are unchanged; the input schema of an `edit-<kind>` (or any act) whose kind has a file field describes it as an object. Ops, stored formats and the wire are unchanged. New: `FileValue`, `imageField`, `fileField`, `isFileValue`, `safeFileHref`, `filesOf`, `isImage`, `fileKindOf` from `@graview/core`; `fileFieldsOf`, `firstPicture` and `fileWords` from `@graview/core/blocks`; `ReadableField.files`; `ArgShape`'s `{ type: "file", image? }` and `FormField`'s `control: "file"`; `MigrationPlan.files`; `Files` from `@graview/primitives` and `/pages`. `capabilities().shipped` gains FR-162. What Graview Cloud changes: nothing until it writes a file field; then it reads `capabilities().shipped` for FR-162 first, and serves each `href` it answers to whoever may read the record.

## 0.1.25

### Patch Changes

- 21365f6: An app fills the phone after it turns (FR-165). The embed took the height it was mounted with once, the whole-page Shell was `100vh`, the pages' seat stood in a fixed `100dvh`, and the scene passed over a box reported as nothing; on a phone turned to landscape and back, the app was drawn in the top half of the screen and stayed so, on both faces. `mount(element, { height: "viewport" })` now measures the screen itself: `innerHeight`, read again on `resize`, `orientationchange` and the visual viewport's `resize`, and once more a beat later for the phones that report a turn late. A height in viewport units — `100dvh`, `calc(100svh - 48px)`, the fallback inside a `var()` — is measured the same way, each `vh`, `dvh`, `svh` and `lvh` read as a hundredth of that figure. The Shell's height and the pages' seat follow the same figure (`useViewportHeight`, from `@graview/primitives`, `/frame` and `/pages`), and the scene reads its box again on every `resize`, so a box that comes back from nothing is followed. The on-screen keyboard does not shrink the app: the figure is the layout viewport's, which the keyboard and a pinch leave alone, so a lens is not laid out again for half a screen while somebody types; the browser scrolls the field into view. `capabilities().shipped` gains FR-165.
  
  Compatibility: ops, stored formats, wire messages, the document format, the compiled format, check finding codes and tool names and schemas are unchanged. `mount`'s `height` takes `"viewport"`, and a viewport unit in it is now measured rather than left to the browser.

## 0.1.24

### Patch Changes

- 6dbad14: A box nobody ticked is a no. A form that makes something with a required yes-or-no — "Induction first" on a tool — sent nothing for the box when it was left unticked, and the act refused "expected boolean, received undefined"; `formArgs` now sends it as `false`, as it sends a list nobody added to as `[]`. The derived form no longer marks a box "*", as if it had to be ticked. (W-177, the eighth walk.)
  
  Compatibility: ops, stored formats, the wire, the document format, the compiled format and check finding codes are unchanged. Tool names and input schemas are unchanged; what a derived form sends for an untouched required box is now `false` where it was nothing.
- 9fe145e: A host's turn knows the app the seat is mounted with, so `describe_place` describes a lens place. Inside `HostAi.turn` the seat built its read tools from the store alone, and the chat's own runtime did the same, so a model asking `describe_place` for a lens or the declared home was told "is not a place this app has": the app never reached the seat. `GraviewProvider` now takes `app` and holds it on its context (`useGraview().app`), inheriting the app of a provider it is drawn inside; `mount` and `<Embed>` pass theirs on both faces, and `PagesApp`'s `context.app` reaches the provider under the pages. The chat hands it to its own tool runtime, to the workbench's agent seat and, as `ChatContext.app`, to a host's turn, whose `describe_place` now answers exactly as the runtime `graview mcp` serves does, for the seat that asked. With `app` given, a provider's reader `settings` default to `app.settings`. `graview create` writes `app` on the provider and on the pages' context. The hosted page carries 132 bytes fewer up front (589 625; 589 757 before), and as many fewer handed a compiled app (547 324; 547 456): the embed now hands the provider each option as given, which the provider's props accept as `undefined`, rather than spreading each one in only when it is set.
  
  Compatibility: ops, stored formats, wire messages, the document format, the compiled format, check finding codes and tool names and schemas are unchanged. New: `app` on `GraviewProviderProps`, `GraviewContextValue`, `PageContext` and `ChatContext`. Changed: `GraviewProviderProps`' `principal`, `seats`, `people`, `memory`, `presenceTtlMs`, `presence`, `brand`, `onKeepLens` and `ai` also accept `undefined`, which means the same as leaving them out. What a host does: one that mounts its own `GraviewProvider` passes `app={yourApp}` (and `app` in `PagesApp`'s context) so its seat and its turn can describe the app's lenses and home; `mount` and `<Embed>` need nothing.
- d13dc18: A kind's count on the home wraps on a phone at twice the text. "What is here" set each kind's plural and its count on one line inside its card, so the gauntlet's longest plural with "101" pushed the page 20px sideways at 320 wide and 32px text; the nightly's watch (`breaks-at-width`, W-189's rule) found it. Each card now keeps to the line, its count wraps under its name, and a name with no break point breaks anywhere.
  
  Compatibility: ops, stored formats, wire messages, the document format, the compiled format, check finding codes and tool names and schemas are unchanged.
- 98105c4: A record's name is changed where it stands. The derived record page drew its title as text while every fact under it was pressed and changed in place, as the scene's record is; a record named by a `label` field is now renamed by pressing its name. (W-181, the eighth walk.)
  
  Compatibility: ops, stored formats, the wire, the document format, the compiled format, check finding codes and tool names and schemas are unchanged.
- eb9aac9: A record's "In the scene ↗" beside each place it is seen in is a fingertip wide: it was nine pixels wide, beside the place's own link. (W-188, the eighth walk.)
  
  Compatibility: ops, stored formats, the wire, the document format, the compiled format, check finding codes and tool names and schemas are unchanged.

## 0.1.22

### Patch Changes

- 9154faf: A kind can say which field is its natural time and which its author. `fieldRoles` was not a document key at 0.1.20, so n-dx could not declare a commit's `committedAt` its time nor its `author` who made it: commits had no declared order, and the Runs timeline bound its date in the lens. A document's kind says `fieldRoles` as `defineNode` does — `"fieldRoles": { "when": "committedAt", "who": "author" }`, and any role a lens binds by name — and the framework reads two of them. `when`: a list of the kind opens newest first by it at any length, indexed by year when it is large (it was a large list only, by a `start` role, which a kind that wants it now says as `when`); a timeline or calendar that binds no `start` for the kind draws each record there (`withNaturalTime`, exported for a lens of an app's own). `who` and `when`: an unchosen glance leads with them, before the fields as declared. `graview check` refuses a role naming no field (`field-role-missing-field`, now at `kinds.<kind>.fieldRoles.<role>` for a document) and a `when` that is no date or a `who` that is no name (`field-role-ill-typed`); a lens that binds no start passes where the kind says `when`. `graview describe` lists each kind's roles; a diff says a role that moves; renaming a field moves its role, and removing one lets it go; `toDocument` carries a kind's roles; the document's JSON Schema takes them, and `defineDocument` holds each to a field the kind has.
  
  Compatibility: ops, stored formats, wire messages, the compiled format, tool names and schemas are unchanged. New: `kinds.<kind>.fieldRoles` in the document, the `when` and `who` roles, the finding code `field-role-ill-typed`, `withNaturalTime` on `@graview/primitives`. Changed: a large list no longer opens newest first by a kind's `start` role — say `when` (Discography's albums do); a list of a kind with `when` opens newest first at any length; an unchosen glance leads with `who` and `when`.
- 3399521: A shell with a rail of its own keeps the bar and goes without its places: `surface("shell", Shell, { without: ["places"] })`. n-dx's face lists its places in a rail, and the only way to stop the bar listing them again was `without: ["bar"]`, which also took away the switch between the scene and the pages, Find, the standing, who is here and the person. With `"places"`, the bar keeps every one of those and names no place on the pages, on the app's own address and inside an embed handed the same registry (`pages`). The scene's pictures are the scene's, and stay.
  
  Compatibility: unchanged.
- 9154faf: Each kind words its own end of a relation several kinds declare. An edge's name was one relation to every surface, so a record listed everything that reached it by `by` under the first declaring kind's words, and `graview check` refused two kinds declaring one name in two sets of words (`edge-name-shared`). n-dx's `under` sits on six kinds: a change wants "the tasks inside it", a zone "the zones inside it", and they could not both be said. Now a record's page, a record's connections in the scene, and the cards around a focus list what reaches a record by each reading: kinds that word a relation alike together, kinds that word it their own way apart, each under its own words (an artist's page says "Their songs" and "Their releases"), and a band of a kind is captioned in that kind's own words. So the refusal is gone: a kind may word its own end of a shared relation.
  
  Compatibility: ops, stored formats, wire messages, the document format, the compiled format, tool names and schemas are unchanged. Changed: the finding code `edge-name-shared` is no longer reported; a document or app it refused now compiles. A record's relation groups (`recordFacts(...).links`, the scene's connections) are one per relation, direction and reading, where they were one per relation and direction.
